## Summary

Modular Ship Assembly is the system behind the game's first promise, "a body they assembled from the corpses of other ships": it owns the three physical acts that change the ship's shape (weld a derelict section onto the home-ship, cut a welded section away along its seam, and seat a carried module into a pre-shaped bay) and the single number of workmanship behind every weld. It does so as a thin orchestrator of one atomic, no-rollback command per act across the Life-Support Graph, the ship layout and the ship body, with no menu and no meter: the player sees a bead, a scar, a seated module and a seam that weeps, and learns whether a cut will be clean from the world before they press the button. Read this GDD if you touch welding, cutting, module install, the channel-then-apply handoff, the seam's life after the weld, or the riskiest MVP build item (the first spike).

> **Quick reference** — Layer: `Feature` · Priority: `MVP` · Key deps: `Command & Simulation Layer (designed, hard); Life-Support Graph (designed, hard); Item & Module Database (designed, hard); Interaction System (designed, hard); Ship Section & Layout Data Model (designed, hard); Ship Body & Interior Physics (designed, hard); Save/Load & Versioned Format (designed, soft); Player Controller & Input and Suit & Equipment (designed, soft); Web Map & Transit (designed, hard for the offer); Boarding & Salvage (designed, hard: Boarding depends on Assembly); Adaptive Audio & Tells (designed, soft); HUD & Suit Layer (designed, soft); Sanity & Hallucination Rules (designed, soft); Perception Filter (designed, soft); Debug Overlay (designed, soft); Horror Entities (designed, soft)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: every "undesigned" and "draft in flight" marker refreshed; all of these files carry Status: Designed)*

## Overview

**Modular Ship Assembly is the system that changes the shape of the ship: it owns the three player commands that weld a derelict section onto the home-ship, cut a welded section away along its seam, and seat a carried module into a hardpoint, plus the one number of workmanship (`q`) that colours every weld.** At the data layer it is a thin orchestrator over four other owners' state. A weld or a cut is one atomic cross-block command whose `apply` calls published mutators on the Life-Support Graph (M2 `weld_join` / M3 `cut_detach`), Ship Section & Layout (LM2 `attach_section` / LM3 `detach_section`) and Ship Body (BM3 `rebase_on_attach` / BM4 `rebase_on_detach`) in one `WriteScope`, in that order, with no rollback (kernel CR-9); an install is a smaller atomic command that calls the Graph's M5 `install_module`, the Suit's `release_carried`, the Controller's `clear_carry_ref` (published, PC-1 b; *Amended 2026-10-27 — design review*) and Interaction's `sync_section`. Assembly owns no geometry (Layout), no seam edges or atmosphere (Graph), no pose or actors (Body), no item vocabulary (Item DB), no channel clock or noise (Interaction). What it does own is small and exact: the five command types, the weld quality `q` (`WELD_Q_DEFAULT`), the channel durations (`WELD_CHANNEL_TICKS`, `SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS`), the offer predicates (`weld_offer`, `seam_cut_offer`), the derivation of the cut mode from true state, the order of calls inside each apply, one outcome block (`assembly.state`) with one hook (`assembly.step`), and its own reason codes (`MSA.*`). Its hard job follows from the kernel's failure model: because there is no rollback, every precondition of every call inside an `apply` must be provable by a pure read in `validate`, so most of this document is the list of those proofs and of the places where the proof deliberately stops (module-body state, which a player-origin check must never read).

The player meets Assembly as a physical act, never as a menu. Docked beside a dead ship's airlock, lit and holding a welder, they hold `E · Weld` for six seconds, standing still while the web may be listening; a four-pixel Hull Grey bead appears across the frame and two hulls become one body. Nothing else happens: a weld moves nothing, the hatch is still shut, the valves still closed, the breaker still open, and the seam weeps faintly through the area the Graph gives it. Making the graft live is the player's own later work (open the hatch, open the valve, close the breaker, each a lit Interaction verb with its own physical answer). With the cutter they can later cut the section away along that same seam, clean if they prepared it and ragged (a breach on both sides) if they did not; the cut leaves a cap plate or a ragged scar that never goes away. Carrying a salvaged scrubber or heater home, one press seats it into a pre-shaped hardpoint with a clunk, and the machine either turns or does not. The bead, the cap plate, the scar and the seated module are the player's visible history on the ship; none of them is ever a number.

Without this system the game's first promise has no verb: "a body they assembled from the corpses of other ships" is a sentence, derelict sections could only be stripped and abandoned, a salvaged module would have nowhere to go, and Pillar 5 (salvage integrates into the body that survives; scarcity pushes inward; loss costs ship and body, never knowledge) would have no ship-shaped record to point at. It is also the kernel's stress test (kernel Interactions row 5: "Atomicity (G2, CR-9), one notification batch, validate-then-apply") and the riskiest MVP build item (index risk PR-SCOPE), so it is the first spike.

**Index reconciliation.** The index carries this system under three numbers that all name the same row: bullet 13 of the Systems Enumeration, entry 9 of the Feature layer in the Dependency Map, and row 16 of the Recommended Design Order. The index wording ("Weld and cut sections as atomic one-tick commands across graph join and layout change: join validation is a pure Graph query; seams set edge properties; cap plates; detach into a new body. Also owns module install/uninstall ... as a graph mutation distinct from section welding") is honoured with five readings, each routed where it differs. (1) *Atomic one-tick*: the **completion** command is atomic in one tick; the player-facing act is a four-to-six-second channel followed by that tick (MSA-3). (2) *Seams set edge properties*: the Graph sets them (`seam_at_weld`); Assembly supplies `q` and the plan and cites the formulas. (3) *Cap plates*: a cap plate is Layout's presentation of a socket in state `CAPPED`; Assembly causes the state change and owns no cap-plate mutator (Layout has no `install_cap_plate`). (4) *Uninstall*: there is no uninstall command in MVP; V8 module salvage (Interaction) is the only way a module leaves `INSTALLED` (MSA-28). (5) *Distinct from welding*: install is its own command family with its own apply (MSA-1).

**MVP slice.** One weld (the authored pair `sock_airlock_e` on the home-ship and `sock_b_w` on derelict B, offset `(696, 384)`), one player-facing seam cut (SEALED or RAGGED), and one install (the salvaged `mod_heater` into the vacant `hp_cargo_heater` that arms the staged PWR-OVL fault). The welded body holds the home-ship's 5 compartments plus derelict B's 5, which is 10 and within the Graph's "<= 12 compartments at the weld" (the loaded set `{home+B, A}` holds 14; whether LS-26's 12 bounds that set is the Graph's call, routed as an ASK, advisor R26). Both sockets go terminal after use, so MVP offers exactly one weld-then-cut cycle (MSA-25). *Scope (user ruling 2026-10-27, X1 / D52, Open Question 1 resolved):* the MVP is weld + one player seam cut + one install; `game-concept.md` MVP item 3 and `systems-index.md` are amended to say so by their own editors, the neighbouring GDDs' slices (Layout's slice, Body's detach, Derelict Authoring's staged faults) stand, and the spike plan covers all three. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* **Scope status: RESOLVED. Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): X1 / D52, option (b), MVP = weld + player seam cut + install.** `game-concept.md` (MVP Definition, item 3) read "One weld: attach one section and join its nodes to the graph" and listed "co-op" under "Explicitly NOT in MVP"; item 3 and `systems-index.md` are amended to weld + player seam cut + install (the edits are made in those files by their own editors; this GDD does not edit them). Families C and I (MSA-18 to MSA-28, D.6, D.10, D.13, T4, T5, AC-MSA-31 to 45, 75, 92, 110, 111 and the cut and install spike items) therefore stay in the MVP (items tagged `[VS]` or `[VS-B]` keep their own tags); the salvaged `mod_heater` has its bay and the staged PWR-OVL fault its resolution; the earlier option (a), "weld only", is closed and the other GDDs do not cut their slices back. **Co-op stays out of the MVP** (concept, "Explicitly NOT in MVP"): every co-op rule, validation target and criterion in this GDD is tagged `[VS-B]` and is excluded from the MVP gate (`/gate-check` for MVP); where a rule has a single-player or double-based unit-test form (for example `CUT_BLOCKED` with a Body double, the multi-seat data shape), the criterion says so and only that form gates MVP.

## Player Fantasy

**Framing: direct, with the ship's answer as its consequence layer.** *(creative-director consulted; Framing A adopted, B kept as the second beat of the anchor moment, C rejected.)* The category is Gameplay, so the player *does* the fantasy with their hands. The Graph's indirect framing (the player feels the ship through its consequences) is the second beat here, not the first: the player performs the graft, and the ship answers in its own body (a draught, a weep, a creak). The grafter never gets a "success" from the UI; they get it from the room.

> *"I join a dead ship to ours with my own hands, and the seam I leave is the only thing on board that is ours."*

The home-ship is "a body they assembled from the corpses of other ships" (concept, Core Fantasy), and this system is the act of assembling it. A derelict section keeps everything it was: "A graft keeps its donor dialect in full; **the seam is the only thing that is the crew's**" (art bible 6.1). The player stands at a dead ship's airlock with a welder, lit and still and loud, and leaves a Hull Grey bead across the frame. That bead is the crew's signature on the ship. It is drawn wherever two sections meet, it ages from fresh to old, and it never goes away. If the section is later cut free, the crew's mark becomes a cap plate or a ragged scar, and that also never goes away.

Welding does not make the derelict *work*; it makes it *joinable*. When the bead closes, the hatch is still shut, the valves are still closed and the breaker is still open (Graph LS-15: "a weld moves nothing"). Making the graft live is a second, deliberate act, one lit verb at a time, and the ship answers each with something physical. The power fantasy is not "I built this". It is **"I chose what this ship is made of, and I chose when to let it in."**

**Aesthetics served** (concept ranking). *Fantasy* (rank 4, primary): "We are the only ones awake"; "a ship that is ours because we welded it". *Discovery* (rank 1): the weld is the payoff of the derelict's one visible unknown, and the player finds out what it does to the air. *Challenge* (rank 2): preparing a clean cut, welding under rising Attention, reading the witnesses before opening a seam. *Fellowship* (rank 3): the crew roles of boarder and ship-keeper meet at the seam, one welding lit and still while the other watches the dark or holds the far side, and the crew both witnesses and can veto a cut (the co-op beat, below) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: `[VS-B]`. Fellowship needs a second seat and co-op is not in the MVP, so this aesthetic is validated from Vertical Slice B and is not part of the MVP gate)*. *Expression* (rank 7) is a Vertical Slice aspiration: MVP has exactly one socket pair, so "where" is not yet a choice, and when it becomes one it must stay functional, never cosmetic. *Submission* is deliberately absent: the weld is never a relaxing build loop.

**Autonomy, stated honestly.** The MVP weld is a **choiceless sequence by construction** (one socket pair, a fixed `q`, no rotation or mirror or free placement), and this GDD says so rather than inventing options; a quality slider or weld pattern would be a false choice and progression creep (MSA-42). The player's autonomy lives in three places: **whether** to weld at all (derelict B can be stripped by V8 salvage with no weld, and neither line dominates: welding brings power, O₂, fuel and five rooms plus an attack surface and a leak; stripping brings portable modules and costs salvage noise and open ends to cap), **when** (relative to Attention, because the channel is 360 ticks of tool noise while standing still), and **how clean a later cut will be** (preparing the seam with V4, V5 and V3 is lit physical work that earns a SEALED cut). Competence comes from reading the consequence channel; relatedness, in solo, from the seam as the previous inhabitants' hand and the player's own earlier self, and, in co-op, from the crew beat below.

**The co-op beat: one welds, one watches (Fellowship, CD-GDD-ALIGN) `[VS-B]`** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: this whole beat is Vertical Slice B; co-op is explicitly not in the MVP, game-concept "Explicitly NOT in MVP", so nothing in this paragraph or its validation targets gates the MVP)*. *This is a fantasy statement and a validation target, not a mechanic: Assembly adds no co-op rule, role, bonus or shared channel.* The concept's two crew roles meet at a socket. The **boarder**, who went out to the derelict, stands lit and still at the airlock with the welder for six loud seconds, unable to see past their own beam; the **ship-keeper** stands a few tiles off at the edge of that beam, watching the dark behind the welder or holding the far side of the seam, with their own chest lamp and ears for whatever the weld is calling. The one who welds trusts the one who watches, and the watcher's job is to be the half of the crew that is not looking at the bead. The same trust runs through the cut: the crew **witnesses** a cut (the burn is loud, the hatch is a place two people can stand on either side of) and the crew **vetoes** it, because a crewmate who steps into the open hatch passage stops the cut (`CUT_BLOCKED`, MSA-18, MSA-21). The seam is where the crew decides, in the room and without a menu, what the ship is made of. Under hallucination the beat deepens: the watcher's chest beam and the welder's sparks are the human channel (P1) and stay true, and a hallucinating crew learns to say "put your hand on the seam" before they trust a lamp *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: "put your hand on the seam" is a verb only if MSA-23b "feel the seam" exists; it is provisional on ASK IW1 to the Inspect View owner. If IW1 is declined this sentence is rewritten to the witnesses that do exist: the hiss, the creak, the frost and the true seam-lane valve and breaker sprites)*.

### Anchor moments

**The weld (primary anchor), in two beats.**

*Beat 1, the join: domestic, not triumphant.* Once Web Map & Transit has established docking (no prompt appears until `docked` holds), the player stands at the airlock; their chest lamp is on the frame and the prompt reads `E · Weld`. They hold still. For `WELD_CHANNEL_TICKS` 360 `[PLACEHOLDER]` (6 s) the welder spits non-emissive sparks at the hand point (never the chest point; the chest beam stays where they are looking), the linear bar with its needle-mark fills, and every accrued tick is tool noise the web hears. Moving stops it at any time; the partial-progress ledger keeps the work for `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` 600. When the bar completes, a Hull Grey bead lies across the frame and two hulls are one body. **The bead is the spectacle**: there is no reward pop, no fanfare and no weld-completion `LOUD`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **the Beat 1 release is the tool loop ending and the room going quiet.** For six seconds the work loop, the sparks and the TOOL hiss fill the frame; at the apply they stop together, on one cut, and the silence that follows is the release, a true one, because the player has stopped being loud. It is not a reward and nothing is added to it. The bead settle (VA-14) is the one soft sound inside that quiet.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: **the quiet is a deliberate gap, and the rest of the document now agrees with it.** At the apply the work loop and the sparks cut together; `ia_done` is suppressed for the weld (VA-13), so the bead settle is the single audible "done"; for the 1 s after (60 ticks, the Game Feel "First weld" window) nothing else sounds; the weep hiss, and the creak when a gradient exists, ramp in at the end of that second (a mix ask to Failure & Trail and Adaptive Audio, ASK FT3). The weep mist and the rim bow or frost are not sound: they are Graph truth and still appear at the first Graph step. AC-MSA-149 measures the window.)* Nothing else changes: no air moves and no lights come on. The Pipeline's solver reset (`BODY_TRANSFER`) happens only for an actor standing on the absorbed (derelict) side and, with `docked` holding, moves nothing on screen.

*Beat 2, letting it in: the ship answers.* The closed hatch is now a door with two jambs and a leaf. Before touching it, the careful player listens and looks. If there is a pressure gradient across the closed leaf it creaks (the Graph's `SEAL_STRAIN` descriptor, emitted by any closed opening under gradient; `WELD_DP_WARN` 300 dkPa is only what Assembly's cue may read as a boolean, not the creak's threshold); if the derelict is frozen, frost rims the jamb. Then they open the hatch and feel **the draught** cross the beam as the pressures meet; they open the O₂ valve and close the breaker, and B's dead lamps either flicker on or do not. From the step the seam exists it weeps, faintly, through `A_seam_m` (LSG D.2); the weep **tapers**, falling linearly with `leak_bp` from its first wear-in period and ending at period 100, about 16.7 minutes, for a default-quality weld, and until then it is the graft's first failure point and the art bible's hero shape #3 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. Beat 1 is *held breath* (exposed, still, loud on purpose, P3). Beat 2 is *earned calm* (art bible 2.3, "the only state the player should want to linger in"), reached only if the player chose right and was patient. *Honest risk (CD-GDD-ALIGN):* the calm leans on the weep wearing in. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the Graph now tapers the weep (LSG D.2), so the settling gradient exists: a fresh default seam weeps at 720 milli flow units, half of that at period 50 (about 8.3 minutes) and none at period 100. The weld's P1 truth tell is therefore the taper plus the bead age and the descriptor floor (`intensity_pm` at least 1 while any flow remains, LS-14), not a permanent weep. A weld at the top of the quality range weeps 2 milli units for only about 6.3 minutes (D.1 table), so there the bead age carries the read.)* The promise that the weld proves itself in the room still needs a **playtest validation target**, not an assumption: players can tell an old, settled seam from a fresh one by the weep, its taper or the bead age alone (AC-MSA-127, Validation targets). Whether the taper is perceptible stays routed to Failure & Trail (Open Question 11).

**The cut (secondary anchor): amputation is a choice you prepare.** At a welded seam with the cutter, a player who has *prepared* the seam (hatch shut, valves shut, breaker open, which is exactly how the weld left it) ends the `SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS` 240 `[PLACEHOLDER]` (4 s) cut with one `LOUD` burn-through, a cap plate and "nothing beyond it". If they did not prepare, or a hallucination told them a valve was shut when it was open, the cut goes **RAGGED**: a breach on both sides, hull stubs, open ends hissing, and about 38 s to the decompression band (96 s to vacuum) in a 20-tile room (Graph LS-16, cited) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. The emotion is *resolve with a price*: the player decides what the ship loses and the ship keeps the scar. Socket states `CAPPED` and `CUT_OPEN` never return to `FREE` in MVP (Layout LY-13). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **the cut is a prepared amputation, not an escape verb.** The solo player's reasons to cut are three, all stated here so the verb has a motive: (1) to take what a graft has already given (weld, strip the modules by V8 salvage, cut the husk away: the extraction line, E-24); (2) to shed a worn graft, because a welded derelict section keeps its derelict wear rate, `rate_pm` 2000, and so ages at twice the home-ship's pace after the weld (Graph LS-15 and LS-25; E-53), which makes the graft the ship's weak point and shedding it a reason to cut; (3) to shed a section that has become a liability (infested, breached, frozen) once the seam has been prepared. A panic cut is RAGGED and breaches the home side too, so cutting under pressure is the worst way to use the verb, and nothing in this GDD offers it as a way out of a fight.)*

**The install (payoff anchor): the clunk, then waiting for the rotor.** The player has carried a salvaged module home at carry gait with rising Attention behind them. At the vacant hardpoint the prompt appears only because what they carry fits. One press, a heavy clunk, and then the real payoff, which Assembly does not own: "a running machine begins its loop only if the Graph runs it" (Item DB). The rotor turns or does not. The player learns the truth from the machine's moving part, not from a confirmation.

### Pillar alignment

- **P5 Deeper Is Richer, Loss Is Not Total** (primary). *"Risk and reward map to depth in the web ... there is no safe place to farm forever."* The weld is the ship's history: a record, not an undo. Scars are terminal, a weld adds edges and mints nothing (Graph conservation), and a reinstalled store starts at stock 0 (Item DB IDB-18).
- **P1 The World Never Lies** (primary). *"Pipes leak, frost forms, air hisses; the physical world is ground truth."* The weld's quality is told by the seam's weep, never by a number; the surge is told by the hatch's creak, never by a preview value.
- **P3 The Web Watches** (serves). *"The players' light, noise, power use and restraint raise or lower its attention."* Six seconds lit and loud at a socket is the price of every graft.
- **P2 Dread Before Chaos** (serves). *"Every breach is preceded by a tell a careful player could have perceived."* A ragged cut is the player's own breach and its witnesses come before the cut; a system cut (VS) waits behind the 10-30 s tell.
- **P4 Sanity Rewrites Reality** (serves, as a constraint). *"No lie is unfalsifiable."* Assembly is a truth anchor, below.

**Tone and vocabulary.** Workmanlike, intimate, unglamorous: the register of a field technician (art bible 5.1), not an architect; the weld is surgery done in the dark (a metaphor for tone, not a pose requirement). Body and graft metaphors are welcome in language only: flesh and violet stay out of every Assembly visual (P1 sane palette). Player-facing words: *weld, seam, bead, graft; cut away, cap plate, scar, ragged; install, seat; the derelict's side; "the seam weeps / holds / creaks"*. Never: *build, construct, place, snap; demolish, refund, undo; upgrade; blueprint, layout, module grid; "seam integrity 92 %", "weld quality", "connected!"*. Prompt labels carry a verb only and never a state word (a label is a function of the static offer, never of module-body state).

### Design test

> **When a choice makes assembly more flexible vs. makes the seam more consequential → the seam. When a choice tells the player the weld is good vs. lets the weld prove itself in the room → let it prove itself.**

Corollaries the Detailed Design must satisfy (each already decided and cited, not re-ruled here): (1) `q` is never displayed anywhere a player can see it; its consequence is the weep and the strength of the bus. (2) A weld that fails to take after its channel *sputters and does not hold*, with no reason text and the same cue for every non-DONE outcome. (3) The cut mode is *earned* by lit preparation and never picked from a menu or a payload. (4) The preview shows only where the graft will go: no number, ring, percentage or "can't weld" icon, and an invalid candidate is simply not offered. (5) The weld finishes the join and hands the connection back to the player; it never opens a hatch, valve or breaker for them. (6) Every scar stays.

### Under hallucination the fantasy deepens rather than breaks

MVP ships one hallucination class, (b) *false system states*; classes (a) phantom entities, (c) crewmates as horrors and (d) phantom salvage are Vertical Slice and recorded here so the rules do not change when they arrive. The seam is the place the crew trusts most. The bead geometry, the cap plate, the ragged edge, socket state and Layout's `welds[]` are **truth channel** and the Perception Filter never wraps them; the draught, weep, creak, frost and hiss are the Graph's **consequence channel** (closed list). What *can* lie is the **module-body channel**: a valve sprite, a breaker lever, a hardpoint's "empty" look, a gauge on B's wall. The experience target: *when my instruments disagree, I go back to the seam and put my hand on it.* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the hand-on-the-seam verb is MSA-23b, "feel the seam", a QUIET consequence-channel read through the Inspect View owner (ASK IW1). It is the game-designer's option (a) and the creative-director's position; the Failure & Trail owner has concurred that no zero-gradient trail or acoustic descriptor exists to draw (FT row 21), so the seam-lane sprites stay TRUTH and the hand-on-the-seam read is the diegetic witness that closes ASK G8. Until IW1 is accepted this experience target describes only the witnesses that exist today.)*

| Class | What the lie can do to Assembly's moments | What stays true | Cost of checking (P4: time, risk or trust) |
|---|---|---|---|
| (b) False system state (MVP) | A valve elsewhere on the ship renders **closed** when it is open; for **the seam's own valves and breaker** the lie is not drawn at all while the zero-gradient witness is unconfirmed (MSA-23 fallback: they are TRUTH at the seam), so the player expecting a clean cut is never misled by that sprite | The hiss at the seam, rim frost and `SEAL_STRAIN` are consequence channel; the seam-lane valve and breaker sprites are truth under the fallback; the cut mode is derived from true positions at apply (MSA-19) | **Time:** stand at the seam and listen or look before cutting. **Risk:** cut anyway and take a RAGGED breach that had a witness. The lie can cost the clean cut elsewhere; at the seam it can neither take it nor leave it without a witness. Once the Graph and Failure & Trail confirm a zero-gradient lane witness the seam valve sprite may revert to corruptible and the hiss or lane state becomes the check |
| (b) False system state (MVP) | The hardpoint renders **empty** when a module is seated | The install prompt depends only on what the player carries, so the lie gains nothing from it; install resolves `BLOCKED(SLOT_OCCUPIED)` after the press | **Time:** a wasted walk at carry gait, not a free oracle |
| (b) False system state (MVP) | B's dead lamps render lit after the breaker closes | Lamp brightness is consequence channel; the beam reveals the real light radius | **Time:** step out of the own beam and look |
| (a) Phantom entity (VS-A) | Something "moves" on B's side while the player welds | Its footprint (sound, frost, hull stress) is P1/P2 truth; weld progress is never paused or faked by perception | **Risk:** finish the 6 s exposed, or step away and keep the ledger for 600 ticks |
| (c) Crewmate as horror (VS-B) | A crewmate welding beside the player renders as a horror | Their own beam and voice are the human channel (P1, inviolable); the sparks and the appearing bead are world truth | **Trust.** A cut is never a weapon; `CUT_BLOCKED` reads true actor presence in the hatch rectangles, not perceived identity, so a hallucinating player cannot seal a "monster" into a cap |
| (d) Phantom salvage (VS-A) | The "scrubber" carried home is garbage | The install offer is computed from the true carried def, so no prompt appears at the hardpoint | The trip home is a fallback reveal, not the only one (cheaper field checks are other owners'). ASK to the Filter: it may not fake or suppress an install prompt |

**No lie is unfalsifiable at the seam.** Every Assembly moment has at least one consequence-channel witness the player can reach before committing: before opening the hatch (creak, frost), before cutting (hiss, frost, creak) and after installing (the rotor).

### Reference techniques

| Reference | Adopt | Reject |
|---|---|---|
| *Hardspace: Shipbreaker* | Cuts only along pre-declared lines (our seams); cutting into a pressurised volume vents and that is the consequence (our RAGGED cut); depressurising first is the skill | Salvage grading, quotas and profit screens; free cutting anywhere; physics-toy fragmentation |
| *Alien: Isolation* (ion torch on doors) | A long, stationary, noisy tool hold that leaves you exposed while something may be listening | Its circular progress dial (art bible 7.3: a linear bar with a needle-mark, never a radial wipe) |
| *Project Zomboid* | Timed actions in a world that keeps running; movement cancels the action; "Death is a bitch. It happens." No invulnerability while channelling | Skill levels and build-quality numbers (`q` is data, never a skill); the right-click construction menu |
| *Barotrauma* | The welder as a tool you stand still with at exactly the place that matters; docking as a physical act whose hatch you open afterwards | Repair-progress percentages; the status-monitor overview |
| *Satisfactory* | Pre-shaped pieces that mate at defined points | The build menu, hologram ghost placement, rotation and blueprints |
| *FTL* | A module slot is a fixed place on a fixed ship; install is one decisive act | Abstract power bars and numeric stat upgrades; installing from a menu anywhere |
| *Oxygen Not Included* | Opening a door between two atmospheres is a visible event in the medium (our draught) | Overlay views of gas or pressure |
| *Pandorum* (film) | The grafted, mismatched ship as wrongness you live inside; working by chest lamp in the dark | n/a |
| *Beyond the Aquila Rift* | The kindness of the lie, which applies to the seam only through the module-body channel; the seam itself never lies | Any lie touching the bead or the cap plate |
| *Space Engineers / Starbase* (counter-reference) | n/a | Merge blocks, free-angle ship design, voxel hulls: everything this game is not |

### What this GDD does not promise (anti-pillar guard rails)

1. **Not a freeform builder.** No rotation, mirror, free placement, resizing, runtime sockets, blueprint export or import, or build mode (Layout LY-34). Welds occur only at pre-declared socket pairs; installs only at pre-declared hardpoints.
2. **No relocation verb.** No `remove_module` in MVP; V8 salvage is the only way a module leaves `INSTALLED`. Moving a working machine always costs a salvage cut.
3. **No meter, readout or overlay.** No weld-quality bar, seam-health number, surge number in the preview, "connection established" panel or ship schematic. The only UI is the prompt plate, the linear bar and the in-world cursor.
4. **No progression from welding.** No `q` upgrades, tool tiers, weld skill, or `Σ q_mods` slot. Better ships come from what the player salvages.
5. **Not a combat game.** A cut is a ship operation, never a weapon or trap; no force, push or knockback at weld or cut (Body BDY-28). A cut never closes a seam on anyone: a system cut that cannot clear the hatch is abandoned, never forced.
6. **Not intentional PvP.** Assembly offers no verb whose only use is to harm a crewmate, no force, push or pin (rail 5), and the one way a cut can leave a crewmate on the leaving piece (a keep-side seam cut, MSA-18) is **counterable by the victim, never silent**: the channel is lit, still and noisy for 4 s (the tell), and a far-side crewmate who steps into the open hatch passage before completion blocks the cut outright (`MSA.CUT_BLOCKED`, MSA-21, AC-MSA-123). This is stated honestly as a counterplay, not as the absence of a harm path: until Web Map & Transit ships a recovery path for stranded crew the stranding exists in MVP and Vertical Slice A under the user's "death is a bitch. It happens." ruling, and from Vertical Slice B `CUT_STRANDS` is a sanity-gated rule like P4(c) lethal friendly harm, adopted by default (user ruling D53; MSA-18, Open Question 2, AC-MSA-124) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **this is a pillar question, not only a Transit contingency, and it was a user decision (Open Question 2), resolved by user ruling D53 below.** An ungated keep-side cut that can strand or decompress a sane crewmate sits against the concept's anti-pillar "NOT intentional PvP" and against P4(c), which gates lethal friendly harm on the harmer's sanity. Creative-director recommendation: **gate `CUT_STRANDS` on the cutter's `harm_eligible` by default, and make "no gate" an exception that needs a recorded user ruling.** It is `[VS-B]` only and does not block the MVP, because the MVP is solo.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): the creative-director recommendation is adopted, `CUT_STRANDS` is gated on the cutter's `harm_eligible` by default, and "no gate" now needs a recorded user ruling.)*
7. **No free reversal.** Scars are terminal; there is no re-weld of a used socket and no seam repair in MVP. The language says "history", never "undo".
8. **No celebration.** No fanfare, no weld-completion `LOUD` added for "feel", no screen shake beyond Body's `JOLT_PX` (default 0), no reward pop. The bead is the reward.
9. **No guarantee the graft is good.** A weld can bring a frozen, airless or dead section home, and opening it can hurt. The failure contract (P5) is the safety net, not Assembly.

### Validation targets (handed to the playtest gate)

At the MVP playtest gate (three or more outside players): at least two of three describe the weld in ownership language ("our ship", "I welded that") and none describe it as "building"; players pause at the closed seam hatch before opening it by their second session; no playtester asks "did the weld work?" after Beat 2 (after Beat 1 is acceptable and intended); a RAGGED cut is reported as "my fault, I should have closed the valve", not "the game killed me".

Three further targets added by CD-GDD-ALIGN (2026-10-26), all manual and all `[PLACEHOLDER]` thresholds: (1) **Fellowship (AC-MSA-128) `[VS-B]`, not an MVP gate:** in co-op playtests (two seats), when one player welds the other is observed standing off the beam or holding the far side for the channel in at least 3 of 4 first welds without being told to, and at least half of the pairs describe the weld or the cut in "we" language ("we welded it", "I held the door"); a cut is described as "we decided", not as one player's act. (2) **Settled versus fresh (AC-MSA-127):** players can tell an old, settled seam from a fresh one by the weep or the bead age alone, with no HUD; the weep's linear taper to a stop (LSG D.2, period 100 at `q` 850) is a P1 promise only if the taper reads *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, so a failure is routed to the Graph and Failure & Trail (Open Question 11), never answered with a number or an indicator. (3) **The veto is usable (AC-MSA-123, manual companion) `[VS-B]`, not an MVP gate:** a far-side playtester told only that "someone is cutting the seam" opens the hatch and stands in the passage in time in at least 3 of 5 trials from a start within the veto radius `R_veto` of MSA-18 (`R_veto = (D - reaction_ticks - door_open_ticks) x V_WALK`, 240 px or 5 tiles at the placeholders; *Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier "8 tiles" ignored reaction time and the door tap and is withdrawn*); fewer is a tuning input on `SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS` and the far-side audibility ask (ASK AU1), and it is a tuning input to the ruled Vertical Slice B `CUT_STRANDS` gate (MSA-18) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **How every judgement-based target above and in the criteria is scored (replaces "4 of 5 players, all answers correct").** (1) *Pooled, not all-or-nothing:* the unit is one judgement (one player answering one scenario). 5 players x 4 scenarios give 20 judgements; the target passes at **17 or more of 20 pooled**. The old rule (at least 4 of 5 players each correct on all four) fails a healthy design most of the time: at 90 % per-judgement accuracy a player is all-correct 66 % of the time and the 4-of-5 rule passes only 44 % of runs (a 56 % false failure), where the pooled rule passes 87 %, and a poor design at 70 % per-judgement accuracy passes the pooled rule only about 11 % of runs. (2) *Word list:* "ownership language" is any of {our ship, we, ours, my ship, I welded, I cut, I fitted, I joined, I held the door}; "building language" is any of {build, built, construct, place, snap, craft, assemble it}; transcripts are coded by sentence, not by keyword count. (3) *Rater agreement:* two raters code the transcripts independently; the target is read only if their agreement is at least 80 % of coded sentences, otherwise a third rater codes and the majority stands. (4) *Targets are not pass-or-fail criteria:* AC-MSA-69, 70, 127 and 128 cannot fail a build; they are tuning inputs and are listed here as Validation targets (the criteria section keeps their ids and says so). Thresholds are `[PLACEHOLDER]` until the first playtest sets them.

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

**Conventions.** Rule ids are `MSA-n`, collision-free across `design/gdd/`. Formulas are `D.n` in this document; a formula owned elsewhere is always prefixed with its owner (`LSG D.14` is the Graph's seam formula, `LY D.6` Layout's weld offset). Acceptance criteria are `AC-MSA-nn` and are pre-assigned below (the Acceptance Criteria section expands each to Given/When/Then and keeps these ids). Diagnostics are `MSA_*`. Assembly's reason codes are namespaced `MSA.<CODE>` under the kernel's `VALIDATION_FAILED(domain_code)`. Every new number carries `[PLACEHOLDER]` and a registry-candidate line (see *Names this GDD introduces*, end of this section). Neighbour rules are cited by rule id (`LS-15`, `LY-16`, `BDY-24`, `IA-14`), never by line number, because sibling GDDs are still being edited. "Advisor R<n>" names an orchestrator ruling that decided the point; "(orchestrator ruling: ...)" names a reconciliation made while authoring. Each rule gives **Rule**, **Criterion** (AC ids), **Pillar**, an *Alternative considered* line where a choice was made, and *Decides*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* One tag is added: `[VS-B]` marks a co-op rule, target or criterion (it needs a second seat, a MIRROR client or a crewmate's presence) and is excluded from the MVP gate; the one-weld-versus-three-verbs contingency is stated once, in the Overview. Every paragraph, row or criterion changed by the first design review carries "*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*". No fault rule relies on an exception being thrown or caught: GDScript has none (MSA-31).

#### A. Shape of the system

**MSA-1 — Five commands, three families.** *Rule:* Assembly registers exactly five command types (table T1). **Family W (weld):** `begin_weld` (PLAYER, CHANNEL) and `weld` (SYSTEM, the atomic completion). **Family C (seam cut):** `begin_seam_cut` (PLAYER, CHANNEL) and `seam_cut` (SYSTEM, the atomic completion). **Family I (install):** `install_module` (PLAYER, TAP). The `begin_*` commands open a channel and mutate no ship state; the completions are the atomic cross-block applies; install is a one-shot atomic apply. No other type is registered. `remove_module` is reserved and unregistered (MSA-28). The name `begin_cut` is Interaction's V8 module salvage and is never used here; `CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS` and `SALVAGE_*` are likewise never reused. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-01. *Pillar:* P5, anti-pillar "not a freeform builder" (three pre-shaped verbs). *Decides:* advisor R1, R4, R5, R10, R27.

**T1 — Command types** (registry: `assembly_commands`).

| Command | Origin | Mode | Payload (primitives only) | Actor binding | Coalesce | `max_age_ticks` | Declared / registered | Reason set |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `begin_weld` | PLAYER | CHANNEL | `{target_ref}` (a `WELD_SOCKET` subject in `FREE`) | `OWNED_BY_ISSUER`, `actor_source` `body.state` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | `NONE` | `MAX_AGE_TICKS_ACTION` 120 (kernel, cited) | Interaction declares the row (IA-23); Assembly registers | `weld_ready`, IMMEDIATE half (T2); tool `tool_welder` |
| `begin_seam_cut` | PLAYER | CHANNEL | `{target_ref}` (a `WELD_SOCKET` subject in `WELDED`) | `OWNED_BY_ISSUER`, `actor_source` `body.state` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | `NONE` | 120 | declaration row requested of Interaction (ASK I1); Assembly registers | `seam_cut_ready`, IMMEDIATE half; tool `tool_cutter`; `CUT_BLOCKED` |
| `weld` | SYSTEM | atomic completion | `{actor, socket_a, socket_b, cause}`, `cause` = `CHANNEL` | explicit `actor` field (SYSTEM origin has no issuer binding) | `NONE` | `PENDING_TTL_TICKS` 12 `[PLACEHOLDER]` (MSA-6) | Assembly | `weld_ready` full; DEFERRED branch resolves to `BLOCKED` |
| `seam_cut` | SYSTEM | atomic completion | `{actor, seam_id, cause}`, `cause` in {`CHANNEL`, `CASCADE`} | explicit `actor` field | `NONE` | `PENDING_TTL_TICKS` 12 | Assembly | `can_detach` set, `CUT_BLOCKED` |
| `install_module` | PLAYER | TAP | `{hardpoint_ref}` | `OWNED_BY_ISSUER`, `actor_source` `body.state` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | `NONE` | 120 | declaration row requested of Interaction (ASK I1); Assembly registers | IMMEDIATE set only; DEFERRED branch resolves to `BLOCKED` |

All reasons are computed from true state and the kernel never filters them (CR-5). `allowed_origins` of `weld` and `seam_cut` is SYSTEM only (a channel completion or a cascade); tests submit them through a SYSTEM-origin double.

**MSA-2 — Assembly owns decisions, not mutations.** *Rule:* Assembly owns the five commands, the weld quality `q`, the channel durations, the offer predicates, the cut-mode derivation, the order of mutator calls inside each apply, `assembly.state`, `assembly.step`, and its reason codes. It owns no geometry (Layout), no seam edges, atmosphere or the formulas `cap_seam`, `hp_seam`, `A_seam_m` (Graph), no pose, velocity or actors (Body), no item vocabulary (Item DB), and no channel clock, abort set, partial-progress ledger or noise (Interaction). Every state change goes through a mutator the owning system publishes (kernel CR-9; H1, H3). Assembly writes exactly one block, its own. It adds no arithmetic of its own to the Graph's seam formulas: it supplies `q` and `L_seam` and cites `LSG D.14` and `LSG D.2` (D.4 below works them at MVP values). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-02 (review: no restated foreign arithmetic or value), AC-MSA-03 (lint: the only block Assembly writes is `assembly.state`; every other write is a published mutator). *Pillar:* P1. *Decides:* advisor R2; KRN H1, H3.

**MSA-3 — Weld and seam cut are two-phase: begin, accrue, handoff, completion.** *Rule:*

1. **Begin (P2 of tick `Tb`, PLAYER).** `begin_weld {target_ref}` or `begin_seam_cut {target_ref}`. `validate` runs the IMMEDIATE half of the predicate only (MSA-7, MSA-8). `apply` calls Interaction's proposed Class B `open_channel(seat, ASSEMBLY, verb_id, target_ref, duration_ticks)` and the Controller's `set_channel_ref(actor, ASSEMBLY, channel_id, face_octant)` (published, PC-1 b; *Amended 2026-10-27 — design review*), where `face_octant = octant_of(anchor_o(target_ref) − foot)` is computed in this apply (Controller D.8: the octant whose D.3 `UNIT` vector maximises the integer dot product, ties to the smaller index; `NONE` only for a zero vector), `anchor_o` being the interaction anchor of the `target_ref` subject (the `WELD_SOCKET` subject for `begin_weld`, the seam subject for `begin_seam_cut`, the same anchor Interaction's `in_reach` uses) and `foot` the actor's `pos_curr`, both in the actor's body frame; the Controller stores it as `channel_face` and locks facing to it for the channel *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6/G1, CONFLICT)*; the apply mutates no ship state. Coalesce `NONE`.
2. **Accrue (P3, Interaction).** `interaction.step` runs the clock, the shared abort set, the lit recheck, the partial-progress ledger and the noise exactly as for its own verbs (MSA-30). Per IA-14, elapsed `e = T - Tb` and accrual happens at `e = 1..D`, so a fresh channel is DONE on the P3 of tick `Tb + D` (a channel resumed from the ledger is DONE `resume_offset` ticks sooner; the arithmetic below is for a fresh one).
3. **Handoff (P3 of tick `Tb + D`, `assembly.step`, ordered after `interaction.step`).** It reads the DONE handoff Interaction exposes in `channel_view` and re-runs the IMMEDIATE predicate. On a fail it writes `last_result = REJECTED(reason)` and submits nothing (the channel's cost is spent; the cause is world-visible). On a pass it writes `pending {kind, target_ref, submit_T}` and submits the SYSTEM-origin completion `weld {actor, socket_a, socket_b, cause CHANNEL}` or `seam_cut {actor, seam_id, cause CHANNEL}`.
4. **Completion (P2 of tick `Tb + D + 1`).** Authority, then `validate` proves everything by pure reads (MSA-7, MSA-14, MSA-20), then `apply` is total.

*Worked example (cited convention, weld at the Save/Load registry's tick):* a weld begins in P2 of `Tb` = 437039; accrual happens on ticks 437040..437399 (`D` = 360); the DONE handoff is in P3 of 437399; the completion applies in P2 of 437400. Because both channel durations are multiples of `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6 (IA-14 lint), the channel spans a whole number of 6-tick blocks, but DONE need not fall on a Graph hook tick. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-04. *Pillar:* P1 (a state change during a six-second channel is re-validated, never assumed), P3. *Alternative considered:* a one-phase shape in which the hook calls M2/LM2/BM3 directly at channel end — rejected: the hook would skip `validate`, and "both verdicts must pass before apply" (Layout's `socket_plan` contract and its Interactions row) would have no home, so a defect would become FAULTED instead of a clean reject; a `begin_*` that is itself the command applied at press with the channel as a wind-up — rejected: the world would change before the work. *Decides:* advisor R10; KRN CR-9; LS-15 ("`can_join` is run at the command's `validate`, the same tick as apply").

*Origin caveat (load-bearing).* Hooks may submit SYSTEM-origin commands only (KRN CR-3), so every channel-completed `weld` and `seam_cut` reaches P2 as SYSTEM. Body's BDY-24 keys the hatch-cap rule on origin ("PLAYER-origin cut rejects, SYSTEM-origin cut defers"). This GDD reads that split as **`cause`**: a `CHANNEL` completion **rejects** with `CUT_BLOCKED` (at begin, at handoff and at completion); a `CASCADE` completion **defers** (MSA-22). The wording refresh is routed to the Body owner (CONFLICT C7).

**MSA-4 — `assembly.state` holds outcomes only: no progress, no history.** *Rule:* block id `assembly.state`; class `SIM`; `persist true`; `persist_scope WORLD`; scope `SHARED` with per-seat records keyed by **PlayerId** (never the transport peer id); `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY`; `schema_version 1`; `restore_after [layout.state, graph.state, interaction.state, body.state]`. Per seat it holds `pending {kind NONE|WELD|SEAM_CUT, target_ref, submit_T}` and `last_result {verb_id, target_ref, outcome, reason, T}`; the `[VS]`-reserved CASCADE deferral record (MSA-22) is the only other field. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **`last_result` is runtime-only.** It is the presenter's input (MSA-34, D.17), not world state: it is outside `snapshot()`, outside the canonical bytes of every hash scope and outside the Save/Load payload, and it is still carried in the `SHARED_SUMMARY` replica. A restore therefore leaves it empty by construction, with no clearing step, and the save-equivalence test (MSA-38, AC-MSA-61) is not defeated by a field that every restore resets. Alternative considered: keep `last_result` persisted and list it as a permitted difference in AC-MSA-61 and S7 — rejected, because after the first `DONE` the hash of a restored run would then differ from the uninterrupted run at almost every later tick, which turns the main equivalence test into a test of nothing. Kernel ASK K3 asks the kernel owner to confirm that a block may declare a field runtime-only in `snapshot()`; ADR candidate "assembly.state persistence".)* Schema in table T6. It stores **no channel progress** (that is Interaction's record, MSA-30) and **no world history** (that is Layout's `welds[]`: `welded_at_T`, `cut_at_T`, state). `snapshot()` writes every seat's `pending` as the NONE record, so `post_restore` clears nothing: it runs the verifier on the as-restored block (the `pending` clause of INV-10 is then vacuous, MSA-32) and the block is restore-idempotent, the Controller's PC-17 pattern *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(orchestrator ruling, QA-lead flag F-10; **Amended 2026-10-27 — design review**: the earlier text said a snapshot in the handoff window still has the weld target `FREE` or the cut seam `WELDED`. That holds for the normal one-tick window but not for a completion that is rejected in P2 after a rival seat's apply (E-33, AC-MSA-59): there `pending` stays set until `submit_T + PENDING_TTL_TICKS + 1` while the target is already `WELDED`. INV-10 is therefore weakened (T7) to "the target exists and is in a legal pre-state or post-state", so the live-state check stays meaningful and no legitimate state is flagged)*. 64-bit `T` values are held as two 32-bit integers or strings and never travel as JSON numbers (Save/Load SL-2). The block holds **no packed array** (at most four seats, plain keyed records), which removes the Godot 4.7 hazard that setting one element of a packed array no longer calls the property setter (breaking-changes.md) from Assembly altogether; dirty keys are still marked explicitly inside every Assembly mutator (the kernel rule). *Why `persist true` for a block whose `pending` is never stored:* `pending` is live from `submit_T` (set in P3 of `T`) until the completion's apply clears it (P2 of `T+1` in the normal case, up to `T + 8` under queue truncation) or, when the completion is rejected or dropped, until the TTL resolves it to `NOT_TAKEN` at `submit_T + PENDING_TTL_TICKS + 1` (13 ticks at the default); a snapshot taken anywhere in that window writes it as NONE (the re-review amendment below). The block stays `persist true` for the `[VS]` CASCADE deferral record, the schema version and the stable shape of the per-seat records, and so that it sits in Save/Load's mandatory set -> ADR candidate "assembly.state persistence" (the persist true/false fork is decided here as: `persist true`, `pending` live but written as NONE, the `[VS]` deferral record persisted, `last_result` runtime-only; the ADR records it). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: **the `pending` contract is Option B, restore-idempotent: one contract, stated here and cited everywhere else (MSA-38, E-29, E-30, T6, FB-6, AC-MSA-05a, 60, 61, 118, 129, S7).** `pending` is a live gate, not saved state. `snapshot()` emits every seat's `pending` as `{NONE, no target, submit_T 0}`, so the stored bytes equal the restored state, `snapshot(restore(snapshot()))` is byte-identical (SL-15 item 10), `assembly.state` leaves Save/Load's `REBASE_BLOCKS`, and the earlier permitted difference PD-2 is deleted. The state hash does not cover a live `pending`; that is safe because `pending` carries no world fact (the world is in `layout.state`, `graph.state` and `body.state`; `pending` only gates a new `begin_*` and anchors the TTL), and a divergence between peers shows in those blocks as soon as the completion applies. The ticks from `submit_T` to the apply or to the TTL (up to 13 ticks, E-30, E-33) are declared **non-snapshot ticks**: the completion is a command in the kernel queue, which no save holds, so a restore there loses the weld or cut and the player redoes the channel; a completion is never replayed or resubmitted from state (MSA-38). The loss is bounded (at most 13 ticks, 0.22 s) and ASK S1(a) (`pending_any()` as an autosave soft gate) closes it. **Answer to Save/Load Open Question 39 (OQ-39):** Assembly's clear moves into `snapshot()` as above; Save/Load may remove `assembly.state` from `REBASE_BLOCKS` once this GDD is accepted. Alternative considered: Option A, persist `pending` and let the hook resubmit the completion from it (the kernel's hook-resubmit pattern); rejected for the MVP because it adds a replay path that MSA-38 forbids, a resubmit that must be de-duplicated against the TTL and the `BUSY` gate, and a hash term for a field with no world fact, to save at most 0.22 s of repeated work. The kernel's restore-equals-uninterrupted criterion is therefore met everywhere except the declared non-snapshot ticks, which Open Question 44 asks the kernel owner to accept.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier sentence said `pending` lives "across exactly one tick boundary", which E-33 and AC-MSA-59 contradict. Option (b) of the review, clearing `pending` at `T+1` on a P2 rejection, is not implementable: `assembly.step` reads only `assembly.state` and `channel_view` (CR-10; ASK K1 asks whether a hook can read a result event at all), so a P2 rejection at `T+1` is invisible to it and only the TTL can resolve the record.)* *Criterion:* AC-MSA-05a and 05b (schema; `pending` stored as NONE and restore-idempotent; the two failure layers), AC-MSA-06 (lint: no packed-array typed field), AC-MSA-129 (a save taken at every tick of the `pending` window restores cleanly) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. *Pillar:* P1. *Alternative considered:* (B) no Assembly block, the channel record in `interaction.state` and Interaction's hook calling back into Assembly's cross-block mutators — rejected: it makes Interaction a second handler for Assembly's atomic apply (IA-23's own rejection); (C) the channel as Adapter-side only — rejected: PC-7 says progress is sim state, and MIRROR identity and abort-before-accrual would break. Assembly therefore owns a **minimal** block and the channel lives in Interaction's record (MSA-30). *Decides:* advisor R2, R12; TD-9. CONFLICT C2 (Save/Load row "Owns no block"), C3 (PCI "accrue in Assembly's block").

**MSA-5 — One hook, `assembly.step`, with two jobs.** *Rule:* `(hook_id assembly.step, owner Assembly, divisor 1, offset 0, after [interaction.step])`. It does exactly two things: (a) turn a DONE ASSEMBLY handoff into the completion command (MSA-3.3); (b) resolve a completion that was Rejected or Dropped by the TTL rule (MSA-6). It submits SYSTEM-origin commands only and writes only `assembly.state`. It iterates seats in ascending PlayerId, reads only `channel_view` and its own block, submits at most one SYSTEM command per seat per tick, and uses no wall-clock, no RNG and no dictionary-order dependence, so the same state always submits the same commands (what makes replay and MIRROR identity possible). It is O(seats <= 4) reads when idle. Divisor 1 because the handoff must follow DONE on the same tick. `interaction.step` must never name `assembly.step` in its own `after` (no cycle); an unknown `interaction.step` id fails boot (CR-3). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-07 (auto: deterministic output, ascending PlayerId, hook-order registered), AC-MSA-62 (lint/budget, MSA-39). *Pillar:* P1. *Decides:* advisor R2, R10, R12; TD-11.

**MSA-6 — The completion carries nothing derived; its life is bounded so `DONE` and `NOT_TAKEN` are mutually exclusive.** *Rule:* the completion payload is exactly `{actor, target, cause}` (target = the socket pair or `seam_id`): no `q`, no plan, no mode. `q` is read from the knob at apply, the plan is re-derived from Layout's `socket_plan`, the mode is derived (MSA-19). A payload that carries nothing derived stays replayable and gives a hallucination nothing to influence. `assembly.step` stores `submit_T` in `pending` when it submits. The completion's `apply` clears `pending` and writes `DONE` or `BLOCKED`. If `pending` is still set when `T - submit_T > PENDING_TTL_TICKS`, `assembly.step` writes the neutral outcome `NOT_TAKEN` and clears it (the true reason goes to the result event for DIAGNOSTIC and replay only; there is no reason text for the player). The two registered completion types set `max_age_ticks = PENDING_TTL_TICKS`.

*Why that makes the two outcomes exclusive by construction.* A hook-submitted command is stamped `queued_tick = submit_T`. At P2 of tick `submit_T + k` its age is `k`, and the kernel rejects `STALE` when age exceeds `max_age_ticks`. So the command can apply only while `k <= PENDING_TTL_TICKS`; at `k = PENDING_TTL_TICKS + 1` P2 rejects it `STALE` and the P3 of that same tick (after P2) is the first tick on which `T - submit_T > PENDING_TTL_TICKS` and `assembly.step` may write `NOT_TAKEN`. No late `Applied` can follow a `NOT_TAKEN`, and no `NOT_TAKEN` precedes an apply. Constraint (formula D.11): `PENDING_TTL_TICKS >= ceil(QUEUE_CAPACITY / MAX_CMDS_PER_TICK)` = 8, which is the kernel's own never-stale invariant (`KRN D.7`: `max_age_ticks(any type) >= 8`), so a local command is never spuriously stale from queue depth; default 12, range 8-119 `[PLACEHOLDER]`. **One guarantee, the BUSY gate** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: `pending.kind != NONE` is a `BUSY` term at site 1 only (MSA-7), so a `begin_*` pressed while a completion is unresolved is refused at the prompt rather than queued behind it; this, not a duration floor, is what makes it impossible for a new handoff to meet an unresolved `pending` (a resumed channel can be as short as 6 ticks, D.7, so a floor on channel length proves nothing). The range tops out at 119 because of clause 2 (`MAX_AGE_TICKS_ACTION` 120 minus 1). A rejection at admission (`QUEUE_FULL`) or in P2 leaves `pending` set, so the same TTL covers it; the cue arrives `PENDING_TTL_TICKS + 1` = 13 ticks (about 0.22 s) after submission, which is acceptable for a neutral cue. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: in that window the pending target may already be `WELDED` (a rival completion applied first, E-7, AC-MSA-59) or the seam already `CUT`. That is legal, `pending` does not assert anything about the target's state, and INV-10 accepts it (T7); what `pending` does until the TTL is gate a new `begin_*` (`BUSY` at site 1) and hold the cue back to `submit_T + 13`. The lifetime of `pending` is therefore "`submit_T` until the apply clears it or the TTL fires", and MSA-4, E-30, E-33, MSA-33 and MSA-38 say so.)* *(orchestrator ruling: supersedes the wording of advisor R12, which wrote `NOT_TAKEN` when `pending` was "uncleared at T+1". Under the kernel's `MAX_CMDS_PER_TICK` truncation a legitimate completion can apply at T+2..T+8, so R12's wording could write `NOT_TAKEN` and then `DONE` for the same weld. The outcome name, neutrality and clearing behaviour of R12 are kept; only the trigger is TTL-based. The specialist proposal `MSA_COMPLETION_MAX_AGE_TICKS` 2 is rejected: it violates the kernel's `max_age_ticks >= 8` boot invariant.)* A sim hook has no confirmed path to command result events (CR-10: simulation systems never subscribe), so the rule reads only `assembly.state`, as Interaction's own door retry reads its `attempts[]`; whether a hook can read a result event at all, and the `issuer_peer` of hook-submitted commands, are ASKs to the kernel owner (ASK K1). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-08 (auto: under an injected backlog of 1..8 ticks the result is `DONE` exactly once and `NOT_TAKEN` never; a completion rejected at admission yields `NOT_TAKEN` at `submit_T + PENDING_TTL_TICKS + 1`), AC-MSA-09 (lint: payload fields; `max_age_ticks == PENDING_TTL_TICKS`; value within 8-119). *Pillar:* P1. *Decides:* TD-6, TD-7, F-TD-1; advisor R10, R12; KRN CR-5, CR-13, CR-17, `KRN D.7`.

#### B. Predicates, reasons and the P4 boundary

**MSA-7 — One predicate per verb, three call sites, one reason order; the completion `validate` is the only safety.** *Rule:* `weld_ready(seat, socket)` and `seam_cut_ready(seat, socket)` are pure. Each is called at three sites: **site 1** the `begin_*` `validate` (PLAYER origin), **site 2** the handoff pre-check in `assembly.step`, **site 3** the completion `validate` (SYSTEM origin). The three sites share one body and one reason order (a lint); the only differences are the declared skips in T2a. Between sites arbitrary other commands can apply (a crewmate opens the hatch, salvages a lane node, welds the same socket), so sites 1 and 2 exist to avoid wasting a channel and to produce the world cue; **site 3 is authoritative** and the only site correctness relies on. Reason order, first failure wins: kernel authority -> `NO_TOOL` -> `OUT_OF_RANGE` -> `NOT_LIT` -> `BUSY` (at site 1 only: the seat already has a channel, **or `assembly.state.pending.kind != NONE` for the seat**; MSA-6) -> `INVALID_TARGET` -> `NOT_ELIGIBLE` (Interaction's order, IA-10) -> Layout's `can_attach` reasons in Layout's order (`SOCKET_UNKNOWN`, `SOCKET_NOT_FREE`, `SAME_BODY`, `FACING_MISMATCH`, `WIDTH_MISMATCH`, `CLASS_MISMATCH`, `LANE_MISMATCH`, `GEOM_OVERLAP`) -> `NOT_DOCKED` (weld only) -> `can_join_static`'s nine structural reasons in the Graph's order (MSA-10). For the cut: kernel authority and the actor terms -> `NOT_ELIGIBLE` (wrong side, MSA-18) -> Layout's `can_detach` (`SEAM_UNKNOWN`, `SEAM_NOT_ACTIVE`) -> Body's `can_rebase_detach` (`BDY_REBASE_UNKNOWN_BODY`, `BDY_REBASE_POSE_OUT_OF_RANGE`, `BDY_VEL_OUT_OF_RANGE`; Body's namespace) -> `CUT_BLOCKED` (MSA-21) -> `CUT_STRANDS` `[VS-B]` (sites 2 and 3 only, never at begin, MSA-18) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. The DEFERRED slot is not part of any site (MSA-8).

**T2a — Predicate terms by site.**

| Term | Site 1 begin | Site 2 handoff | Site 3 completion `validate` | Why the skip |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Actor terms: `NO_TOOL`, `OUT_OF_RANGE`, `NOT_LIT`, `INVALID_TARGET`, `NOT_ELIGIBLE` | yes | yes | yes | cheap; the tool or light may have changed |
| `BUSY` (the seat has a channel, **or `pending.kind != NONE`**) | yes | no | no | after begin the seat's own channel is the one finishing, and at sites 2 and 3 `pending` is the very completion in flight, so the term would be self-blocking; it guards only a *new* begin (MSA-6 belt and braces) |
| Layout `can_attach` / `can_detach` | yes | yes | yes | |
| `NOT_DOCKED` (weld) | yes | yes | **no** | Body's BDY-27 (locked): weld legality takes no pose input and the *offer* is gated, so a kernel `validate` must not add a pose term; the handoff pre-check is not a kernel `validate`, so it re-checks and shrinks the loss window to one tick *(orchestrator ruling: reconciles advisor R12 "same body at three sites" with R17 "`NOT_DOCKED` is a backstop in begin" and BDY-27; the declared skip is the only exception)* |
| `can_join_static` | yes | yes | yes | |
| `CUT_BLOCKED` (cut) | yes | yes | yes | |
| `CUT_STRANDS` (cut, `[VS-B]`, user ruling D53) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | **no** | yes | yes | A begin-time rejection would make the prompt a free occupancy oracle for a crewmate on the leaving piece; the cost (the channel's time and noise) is paid before the verdict, as for `NOT_DOCKED`'s site 3 skip; it reads true positions and Sanity's `harm_eligible`, never perceived identity |
| full `can_join` (reads `PORT_ABSENT`, `surge[]`) | no | no | no | module-body state; evaluated only in the completion `apply` (MSA-8) |

*Criterion:* AC-MSA-10 (lint: identical bodies and reason order modulo T2a; site 3 marked authoritative). *Pillar:* P4. *Decides:* advisor R12; TD-3; Layout's `socket_plan` contract ("both verdicts must pass before apply").

**MSA-8 — IMMEDIATE reasons reject; DEFERRED reasons are outcomes after cost.** *Rule:* a reason is **IMMEDIATE** iff it depends only on geometry, light, the actor's own state, tool or stock, registry structure, pose or the consequence channel. IMMEDIATE reasons reject at the `begin_*` commands and are shown in the world (a missing prompt or the standard rejection cue). A reason is **DEFERRED** iff it depends on module-body state (`hp`, `state`, `forced_mode`, `served_pm`, a node being ABSENT, DESTROYED or SALVAGED). Two exist: `PORT_ABSENT` (weld) and `SLOT_OCCUPIED` (install). A DEFERRED reason is **never read by a PLAYER-origin `validate`** (MSA-9). In a completion `apply`, step A0 re-runs the full Graph `can_join` (weld) or `check_install_module` (install), SYSTEM-side. If the only failing verdict is DEFERRED, `apply` writes `last_result = BLOCKED(reason)`, clears `pending` and **mutates nothing else** (M2 or M5 is never called); both branches of `apply` are total, an Applied command with an outcome, not a fault, following the IA-12 / IA-24 precedent. The structural half (`can_join_static`) runs first in `validate`, so a DEFERRED verdict can never mask a structural failure (`PORT_ABSENT` precedes later structural reasons in the Graph's own order; a masked read would be lossy). Because `validate` and `apply` run contiguously in one P2 slot with no other command, hook or snapshot between them (CR-2, CR-9), the structural verdict at A0 equals the one `validate` computed; a difference is a defect (`MSA_VERDICT_DRIFT`; *Amended 2026-10-27 — design review:* a release-fatal fault through the explicit check of MSA-14, not a debug assertion, because `assert()` is compiled out of a release export). `PORT_ABSENT` is reachable in MVP only through a salvaged def-backed lane node (`j_main` or `j_b_main`, both CABLE_JUNCTION); the bare duct junctions `dj_air` and `dj_b_dock` are not salvageable (DA-19). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **the strip-first line must not kill the headline verb.** Salvaging `j_b_main` makes `weld_join` `PORT_ABSENT` for the whole run, and the player gets the same neutral sputter with nothing to teach why. Rule: MVP content authors the seam-lane node modules (`j_main`, `j_b_main`) as **non-salvageable**, a static-data fact and so safe under P4 (a hard content requirement owned by Derelict Authoring, ASK D2 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*); the fallback is that a reinstall of the salvaged module into its vacant bay restores the lane. With D2 accepted, `PORT_ABSENT` is unreachable in MVP content and stays as the Vertical Slice and defect-class path, and E-15 and AC-MSA-11 use a test fixture that salvages a lane node. AC-MSA-131 asserts that no strip line makes `weld_join` permanently impossible.)* *Criterion:* AC-MSA-11 (auto: `PORT_ABSENT` -> `BLOCKED`, nothing mutated, no `weld_join` call), AC-MSA-12 (auto: `SLOT_OCCUPIED` -> `BLOCKED`, instance stays carried). *Pillar:* P4. *Alternative considered:* treat every `can_join` reason as immediate — rejected: `PORT_ABSENT` would be a free oracle for a hallucinated "fixed/absent" module. *Decides:* advisor R12, R18; IA-10, IA-12; TD-1, TD-2.

**MSA-9 — The P4 boundary: IA-11 is adopted as Assembly's own numbered lint.** *Rule:* no `validate` of a PLAYER-origin Assembly command (`begin_weld`, `begin_seam_cut`, `install_module`) reads `node()` or `edge()` `hp`, `state`, `forced_mode` or `served_pm`, or calls the full `can_join` or `check_install_module`. The existence of a prompt is a function of (object classes, tool in hand, `lit`, `in_reach`, `eligible`, registry and static data) only. Channel duration and bar length are identical regardless of state. No payload carries a believed state, direction or kind. Design test, quoted from IA-11: *"could a player learn a module-body truth from this at zero time, risk or trust?"* **One admitted exception, install's apply (MSA-27):** reading a node's `state` is allowed inside the TAP's `apply` because the prompt itself is state-independent and the attempt has already cost the carry (time, carry gait, carry noise); this is routed to Interaction as an extension of IA-11(a) for install only. **One kept oracle, `CUT_BLOCKED` (MSA-21):** it reads true actor presence, not module-body state, and is deliberately a truth channel. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-13 (lint: PLAYER-origin validate sources reference no full `can_join`, no `check_install_module`, no node-state accessor; only `can_join_static`). *Pillar:* P4. *Decides:* advisor R12, R18, R29.

**MSA-10 — The structural join query: `can_join_static` (Graph ASK, **answered 2026-10-27**).** *Rule:* the Graph publishes a second pure query `can_join_static(plan)` returning exactly the nine structural reasons of LS-15 (`SECTION_UNKNOWN`, `SECTION_NOT_LOADED`, `SOCKET_OCCUPIED`, `PORT_INCOMPAT`, `SEAM_LIMIT`, `ALREADY_JOINED`, `CAPACITY`, `POWER_CYCLE`, `STABILITY`) in the Graph's order, reading no node `hp`, `state`, `forced_mode` or `served_pm` and computing no `surge[]`; the existing `can_join` is `can_join_static` plus `PORT_ABSENT` plus `surge[]`. `STABILITY` sums static template areas and `POWER_CYCLE` is wiring topology, so both are structural. Assembly calls `can_join_static` at all three sites; only the completion `apply` calls the full `can_join`, and the Graph's M2 `weld_join` has the full `can_join OK` as its own Class A precondition. Cost: `STABILITY` recomputes the Graph's D.12 sum for the two seam compartments; that is acceptable once per `begin_*` press and once per completion, and it must **not** run inside `weld_offer`, which Interaction evaluates every tick for a focused socket (MSA-12). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **answered**; the Graph publishes `can_join_static(plan)` as a pure query in LS-15 ("Structural half, returns, seam read", consistency pass 2026-10-27) and lists it in its Assembly Interactions row. This is the one name used for the ask that the systems-designer wrote as a "masked `can_join`" and the technical-director as `can_join_static`. ASK G2 is closed; the fallback below is retired.)* *Fallback if the Graph refuses:* begin checks only Layout's verdicts and the Graph's structural reasons surface at the handoff as `REJECTED`, which costs the player the channel (a P2-fairness degradation, flagged). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-14 (auto: structural order; `PORT_ABSENT` never masks a structural reason). *Pillar:* P4, P2. *Decides:* advisor R12 (resolves its internal tension); TD X-2.

Reason table (T2; registry: `assembly_reasons`). "Sites" are 1 begin, 2 handoff, 3 completion `validate`, A the completion `apply`.

| Code (`MSA.` namespace for Assembly's own) | Class | Source | Sites | Cue |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| kernel auth; `NO_TOOL`, `OUT_OF_RANGE`, `NOT_LIT`, `BUSY`, `INVALID_TARGET`, `NOT_ELIGIBLE` | IMMEDIATE | Kernel / Interaction (shared vocabulary); `BUSY` also fires when `assembly.state.pending.kind != NONE` for the seat (MSA-6) | 1, 2, 3 (`BUSY` site 1 only, including the `pending` term) | shared prompt grammar; most appear as an absent prompt |
| `SOCKET_UNKNOWN`, `SOCKET_NOT_FREE`, `SAME_BODY`, `FACING_MISMATCH`, `WIDTH_MISMATCH`, `CLASS_MISMATCH`, `LANE_MISMATCH`, `GEOM_OVERLAP` | IMMEDIATE | Layout `can_attach` | 1, 2, 3 | no prompt: an invalid candidate is simply not offered |
| `MSA.NOT_DOCKED` (new) | IMMEDIATE | Assembly, from Body `docked` | 1, 2 | no prompt (the offer is gated, MSA-12); backstop uses the standard rejection cue |
| the nine structural reasons, in LS-15 order: `SECTION_UNKNOWN`, `SECTION_NOT_LOADED`, `SOCKET_OCCUPIED`, `PORT_INCOMPAT`, `SEAM_LIMIT`, `ALREADY_JOINED`, `CAPACITY`, `POWER_CYCLE`, `STABILITY` | IMMEDIATE | Graph `can_join_static` (surfaced in the Graph's namespace, not `MSA.`) | 1, 2, 3 | standard rejection cue (structural, falsifiable in the world; in MVP these are authoring defects) |
| `SEAM_UNKNOWN`, `SEAM_NOT_ACTIVE` | IMMEDIATE | Layout `can_detach` | 1, 2, 3 | no prompt |
| `NOT_ELIGIBLE` for a cut from the wrong component (MSA-18) | IMMEDIATE | Assembly `seam_cut_offer` | offer; 1 | no prompt |
| `MSA.CUT_BLOCKED` (new, suggested by Body BDY-24) | IMMEDIATE | Assembly, from Body `actors_overlapping` | 1, 2, 3 | at begin the standard rejection cue; at handoff and completion the cut family's cue, the flame gutters and the seam holds; the crewmate in the hatch is the witness |
| `MSA.CUT_STRANDS` (new, `[VS-B]`, user ruling D53; *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*) | IMMEDIATE (geometry, pose and sanity-bit read), checked at sites 2 and 3 only, so a probe costs the channel | Assembly, from `detach_preview.leave_sections[]`, Body actor poses and Sanity `harm_eligible` | 2, 3 (never 1) | the cut family's one post-channel cue: the flame gutters and the seam holds; no reason text |
| `PORT_ABSENT` | **DEFERRED** | Graph full `can_join` | A only | "the bead sputters and does not hold"; no reason text |
| `SLOT_OCCUPIED` (install) | **DEFERRED** | Assembly: maps the Graph's unnamed occupancy failure of `check_install_module` (slot node not ABSENT or SALVAGED, LS-18 M5) to the outcome `BLOCKED(SLOT_OCCUPIED)`; answered by the Graph's M5 row (ASK G9 closed, *Amended 2026-10-27 — design review*) | A only | "the module bumps against the socket and stays in your hands" |
| `PORT_INCOMPAT` (install, **defect-class backstop**) | static, not DEFERRED: it fires only if `compatible_defs(h)` and the Graph disagree, which is a content or code defect | Graph `check_install_module` | A only (install `apply` step A1) | the same install bump; a debug build also raises a diagnostic |
| `NOT_TAKEN` | outcome | Assembly (TTL rule, MSA-6) | n/a | the verb family's one cue: weld sputter, cut "flame gutters and the seam holds", or the install bump (MSA-34) |

*Namespaces.* Layout's reasons surface as `VALIDATION_FAILED(layout.<REASON>)` (LY-29), the Graph's as its own, Body's as its own; owner reasons keep their owner's namespace. Only Assembly's own codes, `CUT_BLOCKED`, `NOT_DOCKED`, the install outcome `SLOT_OCCUPIED` and the `[VS-B]` `CUT_STRANDS`, are `MSA.<CODE>`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

#### C. Weld

**MSA-11 — Host and mate selection: the home-ship side goes first.** *Rule:* the Graph's section `kind` (LS-17: HOME or DERELICT) decides the order. `socket_a` is the socket whose section has `kind` HOME; the other is `socket_b`. The seat may stand on either side and may focus either socket; Assembly derives the ordered pair, so the surviving body is always the home-ship's and the absorbed derelict body is re-based (this answers Layout's LY-16 host-rule clarification and Open Question 21: confirmed). The **mate** is the unique `FREE` socket `b` for which `can_attach(a, b)` passes **and** `docked(a, b, frame_shift_px)` holds. Two non-home bodies `[VS]` tie-break on the lower `body_id`; several mates `[VS]` tie-break on the lowest `(socket_key_a, socket_key_b)` (formula D.3). MVP has exactly one pair: `sock_airlock_e` (home-ship, facing E) and `sock_b_w` (derelict B, facing W), `class_id` 1 (`SOCKET_CLASS` = DOCK_STD), `width_tiles` 2, lanes POWER lane 0 and O₂ lane 0, no COOLANT lane, one hatch each, weld offset `(696, 384)`. Assembly repeats the lane set and the offset as Derelict Authoring's must-repeat asks (DA-10). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-15. *Pillar:* P5 (the home-ship body survives). *Decides:* advisor R14; LY-16.

**MSA-12 — The player weld is offered only once the pair is docked.** *Rule:* `weld_offer(seat, socket)` is a pure read published by Assembly (formula D.2) and is the `eligible` predicate of Interaction's `begin_weld` declaration row: it is true iff the welder is the equipped tool, the seat is `lit` and `in_reach` of the socket, the socket is `FREE`, a unique mate passes `can_attach`, and `docked(a, b, frame_shift_px)` holds. No prompt exists unless the offer holds. `NOT_DOCKED` exists only as a backstop (T2). Web Map & Transit establishes `docked` (WM-21 `berth`; *Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: no longer provisional*); Body tests it; Assembly gates the *offer*, as BDY-27 asks ("the offer, not Body's `validate`, is gated"), so the player never sees "the ship jerking". Four consequences. (1) `weld_offer` reads only tool, light, reach, Layout socket state and Body `docked`; it never calls `can_join_static` or the full `can_join`, never reads a node or edge accessor and never reads `surge[]`, and it is a hot-path read (cached static half, O(1) `docked`; its per-call budget is `MSA_WELD_OFFER_BUDGET_US`, a placeholder in T9 whose value the spike sets). (2) `eligible` currently has one member, `actor_sane_gate` (IA-2), so adding `weld_offer` is an ASK to Interaction; the dependency runs Interaction -> Assembly read view while Assembly calls Interaction's mutators, so it is an injected read-view lookup, not a code import -> ADR candidate. (3) The channel lasts 360 ticks, so Transit could separate a pair during it: the mitigation order is (i) ASK to Transit — do not break `docked` for a pair while an ASSEMBLY weld channel is open on it (readable from `channel_view`) *(answered: WM-22 holds `docked` and makes `pod_hop`, `berth` and `unberth` `BUSY` while an ASSEMBLY channel is open; Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; (ii) the handoff pre-check re-tests `docked` (T2a), which closes all but one tick. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: Body's open request is accepted and the former fallback (iii) is deleted: the Pipeline's `BODY_TRANSFER` camera cut hides camera motion, not the exterior hull jump (BDY-27), so the offer never drops the `docked` term; `docked` is a hard term of the offer, closed by (i) and (ii).)* *(orchestrator ruling: the technical-director's option B, re-checking `docked` in the completion `validate`, is rejected because Body's locked text says weld legality takes no pose input; the handoff re-check is not a kernel `validate`, so it does not contradict it.)* (4) The visual cursor is Layout-supplied data drawn in world space (UI Requirements). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-16 (auto: offer false when not docked, no prompt), AC-MSA-17 (lint + spike: `weld_offer` source calls no `can_join*` or node accessor and meets its budget). *Pillar:* P1. *Alternative considered:* not gating and relying on the camera cut — kept as the named fallback only. *Decides:* advisor R17; BDY-27; TD-8, TD-16.

**MSA-13 — `q` is a data constant: never an input, never displayed.** *Rule:* `q = WELD_Q_DEFAULT` 850 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, loaded from the data file, range **`Q_MIN`(`LEAK_HP`) to 990, stated once in D.1** (400 to 990 at the default `LEAK_HP` 4000) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: every other mention of the range in this GDD cites D.1 instead of restating it)*, default 850 *(refines advisor R11, which ruled 400-1000: LSG D.2's floor form gives `A_seam_m` 0 from birth only at `q` of 993 or more, and 0 once `leak_bp` is low enough, and the Graph's own `SEAM_LEAK_INITIAL_BP` row calls "welds never weep" an R9 violation, so q 1000 would delete the weld's P1 tell)*. There is no player input, no modifier slot and no `Σ q_mods` term anywhere in the rules; the Vertical Slice options (a hold-longer trade, a weld-rod part) appear in Open Questions only, because a modifier slot in the rules invites progression creep. `q` goes into the plan handed to `weld_join` and the Graph stores it on the SeamRecord. `q` is never shown in any prompt, preview, tooltip or player-visible debug HUD; the Debug Overlay reads the true value.
