Its consequences are physical and come from the Graph, cited and never restated as Assembly's: `cap_seam` is 925 permille of the lesser port at q 850, `hp_seam` is 8500 (above `HP_OK` 7000), `leak_bp` starts at `SEAM_LEAK_INITIAL_BP` 10000 unscaled, and the D.2 seam area `A_seam_m` gives the weep (`LSG D.14`, `LSG D.2`; worked in D.4). **Named cliffs for the knob.** Below 400, `hp_seam = q * 10` falls below `LEAK_HP` 4000 and every MVP weld would start leaking at birth, which breaks P2 fairness (a leak with no prior tell); data outside the range is clamped at **both** ends, to `Q_MIN` (400 at the default `LEAK_HP`) or to 990, and logged `MSA_Q_CLAMPED`. **The upper cliff is q of 993 or more** (q = 1000 is the limiting case, LSG D.2 Output Range): `A_seam_m` is 0 from birth, the seam has no weep, and the weld's own P1 tell vanishes; at q 990 (`Q_MAX`) the fresh seam still weeps, at 2 milli flow units, for about 6.3 minutes (worked in D.1) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. **The lower cliff is q < 400**, named above. Default 850 keeps the Graph's q-dependent paths exercised only at one value in play; the Graph side tests q 400 and 1000. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-18 (lint: clamp and diagnostic), AC-MSA-19 (lint: `q` reaches no player-visible string or UI path), AC-MSA-20 (review: no modifier term). *Pillar:* P1, anti-progression. *Alternative considered:* `q` from hold time, a consumed weld rod or tool condition — rejected for MVP (channel length would become player-variable, no such Item DB def exists, tools have no durability). *Decides:* advisor R11; `LSG D.14`.

**MSA-14 — The weld `apply` runs in one fixed order; every call has a named proof.** *Rule:* after the completion's `validate` passes and the A0 deferred check is clear, `apply` runs in this order (table T3): Graph M2 `weld_join(plan)` -> Layout LM2 `attach_section(socket_a, socket_b, seam_id, welded_at_T)` -> Body BM3 `rebase_on_attach(surviving_body_id, absorbed_body_id, frame_shift_px)` -> Interaction `sync_section(section_a)` and `sync_section(section_b)` -> `assembly.state` (clear `pending`, write `last_result = DONE`). The order Graph -> Layout -> Body is Body's (BDY-21): BM3 or BM4 before its Layout mutator is `BDY_PLAN_MISMATCH`, a defect. Assembly allocates no id; the `seam_id` that LM2 needs is returned by M2 *(answered 2026-10-27: Graph LS-15, `weld_join` returns `{seam_id, opening_ids[]}`; Layout Open Question 4 closed; Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, and without that return the chain cannot be written (fallback: Assembly allocates, which advisor R6 rejects on H3 grounds). The apply is infallible *by proof*, not by hope: each call's precondition is a pure read already run in `validate`, and a call with no proving read is a FAULTED path and a design defect (TD-1). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **fault detection is explicit, because GDScript has no throw and no catch.** A runtime error ends the function with a default return and the caller carries on; `assert()` is compiled out of a release export. "Infallible by proof" therefore needs a detector that does not depend on either. The contract (MSA-31): every Class A mutator Assembly calls (M2, LM2, BM3 for a weld; M3, LM3, BM4 for a cut; M5 for an install) returns a status, `OK` or a `FAULT_CODE` naming the violated precondition; Assembly's `apply` checks the status after **every** call, and on the first non-`OK` it calls `ctx.fault(code, detail)` (kernel CR-19(a), ASK K2 answered *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*) and returns `ctx.complete()` at once, making no further mutator call. A mutator that returns `OK` but leaves a cross-block disagreement is caught by the release postconditions of MSA-32 (INV-1, INV-2, INV-4, INV-5 and INV-7 after each weld or cut) and by the always-on integer comparisons `MSA_SHIFT_DRIFT` (A3 against A4) and `MSA_VERDICT_DRIFT` (A0 against V4), which are promoted from debug assertions to release-fatal faults.)* One P5 notification batch results, with the Graph's keys `T`, `S/<seam_id>`, `SEC/<a>`, `SEC/<b>` (and nothing else from the Graph) plus Layout's `BODY/` and `SEC/` keys for moved sections and Body's keys. Weld `apply` is integer arithmetic only: no RNG, no wall-clock (H9). The Graph's keyed draws (`rng_derive(world_seed, "graph.fail", (step_no << 20) | subject_id)`) are unchanged by a weld or a cut because a weld only appends ids and never renumbers (LS-9, LS-15), so replay identity across a weld needs no Assembly term. Budget: M2 alone <= 0.2 ms Release (locked, Graph); the whole weld `apply` is a placeholder budget measured in the spike (Tuning Knobs). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-21 (auto: recorded call trace equals the T3 order; a BM call before its Layout call raises `BDY_PLAN_MISMATCH`), AC-MSA-22 (auto: exact P5 batch keys), AC-MSA-23 (spike: budget), AC-MSA-24 (auto: a table-driven test enumerates T3 and asserts every apply call has a proof entry), AC-MSA-142 and AC-MSA-143 (auto: a mutator returning a `FAULT_CODE`, and a half-applied A3, each fault the kernel in a release-configured build) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. *Pillar:* P1 (the world is never half-welded). *Decides:* advisor R6, R10; KRN CR-9; BDY-21.

**T3 — Weld apply ledger.**

| # | Phase | Call | Owner / class | Proof (pure read in `validate`, or total by contract) | Data in -> out | If the proof were wrong |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| V1 | validate | kernel authority (SYSTEM allowed; explicit `actor`) | Kernel | CR-5 | -> | `ORIGIN_NOT_ALLOWED`, Rejected |
| V2 | validate | `can_attach(socket_a, socket_b)` | Layout, pure | Layout's eight reasons | sockets -> verdict | Rejected `VALIDATION_FAILED(layout.<REASON>)` (LY-29); owner reasons keep their owner's namespace |
| V3 | validate | `socket_plan(a, b)`; add `q := WELD_Q_DEFAULT` | Layout, pure | returns `{pairs[], openings[], L_seam}` | -> plan | Rejected |
| V4 | validate | `can_join_static(plan)` | Graph, pure | nine structural reasons | plan -> verdict | Rejected |
| V5 | validate | `can_rebase_attach(surviving_body_id, absorbed_body_id, frame_shift_px)` (Body's published arity, BM3) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | Body, pure | `BDY_REBASE_SAME_BODY`, `BDY_REBASE_UNKNOWN_BODY` and `BDY_REBASE_SHIFT_OFF_GRID` (a published reason) | -> OK | Rejected with Body's reason in Body's namespace |
| A0 | apply | full `can_join(plan)`: compare structural verdict with V4 (`MSA_VERDICT_DRIFT`); read `PORT_ABSENT` | Graph, pure | same-slot guarantee (CR-2, CR-9) | -> deferred? | defect: `MSA_VERDICT_DRIFT` faults the kernel (release too) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| A1 | apply | **if `PORT_ABSENT`**: `last_result = BLOCKED(PORT_ABSENT)`, clear `pending`, return | own block | total | -> | none; the one "applied but did nothing" branch |
| A2 | apply | M2 `weld_join(plan)` | Graph, Class A | V4 + A0 (full `can_join OK` is M2's own precondition) | plan -> `{seam_id, opening_ids[]}` *(answered, LS-15; Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | FAULTED |
| A3 | apply | LM2 `attach_section(socket_a, socket_b, seam_id, welded_at_T)` | Layout, Class A | V2 plus the `seam_id` from A2 | -> `{surviving_body_id, absorbed_body_id, moved_sections[], frame_shift_px}` | FAULTED |
| A4 | apply | BM3 `rebase_on_attach(surviving, absorbed, frame_shift_px)` | Body, Class A | V5; the `frame_shift_px` of A3 equals Layout's pure prediction (`MSA_SHIFT_DRIFT`, formula D.5) | -> actors on the absorbed body moved | FAULTED (`BDY_PLAN_MISMATCH` if before A3) |
| A5 | apply | `sync_section(section_a)`, `sync_section(section_b)` | Interaction, Class B | total; fails dark on subject overflow, counted `IA_SUBJECT_OVERFLOW` (MSA-16) | -> subjects re-registered | none |
| A6 | apply | `assembly.state`: clear `pending`, `last_result = DONE(WELD, seam_id, T)` | own block | total | -> | none |

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Reading the "FAULTED" cells of T3, T4 and T5.** "FAULTED" means: the mutator at that step returned a non-`OK` status, or a release postcondition failed after it, and `apply` called `ctx.fault(code, detail)` (kernel CR-19) and stopped *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. It never means that an exception was thrown or caught: GDScript has none. The status contract (every Class A mutator returns `OK` or a `FAULT_CODE`) is published by the Graph, Layout and Body owners (Body's BDY-21 states it; CONFLICT C14 is closed against the kernel's CR-19) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; the S6 spike item (below) is the proof that the path works in an exported build. Postconditions that run in a release build after each weld or cut apply: the status of each of A2 to A4, `MSA_SHIFT_DRIFT` (A3's `frame_shift_px` equals V3's prediction), `MSA_VERDICT_DRIFT` (A0 equals V4), and the verifier subset INV-1, INV-2, INV-4, INV-5 and INV-7 (MSA-32). A `Logger`-style interception of engine errors is `[UNV]`: the engine reference has no scripting module, so whether a custom logger exists and sees script errors on 4.7.2 is verified in the spike (S6) before anything relies on it, and nothing in this GDD does.

**MSA-15 — A weld moves nothing; connecting is the player's later lit work.** *Rule:* the Graph creates the seam with **all** sub-edges disconnected: opening `area` 0, PIPE and DUCT valves CLOSED, CABLE breaker OPEN (LS-15). No pressure, temperature, charge or flow moves at the weld, onto a vacuum, frozen or dead section included. The weld's own P1 tell is the weep through `A_seam_m` from the step the seam exists. `can_join` warns and never rejects: it flags an opening whose `|dP|` exceeds `WELD_DP_WARN` 300 dkPa in `surge[]`. **no Assembly presenter reads `surge[]`**; the creak and rim frost are the Graph's own `SEAL_STRAIN` descriptor (VA-16), and an optional audio sting may read `surge[].warn` as a boolean only, never formatting a magnitude; the Graph's lint (LSG AC) is repeated here as an Assembly lint over the weld-cue source. Assembly never opens a hatch, valve or breaker for the player at the weld (MSA-17). Connecting afterwards is Interaction's V4 (`set_opening_area`), V5 (`set_valve`) and V3 (`set_breaker`), each with its own surge read-out. Breach during the channel: the Graph does nothing special; when `weld_join` applies the seam opening starts at 0, so the breach cannot cross into the other section until the player opens the hatch, and the full `can_join` at apply step A0 reports the now-larger `surge` (LS-15 and the Graph's edge cases). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-25 (auto: a fresh seam is fully disconnected on the apply tick), AC-MSA-26 (auto: weld onto a vacuum, frozen and unpowered section moves nothing), AC-MSA-27 (lint: the weld-cue source reads `surge[].warn` only). *Pillar:* P1. *Decides:* advisor R23, R24; LS-15.

**MSA-16 — Weld and cut re-register Interaction's subjects.** *Rule:* both completion applies end with `sync_section` on both sections. A weld turns the HULL plate into a door with two jambs and a leaf (LY-15); without re-registration the seam door has no DOOR_FACE subject, the seam is unopenable and the Graph's "connecting is then three lit verbs" would be false. A cut turns the door back into a HULL plate and the subjects must be removed (and, for a RAGGED cut, `stubs[]` entries created for every Graph `open_end` not already recorded; MSA-20). `sync_section` is Class B, idempotent and total: it never refuses and never faults, so it is legal as a trailing call in an infallible apply; but it is **not safe**: at the subject cap an object fails dark (counted `IA_SUBJECT_OVERFLOW`), which for a seam door reads as a hallucinated dead door (a P4 breach by accident). The defence is a content lint, not a runtime reject: the post-weld loaded set must satisfy `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` 120 plus `PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE` 24 <= 144 with the seam's door faces counted (at most four subjects for the MVP pair; Interaction's worked example puts `{home+B}` at about 156 before player drops against a hard cap of 192, and the loaded set `{home+B, A}` at about 190, so the breach is "A also loaded", a Web Map and Authoring loading decision, not the weld) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **the figures above are superseded by the registered ones, 143 and 175** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* (were 144 and 176 before Derelict Authoring's tightened part ledger, L_seed B 3). Interaction's registered `legal_loaded_sets` give `S_total` 143 for S3 (home + B welded) and 175 for S4 `{home+B, A}`, against the hard cap of 192 (D.14, AC-MSA-29b). Every registered neighbourhood's `F + L_seed + C` is at most `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` 120 (home 70, A 50, B 61, home+B 113), so no WARN remains; nothing in the weld depends on it)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*. Asks to Interaction: `sync_section` marks only the subjects whose registered state changed (otherwise a weld could approach the `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256 collapse in `interaction.state`); and IA-37's caller list gains weld and cut. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-28 (auto: after a weld the seam door has DOOR_FACE subjects; after a cut it has none), AC-MSA-29 (auto: subject count including the seam's door faces satisfies the lint, no `IA_SUBJECT_OVERFLOW`). *Pillar:* P1, P4. *Decides:* advisor R8; IA-37.

**MSA-17 — Calls Assembly does not make.** *Rule:* weld and cut call **no Emitter mutator**: both sections are already instantiated, E1 and E2 run only in instantiate and discard applies (Web Map's in MVP), and the Emitter rebuilds from Layout's `SEC/` and `BODY/` dirty keys through its `body_id` partition (EM-30). Assembly calls neither M13 `set_opening_area` (opening the seam hatch is Interaction's V4; a cut opens and closes nothing by itself, the Graph tombstones or holes the openings) nor M18 `apply_damage` (no impact side effect exists in MVP). It never calls `push_stimulus` (MSA-29), never calls Save/Load (MSA-33), and never calls the Graph's M4 `discard_section` or Layout's LM4 (MSA-24). **It calls none of Body's BM1 `place_actor`, BM2 `remove_actor`, BM5 `transfer_actor` or BM6 `set_body_velocity`:** actor placement, removal (despawn) and pod transfer belong to Web Map & Transit and respawn, and the detach default velocity 0 is BM4's own business (BDY-26, BDY-28). Assembly calls exactly two Body mutators, BM3 and BM4. Body's BM2 caller column currently reads "Transit, Assembly (despawn)": refresh owed under C7. It writes no perception state and reads no `PERCEPTION_OUT` block (MSA-35). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-30 (lint: none of these calls appears in Assembly's source). *Pillar:* P1, P3. *Decides:* advisor R3, R8, R23. CONFLICTS C6, C8.

#### D. Seam cut

**MSA-18 — The seam cut is offered only from the component that keeps its body.** *Rule:* `seam_cut_offer(seat, socket)` (formula D.2) is true iff the cutter is the equipped tool, the seat is `lit` and `in_reach` of a `WELD_SOCKET` subject whose socket is `WELDED` on an `ACTIVE` seam, **and the seat's foot-origin section lies in the component that keeps its `body_id`** (the component containing the body's `origin_section_id`, Layout LY-17; in MVP the home-ship side). On the derelict side of a welded seam no cut prompt exists. This is a geometry-plus-own-position read, so it is IMMEDIATE and P4-safe; the begin-time backstop is the existing reason `NOT_ELIGIBLE`, deliberately **not** `CUT_BLOCKED`, which stays the clean "a human is in the passage" oracle (MSA-21). It needs a Layout read: ASK L1, a pure `detach_preview(seam_id) -> {keep_sections[], leave_sections[], moved_sections[], frame_shift_px, rects[]}` computing the same components as LM3 and `LY D.12` without mutating (if refused, Assembly would derive components itself from `welds[]`, a duplicate of Layout logic that is not recommended). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **answered**; Layout publishes `detach_preview` in its read table (Layout 1.5 (read table), "the same components and re-base as LM3 / D.12 computed without mutation") and lists it in its Assembly row. ASK L1 is closed.)* *Why:* body transfer follows the actor's foot origin (BDY-23). A player who cuts from the derelict side ends alone on a new, sealed, drifting body with no free socket (both are terminal) and no pod: an unrecoverable, untelegraphed trap whose only recovery path (Web Map & Transit) is out of MVP scope (WM-23: stranded-crew recovery deferred to Vertical Slice A / B). A trap fails "no lie is unfalsifiable" and the flow principle that the cost of a failure be proportional to its frequency. *Cost:* the player cannot abandon the home-ship and keep the derelict; that play is deferred to Vertical Slice design with a Web Map recovery path (Open Question 3; Default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20): D57) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. *(orchestrator ruling: adopts the systems-designer's keep-side rule and the game-designer's G6 option A; **rejects** the systems-designer's second clause, "no cut while anyone is on the leaving piece", as a **blanket gate** for MVP and Vertical Slice A. It is a far-side crewmate gate, which the game-designer's G8 and the binding decision "death is a bitch; it happens" exclude for those builds, and in solo play it is vacuous. The creative-director's `CUT_STRANDS` proposal is **adopted by default for Vertical Slice B by user ruling D53** (below) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. CD-GDD-ALIGN revision 2026-10-26: the ruling stands, but it is no longer written as an unqualified "there is no gate"; the victim's counterplay is a rule of this section and the Vertical Slice B outcome is tied to a recovery path.)*

**The victim's counterplay (rule, CD-GDD-ALIGN) `[VS-B]`.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the whole paragraph needs a crewmate on the leaving piece, so it is co-op, is not in the MVP and gates nothing in the MVP. The `CUT_BLOCKED` reject itself, the hatch-cap hazard of Body BDY-24, is an MVP rule and is exercised in the MVP with a Body double (AC-MSA-36); only the far-side crewmate's use of it as a veto is `[VS-B]`.)* A keep-side cut can leave a crewmate on the leaving piece, so the victim is given a way to refuse it, using only rules this GDD already owns. (1) *The tell.* The cut channel is lit, still and loud: `SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS` 240 `[PLACEHOLDER]` (4 s) of TOOL noise on every accrued tick and one `LOUD` at DONE (MSA-29). The closed leaf is solid and occluding (Layout's class table), so on the far side the flame is not seen; **the tell the victim gets is the sound of the burn through the hull** (ASK AU1 to Adaptive Audio and Failure & Trail: the seam-cut TOOL loop is a positional sound audible on both sides of a closed seam). (2) *The veto.* A closed leaf cannot be entered, so the far-side crewmate opens the hatch from their own side with the ordinary DOOR_FACE verb (V4) and steps into the passage. `actors_overlapping` over the seam's two hatch rectangles then lists them and the cut is rejected `MSA.CUT_BLOCKED` at completion (MSA-21, D.13): the crewmate is never sealed out, stranded or crushed, and the cutter has paid the 4 s and the noise. The reach is in budget *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the veto distance was stated three ways (8 tiles, 10 tiles, 480 px) and ignored reaction time; it is now one formula)*: the veto radius is **`R_veto = (D - reaction_ticks - door_open_ticks) x V_WALK`**, with `D` = `SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS` 240, `reaction_ticks` = `VETO_REACTION_TICKS` 90 `[PLACEHOLDER]` (1.5 s from the onset of the burn sound to the first step), `door_open_ticks` Interaction's door-tap time `[UNV]`, 30 as a worked placeholder, and `V_WALK` 2 px per tick (Controller): `R_veto = (240 - 90 - 30) x 2` = 240 px = 5 tiles at the placeholders. **Boundary:** the authoritative check is the completion `validate` in P2 of `t0 + D + 1` = `t0 + 241` (D.7), which reads the body poses committed by P3 of `t0 + 240`; a foot box that is inside a hatch rectangle by then blocks the cut, so the crewmate has `D - reaction_ticks` ticks, minus the door tap, to cover `R_veto`. The handoff pre-check (site 2) runs in P3 of `t0 + 240` and may read the poses either before or after the movement hook of that same tick (the hook order of `assembly.step` against the Controller's movement step is `[UNV]`, ASK K1); the completion `validate` at site 3 is therefore the only site the veto can rely on, and AC-MSA-123 uses a Body-double test ingress that places the foot box at a chosen tick so that the order is fixed. A seat further than `R_veto`, downed, asleep or unaware cannot arrive, and that residue is the honest remainder of the harm path. (3) *The cost of the veto.* Opening the hatch makes the seam unprepared, so **a victim who opens the hatch and does not stand in the passage turns the cut RAGGED** (the mode is re-derived at completion, MSA-19, E-20); the veto is stepping in, not merely opening. (4) *What it is not.* No sanity read, ownership check or pose oracle is added: `CUT_BLOCKED` reads true actor presence only (MSA-21). It is a veto available to the victim, not a gate on the cutter. Criteria: AC-MSA-123 (a far-side crewmate who reaches the hatch passage before completion always blocks the cut), AC-MSA-75 (the cut proceeds, with `abs` conserved, when no one is in the passage).

**The Vertical Slice B outcome: `CUT_STRANDS` is gated by default (rule, user ruling D53) `[VS-B]`.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): the creative-director recommendation is adopted. The earlier condition ("no gate only if Transit ships a recovery path and the far-side tell is confirmed") is withdrawn: neither a Transit recovery path (ASK T2, answered by WM-23 as none in MVP) nor the audible far-side tell (ASK AU1) lifts the gate. Nothing in the MVP depends on this paragraph: solo play has no other seat, so the gate is vacuous in the MVP and Vertical Slice A, where the no-blanket-gate ruling of MSA-18 and "death is a bitch; it happens" stand.)* A `CHANNEL` seam cut is rejected `MSA.CUT_STRANDS` at the **handoff and completion sites only** (never at begin, so probing costs the channel's time and noise and is not a free occupancy oracle) while another seat's foot origin lies in the leaving component, **unless the cutter's Sanity `harm_eligible` bit is set** (Sanity SN-10, SN-11, SN-13: system-gated by the actor's authoritative sanity, preceded by the victim tell, recorded in `incidents` and attributed to the web; ASK F2, now an adopted read and no longer conditional). The check reads true positions (`detach_preview.leave_sections[]` and Body's actor poses), never perceived identity, so a hallucinated crewmate does not change it (MSA-21's truth-oracle line applies). "No gate" is an exception that needs a recorded user ruling; a Transit recovery path and the far-side tell are mitigations that stack with the gate, not conditions that remove it. `MSA.CUT_STRANDS` joins the reason table (T2) tagged `[VS-B]`; it is outside the MVP's raised reasons because nothing in the MVP can raise it. Criterion: AC-MSA-124.

*Criterion:* AC-MSA-31 (auto: no prompt on the derelict side, prompt on the home side). *Pillar:* P5, flow. *Alternative considered:* (B) allow either side with a diegetic tell — rejected: a new presentation system and the unrecoverable state remains; (C) allow either side and rely on the autosave — rejected: an unfair failure that the autosave hides and that teaches players cutting is dangerous. *Decides:* game-designer G6; advisor R4 (the cut is a derived, state-independent offer).

**MSA-19 — The cut mode is derived from the seam's true prepared state at apply, never chosen.** *Rule:* `cut_mode(seam)` (formula D.6) is **SEALED** iff, for this seam, every OPENING has `area == 0`, every PIPE and DUCT sub-edge valve is CLOSED and every CABLE breaker is OPEN (exactly as `weld_join` left it); **RAGGED** otherwise. The reads are positions, never health fields: completion is SYSTEM-origin and the channel cost has been paid, so the read is admitted (MSA-9). It uses a Graph read `seam_view(seam_id) -> {state, sub_edges[{kind, id}], leak_bp}` (*Amended 2026-10-27 — design review:* **answered**; the Graph publishes the pure `seam_view(seam_id) -> {state, sub_edges[{kind, id}], leak_bp, opening_ids[]}` in LS-15; ASK G3 is closed, and ASK G10 asks for the uncapped `open_end` count of a `CUT` seam, MSA-33). No payload carries a mode and the prompt is the same in both cases (no "clean cut / burn through" toggle). The derived mode is passed identically to M3 and LM3. A RAGGED cut is EXOGENOUS and player-caused, so it is exempt from the fairness gate (LS-16): per former opening a virtual hole `A_CUT` 24 on each side as a hull stub of `hp` 2500 (severity 4 BREACH), fluid ports `open_end` leaking at `OPEN_END_RATE_PM` until `cap_port` (M12, Interaction's `begin_cap`); a 20-tile room reaches the DECOMP band in 382 steps (38 s) and VACUUM in 960 steps, all cited from the Graph *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. A clean cut is earned by lit physical work (V4 close, V5, V3). The mode is re-derived at completion, so a hatch opened during a cancelled-and-resumed channel makes the cut RAGGED; the witnesses apply (MSA-23). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-32 (auto: the SEALED/RAGGED matrix over hatch, valve and breaker positions), AC-MSA-33 (lint: the derivation reads positions only). *Pillar:* P1, P4. *Alternative considered:* two commands `..._clean` / `..._ragged` with two prompts — rejected: a player-chosen "kind" in the command type; always SEALED in MVP — rejected: it removes the Graph's `A_CUT` and `open_end` consequences from the weld-and-cut story. *Decides:* advisor R15; IA-11(d).

**MSA-20 — The cut `apply` runs in one fixed order.** *Rule:* (table T4) Graph M3 `cut_detach(seam_id, boundary_mode)` -> Layout LM3 `detach_section(seam_id, boundary_mode)` -> Body BM4 `rebase_on_detach(old_body_id, new_body_id, moved_sections[], frame_shift_px, rects[])` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* (`rects[]` is taken from Layout's real `detach_section` record, equal to V3's `detach_preview().rects[]`) -> Interaction `sync_section` x2 -> `assembly.state`. One P5 batch with the Graph's `T`, `S/<seam_id>` and affected `C/<id>` keys (LS-16), plus Layout's and Body's keys. The detached body translates **without rotation** (`heading_q` 0, BDY-26 and the Pipeline's routed constraint) and its drift default is 0 (`JOLT_PX` 0, BDY-28). Detach follows BDY-23 (the foot-origin section picks the side; actors on the new component get `pos_curr -= frame_shift_px` and `sub := (0,0)`; `abs(actor) = world(body) + pos_curr` is conserved). **A weld followed by a cut of the same seam is not in general an identity on `pos_curr`**, so tests assert `abs` and section-local position, never raw `pos_curr`. For a RAGGED cut, `sync_section` must create `stubs[]` entries for every Graph `open_end` left and not already recorded (today only V8's apply creates stubs): without them a RAGGED cut's O₂-lane open ends could never be capped, a port hiss the player can never stop, which breaks P2 fairness (ASK I1). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-34 (auto: call-order trace), AC-MSA-35 (auto: `heading_q` 0, no rotation; `abs` conserved). *Pillar:* P1, P2. *Decides:* advisor R8, R10, R21; BDY-23, BDY-26.

**T4 — Seam-cut apply ledger.**

| # | Phase | Call | Owner / class | Proof | Data | If wrong |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| V1 | validate | kernel authority | Kernel | CR-5 | | Rejected |
| V2 | validate | `can_detach(seam_id)` | Layout, pure | `SEAM_UNKNOWN`, `SEAM_NOT_ACTIVE` | | Rejected |
| V3 | validate | `detach_preview(seam_id)` prediction of `moved_sections[]`, `frame_shift_px`, `rects[]` (`LY D.12`) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | Layout, pure *(ASK L1)* | pure formula | -> prediction | |
| V4 | validate | `can_rebase_detach(old_body_id, frame_shift_px)` (Body's published arity, BM4; `frame_shift_px` is V3's `detach_preview` value) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | Body, pure | `BDY_REBASE_UNKNOWN_BODY` (the old body is not live in Layout), `BDY_REBASE_POSE_OUT_OF_RANGE` (`world_old + frame_shift_px` beyond the world limit) and `BDY_VEL_OUT_OF_RANGE` (BDY-21, BDY-26); the `moved_sections[]` / `frame_shift_px` equality proof stays in V3 (`detach_preview`, ASK L1) and in the A4 `MSA_SHIFT_DRIFT` assertion | -> OK | Rejected with Body's reason in Body's namespace |
| V5 | validate | `actors_overlapping(body_id, [hatch_rect_a, hatch_rect_b])` empty | Body, pure | `LY-15` rectangles `width_tiles * 48` by 24 px | -> list | `CUT_BLOCKED` (CHANNEL); deferral (CASCADE) |
| V6 | validate (assert) | `body_count + 1 <= MAX_BODIES` (hard: Layout rejects `CAPACITY` before any Body mutator) and `body_count + 1 <= BODIES_LIVE_MAX` (warn-only, `MSA_BODIES_LIVE_WARN`) | Body, pure | MSA-41; the same split as INV-6 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | | debug assertion for the first, a diagnostic for the second |
| A1 | apply | derive `boundary_mode` (D.6) | Graph read (`seam_view`) | positions only | -> mode | |
| A2 | apply | M3 `cut_detach(seam_id, boundary_mode)` | Graph, Class A | V2; "cutting through a compartment" is unrepresentable (LS-16) | -> tombstones or holes | FAULTED |
| A3 | apply | LM3 `detach_section(seam_id, boundary_mode)` | Layout, Class A | V2, V3 | -> `{new_body_id, moved_sections[], frame_shift_px, rects[]}` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | FAULTED |
| A4 | apply | BM4 `rebase_on_detach(old, new, moved_sections[], frame_shift_px, rects[])` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | Body, Class A | V4; A3's `frame_shift_px` and `rects[]` equal V3's (`MSA_SHIFT_DRIFT` also compares `rects[]`; a mismatch is the same fault) | -> actors on `C_new` shifted; net: Body returns `-BDY_REBASE_ACTOR_IN_CAP` if an actor overlaps `rects[]` (BDY-24 iii); written nothing; the apply faults the kernel (MSA-31) | FAULTED (`BDY_PLAN_MISMATCH` if before A3; `-BDY_REBASE_ACTOR_IN_CAP`) |
| A5 | apply | `sync_section` x2 (door faces back to HULL; `stubs[]` for RAGGED open ends) | Interaction, Class B | total | | none |
| A6 | apply | `assembly.state`: clear `pending`, `last_result = DONE(SEAM_CUT, seam_id, mode, T)` | own block | total | | none |

**MSA-21 — `CUT_BLOCKED`: a CHANNEL cut rejects, a CASCADE cut defers; it is a kept oracle.** *Rule:* this is Body's named must-repeat 1 (the hatch-cap hazard, BDY-24). If `actors_overlapping(body_id, [the seam's two hatch rectangles])` is non-empty, a `CHANNEL` cut **rejects** with `MSA.CUT_BLOCKED` at begin, at handoff and at completion (MSA-7), and a `CASCADE` cut defers (MSA-22). The ship never closes a seam on a crewmate. Body adds no push-out (BDY-24 struck it). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* V5 is the only safety on both paths (CHANNEL rejects, CASCADE defers/abandons); Body's BM4 net exists for a caller that bypassed V5 and is never reached in a clean build; V4 (`can_rebase_detach(old_body_id, frame_shift_px)`) and the MSA-7 reason order are unchanged. The handoff and completion cue (the seam-cut family's one post-channel non-DONE cue, MSA-34): *the flame gutters and the seam holds*, and the channel has ended; the crewmate in the hatch is the witness; the `LOUD` was already pushed at DONE by Interaction (MSA-29). **Kept oracle:** `CUT_BLOCKED` reads true actor presence in the hatch, not module-body state, so it passes IA-11; it is deliberately a truth channel for hallucination classes (a) and (c) (a phantom entity is not an actor, a real person is), costs one attempt and zero time, and the Perception Filter must never wrap it. It is also a griefing vector in co-op (a crewmate can stand in the hatch to veto a cut), recorded as a Vertical Slice B concern `[VS-B]`. **The cutter's own body.** `actors_overlapping` lists every actor including the cutter, and each rectangle is `width_tiles * 48` by 24 px (96 x 24 px for the MVP pair), `REACH_PX` is 72 and the foot box is `FOOT_BOX_W` 16 by `FOOT_BOX_H` 8. If the `WELD_SOCKET` subject's anchor sat inside a rectangle, the cutter's own body would block their own cut at all three sites, a defect no unit test with a stub actor would find. Required resolution (a): Layout and Interaction place the subject's anchor and reach so that a legal lit stance exists outside both rectangles with at least one foot-box half-extent of clearance, enforced by an authoring lint `SOCKET_STANCE_BAND_EMPTY` (ASK L2; *Amended 2026-10-27 — design review:* **answered**: Layout carries the lint `SOCKET_STANCE_BAND_EMPTY` (LY-30) and the registered `SOCKET_STANCE_CLEARANCE_PX` 8 (Layout Tuning Knobs); the rectangle coordinates of AC-MSA-36 and 37 remain `[UNV]` until Layout publishes them). The alternative, an exclusion parameter on `actors_overlapping` (ASK to Body), is not preferred. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-36 (auto: reject at the three sites with a real second actor), AC-MSA-37 (spike: one actor at maximum legal reach starts a SEALED cut without `CUT_BLOCKED`; one foot-box into a rectangle fires it). *Pillar:* P4, P1, anti-pillars. *Decides:* advisor R10, R16; BDY-24; game-designer G5, G7.

**MSA-22 — The CASCADE (system) cut is specified and submitted by nothing in MVP. `[VS]`** *Rule:* `seam_cut {cause CASCADE}` exists in the command table and its rules are specified and tested through a stub submitter; no MVP system submits one (Horror and Attention have no cut verb; the breach rhythm uses STRESS and M17, not detaches). It **defers** while `actors_overlapping` is non-empty for the seam's hatch rectangles; it is preceded by the Pillar 2 tell window that Assembly and Adaptive Audio own (the 10-30 s escalating tell) and by the Graph's `W_MIN_WARNING_S` 10 s fairness gate (ENDOGENOUS transitions only); it expires after `CASCADE_CUT_DEFER_MAX_TICKS` `[PLACEHOLDER]` (a multiple of `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6, formula D.10). On expiry with an actor still in the hatch rectangles the cut is **abandoned, never forced** ("the ship will not close a seam on a crewmate", BDY-24). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* The CASCADE cut is the 'crisis cut' of Layout LY-17; its entombment guard is this deferral plus the BM4 net. `assembly.state` gains the deferral record `{seam_id, first_deferred_T}` at Vertical Slice; the field is reserved in schema v1. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-38 (auto, stub submitter: defer, apply when clear, abandon on expiry). *Pillar:* P2. *Alternative considered:* a scripted system cut for the breach rhythm in MVP — rejected (scope; the tell is not built). *Decides:* advisor R16.

**MSA-23 — A cut's mode must be predictable from the world alone.** *Rule:* at any moment between "prompt offered" and "channel complete", a player who has looked at the seam within the last `CUT_WITNESS_LOOKBACK_S` 60 `[PLACEHOLDER]` (a manual-AC parameter for AC-MSA-39, not a sim knob, and unrelated to `OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS`) can tell from the world alone whether the cut will be SEALED or RAGGED. The witnesses are the consequence channel, owned elsewhere: a hatch not fully closed is visibly open with a draught across the threshold (Layout hatch presentation, Graph opening area); a closed hatch under a pressure gradient creaks (`SEAL_STRAIN`); under a thermal gradient its rim frosts (`HATCH_DT_FROST_MC` 20000 mC); an open seam valve or a closed seam breaker must have a witness at the lane (a hiss from the open valve's descriptor, the junction's own powered state), **and that witness is a BLOCKING dependency, not a provisional ask** (CD-GDD-ALIGN; *Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the "BLOCKING" label now attaches only to AC-MSA-126 and to the fallback, see item 4 at the end of this rule*): at zero gradient (equal pressure and temperature across an open seam valve, or a closed seam breaker with nothing to carry) there is no hiss, creak or frost, so a class-(b) lie on the valve or breaker sprite alone would make a RAGGED cut with no witness, failing P1, P2 and P4 together. **Fallback written now:** until the Graph and Failure & Trail confirm in their GDDs a consequence-channel lane witness for each of those two states that exists at zero gradient (ASK G8 to the Graph, ASK FT1 to Failure & Trail), the Perception Filter **may not wrap the seam-lane valve and breaker sprites**: the sprites of the seam's own PIPE and DUCT valves and CABLE breaker are classed **TRUTH at the seam** (they render the true position, with no class-(b) lie and no violet gleam), while every other valve, breaker, lamp and gauge stays corruptible. The fallback lifts only when both owners confirm the witnesses and the Graph or Failure & Trail rule it in writing; it is then a content change to the class column of the Truth-channel table, with no change to this rule's shape. AC-MSA-126 covers the zero-gradient case; AC-MSA-39 cannot be signed off before it. An as-welded seam is **quiet** (no creak, frost or hiss). If a fresh weld could emit `SEAL_STRAIN` on a closed hatch with a latent gradient, the "quiet seam" witness would be false, which is why the first-connect beat needs a small or deliberate gradient (a verify handed to the Graph and Derelict Authoring owners). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review, three corrections.)* **(1) "Quiet" is scoped.** The Graph's LS-15 says an as-welded seam "is not quiet": it weeps (a HISS at the seam) from its first run until wear-in ends (about 16.7 minutes). That weep is the same whether the cut will be SEALED or RAGGED, so it is **not** a cut-mode witness; "quiet" in this rule, in D.6, in VA-16 and in AC-MSA-93 means *no creak, no rim frost, no valve hiss and no draught*, not *no sound at all*. The seam weep's hiss is anchored on the bead and loops, an open seam valve's hiss is anchored on the lane; Failure & Trail must keep the two distinguishable by anchor (ASK FT2, `[UNV]`, a playtest item of AC-MSA-39). **(2) Starting gradient is a content requirement, not a verify.** The first connect (Beat 2) must have something to read, so Derelict Authoring must author derelict B's starting state so that at least one of `SEAL_STRAIN` (a pressure difference over `WELD_DP_WARN`) or rim frost (a temperature difference over `HATCH_DT_FROST_MC`) fires on the closed seam hatch on the first Graph step after the weld (ASK D3; visually at once, its creak sounds after the 1 s quiet window of Beat 1 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*); AC-MSA-140 is the fixture check. This does not make the seam lie: it is B's true state. **(3) MSA-23b, "feel the seam",** is added below as the hand-on-the-seam witness. **(4) What is BLOCKING and what is not.** AC-MSA-126 (the TRUTH fallback) stays gating. The BLOCKING label is removed from AC-MSA-39 and AC-MSA-93: with FT1 answered and the fallback in force, they depend on playtest evidence and on the Graph's G8 answer, not on a missing mechanism. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-39 (manual: pooled, at least 17 of 20 judgements correct across 5 first-time players and 4 scenarios, no ragged cut reported as "random"; see Validation targets for the scoring rule). *Pillar:* P1, P2. *Decides:* game-designer G9.

**MSA-23b — Feel the seam: a QUIET consequence-channel read at the seam. `[PROVISIONAL — ASK IW1]`** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; creative-director position, game-designer option (a))*. *Rule:* the Inspect View owner (In-World Instruments & Inspect View, Designed) defines a QUIET, no-tool, no-noise `inspect` read on the seam's hatch frame: while the seat holds `inspect` within `REACH_PX` of a `WELDED` seam's frame for the owner's hold time, the presenter shows the **true positions** the cut mode reads (D.6): the hatch leaf open or shut, each seam sub-edge valve open or shut, the seam breaker open or closed, as the existing TRUTH wheel and lever frames, enlarged and held in the card grammar the owner already has. It shows **no number, no mode word ("clean" or "ragged"), no `hp`, `state`, `forced_mode`, `served_pm`, `q` or `leak_bp`**, and it reads the same positions that are already TRUTH sprites under the MSA-23 fallback, so it adds no new oracle (it is a larger, deliberate look at what the sprites already show) and costs time, which is the P4 price. It is a consequence-channel read that exists at zero gradient, which is the witness ASK G8 and FT1 asked for: with FT1 answered (Failure & Trail concurs that no zero-gradient descriptor exists, FT row 21) and this read in place, the Graph's G8 can be answered "permanent TRUTH for the seam's own sub-edge sprites, plus MSA-23b". It delivers the Player Fantasy promise "put my hand on the seam" (L44, L76). It is not a seam-health readout. *If IW1 is declined:* L44 and L76 are rewritten to the witnesses that exist (hiss, creak, frost, the true seam-lane sprites) and the fallback of MSA-23 stays in force permanently. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-145 (auto, with an Inspect View double) and AC-MSA-126 (unchanged, still gating). *Pillar:* P1, P4.

**MSA-24 — The detached piece stays as its own Layout body; Assembly never discards.** *Rule:* after a cut the piece keeps a Layout body and a Body pose row (an orphan if empty; Body's BDY-4 and its cut edge case). Unload, `discard_section` (M4 + LM4 + E2 + subject unregister) and the orphan body are Web Map & Transit's. Assembly adds no shake, impulse or push. Items lying in the piece are not Assembly's concern in MVP. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-40 (auto: the body persists after a cut; Assembly source calls neither M4 nor LM4). *Pillar:* P5. *Decides:* advisor R21.

**MSA-25 — Sockets are terminal.** *Rule:* a socket goes `FREE -> WELDED(seam_id) -> CAPPED` (SEALED cut) or `CUT_OPEN` (RAGGED cut) and **never back to `FREE`** in MVP (LY-13). Weld, then cut, then a second weld attempt on the same socket yields `SOCKET_NOT_FREE` in the world with no prompt; the playtest gate must not require a second weld. Illegal transitions: `FREE -> CAPPED`, `WELDED -> FREE`, `CAPPED -> WELDED`, any state to `FREE`. There is no `install_cap_plate` and no `set_socket_state` mutator (LY-27): a socket's state changes only inside LM2 and LM3, atomic with the Graph's weld and cut. The socket economy (one home socket, terminal scars, net cycle minus one) is **not solved here** and is recorded `[VS]` with Layout's three options (a re-cut verb that reopens a `CAPPED` socket keeping history; more home sockets; derelicts that bring three or more sockets) and the lint `SOCKET_ECONOMY_LOW`. **It is an explicit Vertical Slice GATE criterion, not an open question (CD-GDD-ALIGN):** MVP can play the core P5 loop (salvage integrates into the body that survives; deeper is richer) exactly once, which is acceptable for MVP only; the Vertical Slice may not close, and `/gate-check` may not pass it, until one option is chosen, authored and played through `VS_WELD_CYCLES_MIN` `[PLACEHOLDER]` 3 consecutive weld cycles with `SOCKET_ECONOMY_LOW` silent (AC-MSA-125, Open Question 7), so that "net cycle minus one" cannot ship past the Vertical Slice and leave the assembly fantasy unrepeatable. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-41 (auto: weld, cut, second weld returns `SOCKET_NOT_FREE`, no prompt). *Pillar:* P5. *Decides:* advisor R20, R22; LY-13.

#### E. Install

**MSA-26 — Install is Assembly's own TAP, with a carve-out to carrying.** *Rule:* `install_module {hardpoint_ref}` (PLAYER, TAP). The prompt exists iff the seat carries an instance whose def is in `compatible_defs(h)` for the focused HARDPOINT subject; that is static Item DB data, so it is IMMEDIATE, and an incompatible install is simply never offered (Item DB: "refused with `PORT_INCOMPAT`, never offered"). IMMEDIATE `validate`: kernel authority, carrying, `OUT_OF_RANGE`, `NOT_LIT`, `INVALID_TARGET`, `NOT_ELIGIBLE`, not staggered or downed, and a `PORT_INCOMPAT` backstop. **CONFLICT against two locked GDDs (C1, ANSWERED: PC-8 and IA-28 now carry the carve-out, AC-IA-78; Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review):** the Controller's PC-8 and Interaction's IA-28 both rejected "every `interact` mode except `drop`" while carrying as `BUSY`. Carve-out sentence for the Controller owner (PC-8) and the Interaction owner (IA-28): *"while carrying, `interact` on a HARDPOINT subject whose `compatible_defs(h)` lists the carried instance's def is admitted, alongside `drop`."* ASK to Interaction (ASK I1): a declaration row, object class HARDPOINT, `tool_required` NONE, a carry precondition, owner Assembly, `noise_class` QUIET. The `begin_weld` and `begin_seam_cut` CHANNELS stay inadmissible while carrying (PC-7; a torch needs two hands). *Named fallback if the Controller owner refuses:* drop first, then install from LOOSE_WORLD with bare hands, which amends the Item DB's instance row 8 instead (source state LOOSE_WORLD). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-42 (auto: prompt iff the carried def is compatible), AC-MSA-43 (auto: every other `interact` mode is still `BUSY` while carrying). *Pillar:* P5, anti-pillar. *Alternative considered:* (a) install as a `drop` onto a hardpoint — rejected: silently overloads Interaction's `drop` and a mis-drop installs; (c) drop-then-install — rejected as primary: the module lies loose between steps and the trophy is on the floor before the payoff. *Decides:* advisor R1.

**MSA-27 — Install `apply`: check first, then the chain (table T5).** *Rule:* `apply` first calls the pure Graph `check_install_module` (Item DB spelling). On a fail it writes `last_result = BLOCKED(SLOT_OCCUPIED)` (Assembly's outcome name for the Graph's "slot not ABSENT or SALVAGED" failure; *Amended 2026-10-27 — design review:* the Graph's M5 row now names that reason `SLOT_OCCUPIED` itself, so the mapping is one to one and ASK G9 is closed) (or `BLOCKED(PORT_INCOMPAT)` as a backstop), changes nothing else and the instance stays carried (IA-24's TAP-outcome precedent). On a pass, in order: Graph M5 `install_module(slot_node_id, def_id, hp_start := condition_bp, hp_cap := cap_bp, patched_at_step)` -> Suit `release_carried(player_id)` (SE-13; "Suit never self-releases") -> Controller `clear_carry_ref(actor)` *(published by the Controller, PC-1 b and PC-8; *Amended 2026-10-27 — design review*)* -> Interaction `sync_section(section_id)` -> `last_result = DONE`. The instance dissolves (Item DB row 8: CARRIED -> INSTALLED). A new store's stock is 0. Cap and decay-clock carry-through follows Item DB IDB-18. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **answered by the Graph; the earlier "provisional, AC blocked" text is withdrawn.** M5 now takes `hp_cap` (default `HP_FULL`) and `patched_at_step` (default 0): "a patched module reinstalls capped and keeps its absolute `patched_at_step` decay clock; a store installs empty" (Graph M5 row, consistency pass 2026-10-27). The install apply therefore passes `hp_start := condition_bp`, `hp_cap := cap_bp` and `patched_at_step := patched_at_step` from the carried instance's fields (Item DB instance rows `condition_bp`, `cap_bp`, `patched_at_step`) in the same single M5 call, so no second mutator call and no new FAULTED row are added. ASK G5 is closed for the install; AC-MSA-44 asserts the carry-through; the hull-plate and seam-repair halves of IDB Open Questions 11 and 12 stay open and are not Assembly's.)* No Emitter call (no `mod_lamp` install exists in MVP; an ASK if one is added). There is no sim seating timer: the seating cue and the install clunk are presentation, Assembly's by the Item DB. A running machine begins its loop only if the Graph runs it. The `BLOCKED` result is a free probe of slot occupancy in one direction (a false-system-state hallucination could show a slot empty), while the opposite direction costs an irreversible install; a short per-seat lockout after `BLOCKED` is proposed by the game-designer but would add state to `assembly.state`, so it is not adopted here (Open Questions). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-44 (auto: chain order), AC-MSA-45 (auto: `BLOCKED` leaves the instance carried and every block unchanged but `assembly.state`). *Pillar:* P5, P4 (state read admitted, MSA-9). *Decides:* advisor R18; IA-24; TD ledger 2.3.

**T5 — Install apply ledger.**

| # | Phase | Call | Owner / class | Proof | Data | If wrong |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| V1 | validate | kernel authority (`OWNED_BY_ISSUER`) | Kernel | CR-5 | | Rejected |
| V2 | validate | IMMEDIATE only: carrying, `compatible_defs(h)` contains the carried def, reach, lit, not staggered | Suit / Item DB / Controller reads | static data | -> OK | Rejected |
| A0 | apply | read the carried instance's `condition_bp`, `cap_bp`, `patched_at_step` and `def_id` before release | Suit read | carry proven in V2 | -> `hp_start`, `hp_cap`, `patched_at_step` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: M5 takes cap and clock)* | |
| A1 | apply | `check_install_module(slot_node_id, def_id)` | Graph, pure | | -> OK / occupancy fail (mapped to `SLOT_OCCUPIED`) / `PORT_INCOMPAT` | on a fail: BLOCKED, return |
| A2 | apply | M5 `install_module(slot_node_id, def_id, hp_start, hp_cap, patched_at_step)` | Graph, Class A | A1 | -> node installed | FAULTED (non-`OK` status, MSA-31) |
| A3 | apply | Suit `release_carried(player_id)` | Suit, Class B | carry proven in V2; same slot | -> instance dissolves | FAULTED (cannot occur) |
| A4 | apply | Controller `clear_carry_ref(actor)` *(published, PC-1 b)* | Controller, status-returning | A3 (the instance is already dissolved) | | FAULTED (non-`OK` status, MSA-31): a stale `carry_ref` to a dissolved and installed instance could be dropped or installed again, an item duplicate *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| A5 | apply | `sync_section(section_id)` | Interaction, Class B | total | | none (an injection point of the test double only, AC-MSA-142) |
| A6 | apply | `last_result = DONE(INSTALL, T)` | own block | total | | none |

**MSA-28 — No `remove_module` in MVP.** *Rule:* V8 salvage (Interaction's `begin_cut` -> M6) is the only way a module leaves `INSTALLED`. The name `remove_module` is reserved and left unregistered. *Why:* an "unbolt cleanly" verb is the relocation freedom the anti-pillar forbids and would open a second faucet beside salvage-once (IDB-17). Wording refreshes owed: the kernel's command-type example list and the Item DB's Assembly row. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-46 (lint: no `remove_module` registration). *Pillar:* anti-pillar, P5. *Decides:* advisor R5. CONFLICT C9.

#### F. Cross-cutting rules

**MSA-29 — Assembly pushes nothing to the web; Interaction pushes all weld and cut noise.** *Rule:* Assembly never calls `push_stimulus`. Because the channel record lives in Interaction's block (MSA-30), Interaction, as host of the channel, pushes **TOOL once per accrued tick** of `begin_weld` and `begin_seam_cut` (the declaration rows carry `noise_class` TOOL, which IA-33 already lists for the welder and cutter) and **one `LOUD` at the DONE of `begin_seam_cut`**, pushed whether or not the completion later applies (the burn-through is loud either way; this needs the one-shot flag on the `begin_seam_cut` row, ASK I1). There is **no `LOUD` at weld completion**: a weld costs 360 TOOL ticks standing still and lit, and that is its P3 price. The earlier idea that Assembly must push its own TOOL ticks "or welding is free to the web" is withdrawn: under this hosting the noise is never free. Install is a TAP with a one-shot QUIET sound and no stimulus. Units (`U_TOOL`, `U_LOUD`) are Attention's. **Agreed with Attention (C5, closed 2026-10-27):** Attention E-44 and AC-AT-72 now assert zero `LOUD` at weld completion; Interaction's IA-33 declares `LOUD` at cut completion, vent apply and throw landing, not at weld completion. Both sides already agree on the part that binds Assembly (Assembly pushes nothing: Attention AT-3 and AC-AT-95 refuse any `push_stimulus` caller other than the Controller and Interaction; Horror Entities pushes no noise and is not a declared caller, HE-36). Attention's must-repeat ("which events push, without duplicating Interaction's `LOUD` at cut completion") is answered by this rule. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-47 (lint: no `push_stimulus` call in Assembly source, mirroring AC-AT-95), AC-MSA-48 (auto, with an Interaction double: weld = D TOOL pushes and zero `LOUD`; cut = D TOOL pushes and exactly one `LOUD` at DONE). *Pillar:* P3. *Decides:* advisor R3; IA-33; AT-3.

**MSA-30 — Durations, abort and partial progress belong to the channel record in Interaction's block.** *Rule:* the weld and seam-cut channel is an IA-14 record in `interaction.state` with `owner_system = ASSEMBLY`. `interaction.step` runs the clock, the shared abort set (IA-15, closed: `MOVED`, `CANCELLED`, `UNLIT`, `OUT_OF_REACH`, `TARGET_GONE`, `BODY`, `NOT_ELIGIBLE`, `RESTORE`), the lit recheck every `LIT_RECHECK_TICKS` 6, the partial-progress ledger (IA-16, `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` 600) and the noise (MSA-29), exactly as for its own verbs; Assembly runs no clock of its own, because a second copy of the IA-14 clock would drift (the "kernel row guessed consumer internals" failure pattern) and the one-channel-per-seat rule (PC-7) is enforceable only inside one block. Durations: `WELD_CHANNEL_TICKS` 360 (6 s, range 240-480) and `SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS` 240 (4 s, range 240-480), both `[PLACEHOLDER]` feel values from the Controller's Open Question 7, both multiples of `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6 (the IA I-2 lint: 60 and 40 blocks), both in the LONG bar class (`BAR_LONG_FROM_TICKS` 240, so the floor is 240, not lower). The ceiling 480 is set by analogy with IA I-9 (which bounds Interaction's own LONG bar at twice `BAR_LONG_FROM_TICKS`), cited as an analogy, not a lock on Assembly. `SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS` and Interaction's `SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS` happen to share 240 at MVP but are distinct names (the Controller's "cut 240" is ambiguous between them). The cut is deliberately faster than the weld: both verbs are terminal on their socket, so neither is a repeatable exploit, and the cut is a prepared amputation paid for by a `LOUD` completion and a possible breach *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: "the P5 escape verb" is deleted. The cut is not an escape verb: a panic cut is RAGGED and breaches the home side, and the Player Fantasy states three prepared reasons to cut)*; revisit if players use it as a free panic button, which would show as RAGGED cuts under attack in the playtest log, not as a reason to make the cut slower. Duration never depends on any state (IA-11c); movement cancels (PC-7: "I can leave now"). `abort_channel(actor)` (IA-35's proposed Assembly mutator) is **refused**: `cancel_channel` already ends an ASSEMBLY-owned record held in `interaction.state`, so Assembly publishes no cancel. Asks to Interaction (all under ASK I1; numbering as in T15): I1(i) a third Class B mutator `open_channel(seat, owner_system, verb_id, target_ref, duration_ticks)` called by the two `begin_*` applies (IA-1 says Interaction publishes "exactly two" Class B mutators, so this is a refresh); I1(ii) `channel_view` exposes, for the tick on which it happens, a DONE handoff `(seat, owner_system, verb_id, target_ref)` for records with `owner_system` other than INTERACTION, and at that DONE Interaction applies nothing itself except to clear `channel_ref` through the Controller mutator (IA-14 row 9) and delete any ledger entry; I1(iii) `begin_weld` and `begin_seam_cut` join IA-16's list of non-incremental channels; I1(viii), answered (IA-14 row 6): for ASSEMBLY-owned records the NOT_ELIGIBLE abort re-tests the owner's `eligible` predicate at `LIT_RECHECK_TICKS`, so a lost `docked` aborts the channel *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-49 (lint: both durations multiples of 6 within 240-480), AC-MSA-50 (auto: abort resumes from the ledger within `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` and not after). *Pillar:* P3, P2. *Decides:* advisor R2, R13; IA-14, IA-15, IA-16, IA-35, PC-7. CONFLICTS C3, C4.

**MSA-31 — Atomicity, the failure model and what a defect costs.** *Rule:* each completion `apply` and the install `apply` is atomic against every other command, hook and snapshot (CR-9), followed by one P5 notification batch per block (CR-10). Dirty keys are marked explicitly in every mutator (Godot 4.7 packed-array element writes call no setter). `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256 is not approached if `sync_section` marks only changed subjects (formula D.12). The failure model is **validate-then-apply, no rollback**: `validate` proves everything through read-only queries, `apply` is infallible by contract, and a failure inside `apply` is a defect that moves the kernel `RUNNING -> FAULTED`: the faulting command is `Faulted(code)` and every other pending command is `Dropped(FAULTED)` (kernel CR-5, CR-19) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, and the kernel never writes or autosaves over an existing save, and in a development build halts with diagnostics. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **how the defect is detected.** The kernel's CR-9 edge case says the kernel "aborts the current `apply` at the assertion" and the earlier text of this rule, E-35, S6 and AC-MSA-51 assumed an `apply` can throw and a batch loop can catch it. GDScript has no throw, try or catch: a runtime error ends the function with a default return and the caller carries on, and `assert()` does not exist in a release export. So detection is **explicit**: (1) every cross-block mutator Assembly calls returns a status, `OK` or a `FAULT_CODE`; (2) the `apply` checks the status after every call and, on the first non-`OK`, calls `ctx.fault(code, detail)` (kernel CR-19(a); the published mutators report on the shared `WriteScope` with `scope.fault` and return their status) and returns `ctx.complete()` without a further mutator call *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; (3) the release build runs the postconditions of MSA-14 and MSA-32 after each weld or cut (statuses, `MSA_SHIFT_DRIFT`, `MSA_VERDICT_DRIFT`, INV-1, INV-2, INV-4, INV-5, INV-7), so a half-applied A3 (Layout written, Body not) is caught by A4's status or, failing that, by INV-4 and INV-5 instead of surviving; (4) the "defect" tested in S6 and AC-MSA-51 is "a mutator returns a `FAULT_CODE` or violates a postcondition", never "throws". The kernel has made this correction (CR-19, CONFLICT C14 closed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R3)* **Callable contract:** every kernel-invoked Assembly callable (`validate` of the five command types, `apply`, `assembly.step`, `snapshot`, `restore`, `post_restore`, `default_state`, `read_view`) receives the kernel's `ctx`, reports through it (`ctx.ok()` or `ctx.reject(reason)` in `validate`, `ctx.put(payload)` in `snapshot`, `ctx.fault(code, detail)` on a defect) and has `return ctx.complete()` as its only return statement; none is declared `-> void`, and a published mutator takes the shared `WriteScope`, reports with `scope.fault` and returns a status that the `apply` propagates. Verified against the engine reference: it has no scripting module, so whether Godot 4.7.2 offers a custom `Logger` that sees script errors is `[UNV]` and is not relied upon.)* The only exits are an explicit user `request_restore` or shutdown. **This is not Save/Load's MVP death rule** (SL-7, restart from the last autosave), which is a separate mechanism with the same loading path; the two must not be conflated. The price of "no rollback", stated plainly: the loss window for an Assembly defect (`fault_loss_ticks`, D.16) is up to 18000 ticks (5 minutes of play, `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` 10800 + `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` 7200) at the ceiling, 10800 at the maximum when no soft gate defers the interval save, and about 5400 on average (half the interval, because a defect lands uniformly inside it) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: "10800 typical" was a maximum, not a typical value; D.16)*, after the player chooses to load. These figures are the bound without an event save, which a defect inside an apply still meets, because the apply's own event save comes after it; weld and cut completions are autosave *events* in the MVP (user ruling D17, MSA-33), which shorten the window for any later defect *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. No pre-weld checkpoint is added: autosave is soft-gated while `CHANNELLING` (SL-5) and a checkpoint would be a second transaction. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-51 (auto, fault injection: a stub mutator that returns a `FAULT_CODE` or violates a postcondition at A3 and A4, and a forced verifier failure, each leave the kernel FAULTED with the faulting command `Faulted(code)`, every other pending command `Dropped(FAULTED)`, the existing save untouched, and `request_restore` returning a state-hash-equal world; *Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: "throws" removed*), AC-MSA-142, AC-MSA-143, AC-MSA-148. *Pillar:* P1. *Decides:* KRN CR-9, CR-10, CR-17, CR-18; advisor R25.

**MSA-32 — A read-only cross-block integrity verifier is the compensating control for "no rollback".** *Rule:* the Graph protects itself (LS-24), Layout and Body each assert their own tables, but nobody owns a check that the three agree, and the three-owner split is exactly what makes each owner's check blind to the others' disagreement. Assembly therefore owns a **read-only verifier** of the **cross-owner** invariants in T7: INV-1 to INV-7 and INV-10, eight in all. Conservation and fresh-seam disconnection (T7's former INV-8 and INV-9) are Graph-internal invariants the Graph already asserts itself (LS-24), so the verifier does not re-own them; they appear as AC cross-checks that cite LS-24 (AC-MSA-25 for the fresh seam, AC-MSA-67 for conservation). It reads only `SIM` views of Layout, Graph, Body and Interaction (never a `PERCEPTION_OUT` block, CR-12), changes nothing, and reports violation ids. Run policy: in `assembly.state`'s `post_restore` (which runs the verifier and clears nothing, the stored `pending` being NONE, MSA-4 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, and runs after `layout.state`, `graph.state`, `interaction.state` and `body.state` because of `restore_after`) all eight, **hard** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: except INV-6's `BODIES_LIVE_MAX` half, which is warn-only, MSA-41, because a surplus live body degrades lighting and corrupts nothing)*: a violation fails the restore: LOADING -> FAULTED naming `assembly.state` and the INV id (kernel edge case on `post_restore` failure; whole-block replace means the pre-restore state is not intact); the save file is not modified and the player may choose another restore; never silently continued; after every Assembly apply in debug and CI builds, all eight, as an assertion; in release builds after each weld or cut apply, INV-1, INV-2, INV-4, INV-5 and INV-7 only (O(seams + bodies + actors): at most 48 seams given `MAX_SECTIONS` 24 and `MAX_SOCKETS_PER_SECTION` 4, at most `MAX_BODIES` 8 bodies and `MAX_ACTORS` 8 actors) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: INV-4 and INV-5 join the release subset, because a half-applied A3, Layout written and Body not, breaks exactly those two and the earlier subset of INV-1, INV-2 and INV-7 could not see it; the cost stays inside the `verifier_release` allocation of D.15 and is re-measured by S1)*, a violation moving the kernel to FAULTED naming the invariant id (through `ctx.fault` of MSA-31, kernel CR-19) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, which is strictly better than playing on in a corrupt state. -> ADR candidate: where cross-block invariants live (Assembly or a kernel integrity service). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-52 (auto: each INV fixture violation reports exactly its id), AC-MSA-53 (auto: a save with an ACTIVE `welds[]` row and no Graph SeamRecord fails restore: the kernel is FAULTED with the block id and the INV id in the diagnostic, no tick runs, and the save file bytes are unchanged). *Pillar:* P1. *Decides:* TD-14.

**T7 — Cross-block invariants checked by the verifier** (registry: `assembly_verifier_invariants`).

| Id | Invariant | Reads | Cost |
|---|---|---|---|
| INV-1 | Each Graph SeamRecord `ACTIVE` <-> exactly one Layout `welds[]` row `ACTIVE` with the same `seam_id`, both sockets `WELDED(seam_id)` | Graph seams, Layout welds and sockets | O(seams) |
| INV-2 | Each `welds[]` row `CUT_SEALED` / `CUT_RAGGED` <-> Graph SeamRecord `CUT` with the same boundary mode; sockets `CAPPED` / `CUT_OPEN` respectively | same | O(seams) |
| INV-3 | Each body's section set equals the connected component over `ACTIVE` welds; the weld relation is a forest (LY-14: `SAME_BODY` is a hard reject, so a weld can never close a ring) | Layout | O(sections) |
| INV-4 | Every Layout body has exactly one Body pose row; a `DISSOLVED` body has none | Layout, Body | O(bodies) |
| INV-5 | Every actor's `body_id` exists in Layout and its `pos_curr` lies in that body's floor area, or `BDY_TRANSFER_NO_SECTION` is counted (a defect-only counter, BDY-23) | Body, Layout | O(actors <= `MAX_ACTORS` 8) |
| INV-6 | `body_count <= MAX_BODIES` **hard** (the Layout cap); `body_count <= BODIES_LIVE_MAX` **warn-only** (`MSA_BODIES_LIVE_WARN`, a diagnostic, never a restore failure or a fault, because a surplus live body only degrades cross-body lighting, MSA-41, LO-24) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | Body | O(1) |
| INV-7 | Id monotonicity: every `seam_id` and `body_id` in any block is below the owner's `next_*`; none reused | Graph, Layout | O(seams + bodies) |
| *(INV-8)* | *Moved out of the verifier: conservation on the apply tick (weld = node count unchanged, edge count up by exactly the plan's edge count; cut = node count unchanged, tombstones equal the former seam sub-edges) is Graph-internal and already asserted by LS-24. Kept as an AC cross-check citing LS-24 (AC-MSA-67).* | Graph counters | n/a |
| *(INV-9)* | *Moved out of the verifier: a fresh seam's sub-edges are all disconnected on the apply tick (opening area 0, valves closed, breakers open) is asserted by the Graph (LS-24). Kept as an AC cross-check (AC-MSA-25).* | Graph | n/a |
| INV-10 | for each seat with `pending.kind != NONE`: the target **exists** and is in a legal state of its verb: a `WELD` target socket is `FREE` (not yet applied) or `WELDED` (a rival completion applied first, E-7, E-33); a `SEAM_CUT` target seam is `ACTIVE` (not yet applied) or `CUT` (a rival completion applied first), and a `pending.submit_T` that is not in the future; `last_result.outcome` (when the runtime field is present) in range *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the `pending` clause runs on live state only; at restore the stored `pending` is NONE, so it is vacuous there)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier text required `FREE` (weld) / `WELDED` (cut) only, which the INV-10 assertion itself broke inside AC-MSA-59(a)/(c) and which made a legitimate save in the `submit_T` to `submit_T + 13` window unloadable under AC-MSA-118)* | Layout, own block | O(seats <= 4) |

**MSA-33 — The autosave event is a derived read.** *Rule:* Assembly publishes the pure read `last_shape_change_T()` (formula D.9): the maximum over Layout's `welds[]` of `welded_at_T` and `cut_at_T`, or NONE. Save/Load polls it at tick boundaries (SL-13); Layout's `S/<seam_id>` key in the P5 batch is the notification. The event names are fixed as `ASSEMBLY_WELD` (the maximum is a `welded_at_T`) and `ASSEMBLY_CUT` (the maximum is a `cut_at_T`), answering Save/Load Open Question 24 for Assembly *(fixed 2026-10-27 — consistency pass)*; enabling them is Save/Load's, and **the MVP ships both events enabled** (user ruling D17: the weld, cut and module-cut event autosaves are confirmed) alongside the interval, QUIT and initial saves *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. The 64-bit `T` values never travel as JSON numbers (SL-2). **Assembly never calls Save/Load.** **No Graph gate covers a RAGGED cut:** SL-5 soft row (a) counts only `ENDOGENOUS` NODE/EDGE/SEAM descriptors, and a RAGGED cut's hull stubs are `EXOGENOUS` and player-caused (LS-16, "no gate"), so, without the withhold below, an autosave **would land during a self-inflicted RAGGED decompression**; the hard DOWNED/DEAD gate (SL-5) still applies. (This corrects advisor R19, which assumed the gate.) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the earlier "This is accepted" sentences and the optional ASK S1(b) are deleted; the withhold below is the rule.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **the earlier acceptance of a save mid-breach is withdrawn for the event save.** MVP death means "restart from the last autosave" (SL-7), so an `ASSEMBLY_CUT` event save that lands mid-breach can reload the player into an unwinnable decompression, which is an unfair failure that the autosave hides. New rule: **a RAGGED cut's `ASSEMBLY_CUT` event is withheld until the keep-side room has vented (band VACUUM) and its keep-side ports are capped or the port ceiling has passed.** Assembly publishes a second pure read, `ragged_breach_open()`, defined in the amendment below and in D.9 (the keep-side cut room still venting through its hull hole, or a keep-side port still uncapped inside the port ceiling; the data come from the Graph's `seam_view` `cut_sides` and `compartment(cid).band_p`, ASK G10 as amended); Save/Load consumes an `ASSEMBLY_CUT` event only while it is false (ASK S1(b), promoted from optional to requested, and extended to the interval save as a soft-gate row). The alternative of keeping a pre-cut slot is rejected: Save/Load's death rule restores the newest verified generation (SL-8), so an older pre-cut generation is not the one the player gets, and a second slot is a second transaction. A save taken inside the breach window by the interval path may still land after `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` 7200, if the window outlasts the cap, the save is written RISKY (`ASSEMBLY_BREACH`). AC-MSA-130 asserts that a restore from any permitted save is winnable.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G2: **the withhold is a function of the breach, not of the event kind.** (a) `ragged_breach_open()` counts only the **keep side** (the sections that keep `body_id` after the cut, `keep_sections`); the leaving orphan never counts and never holds a save (the predicate itself is amended 2026-10-28 below). (b) The withhold is Save/Load F-1's `NOT (ragged_breach_open() AND ASSEMBLY_CUT IN kinds(pending_events))`: keyed on the pending SET, not on the single newest `event_kind`, so a weld latched behind an open-breach cut waits with it (F-1; AC-SL-146 (i)); in the MVP (sockets terminal, LY-13, one weld-then-cut cycle) it gives the same answer for every save as the unconditional form, so D54's "a weld is never withheld" holds for every MVP save. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* (c) A ceiling, `RAGGED_WITHHOLD_MAX_TICKS` 2400 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, on the **port clause only** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*: `ports_open` is false from `cut_at_T` plus the ceiling on, so a port nobody caps does not hold the event save forever; the ceiling does not bound the decompression, which ends on the Graph band (VACUUM), not on a timer. (d) `event_due` carries NO settle conjunct: Save/Load owns the function (F-1, registry `autosave_due`) and its form is `event_due = (pending_events != empty) AND elapsed >= AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS AND NOT (ragged_breach_open() AND ASSEMBLY_CUT IN kinds(pending_events))`, `elapsed = T - last_save_T`; Assembly withdraws the third S1 ask (an event-capture settle) and its knob (D.9) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G1/G3)*. (e) The remaining decompression time of a permitted save is bounded by construction: no autosave of either kind is written inside the window, so none lands while the room is partly vented; a Save & Quit inside it is Save/Load's own RISKY-tagged write (cause `ASSEMBLY_BREACH`).)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions) (X7 / D54): adopted as written. The `ASSEMBLY_CUT` event save of a RAGGED cut is withheld until `ragged_breach_open()` is false, and the open RAGGED window is a soft-gate row for the interval save (bounded by `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` 7200, so the D.16 ceiling is unchanged); the `ASSEMBLY_WELD` event and a SEALED cut's `ASSEMBLY_CUT` event are never withheld by it. Any bound seat at `vitals_state` CRITICAL or worse is a further soft-gate input of Save/Load's own (X7 (a), Save/Load's rule, not restated here). Formula: D.9.)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* **The predicate follows the keep-side room's decompression, not the ports alone (creative-director spot-check of D54; Graph LS-16 gives a RAGGED cut two independent hazards, and the Graph, as owner of the physics, wins, G1).** Definition, used everywhere: `ragged_breach_open() = EXISTS seam s in welds[] with state CUT and boundary RAGGED such that venting(s) OR ports_open(s)`; `venting(s) = EXISTS side IN seam_view(s).cut_sides, side.section_id IN keep_sections(s), EXISTS h IN side.cut_holes: h.a_hole > 0 AND compartment(h.cid).band_p != VACUUM` (the hull hole is still open and the keep-side cut room has not yet fallen below the VACUUM band, P < 100 dkPa, LS-7); `ports_open(s) = (SUM over keep sides of side.uncapped_open_ends) > 0 AND T - cut_at_T < RAGGED_WITHHOLD_MAX_TICKS`. (a) The two clauses are the two LS-16 hazards and are independent: `cap_port` (M12, `begin_cap`) closes the fluid-port hiss only and never changes `venting`; the `A_CUT` hull hole (hp 2500) has no subject in the MVP, no verb repairs it, and no verb is added for this (creative-director ruling); the keep-side cut room is lost to vacuum until Vertical Slice content (a plate install on the hardpoint, LS-23 / E 'hull segment reaches hp 0') closes the hole. (b) `venting` has no ceiling: it ends by construction when `band_p` reaches VACUUM (P falls geometrically to a residual of at most `ceil(1/k)` mGU, below 100 dkPa), or earlier when the hole is closed (`a_hole` 0, Vertical Slice) or the section is discarded or jettisoned (a tombstoned compartment reads as not venting). Only the port clause carries `RAGGED_WITHHOLD_MAX_TICKS`. (c) A closed hole, a SEALED cut, a cut whose keep-side room is already VACUUM, or a seam with no `cut_sides`: the read is false. (d) Assembly's new Graph reads are `seam_view(seam_id).cut_sides` and `compartment(cid).band_p` (both pure, Graph LS-19; ASK G10). Numbers (Graph D.1 at registry defaults, `G_OPEN` 2, `A_CUT` 24, `GAS_SCALE` 1000, `P0` 1000 dkPa nominal, `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6, one hole per former opening so `n_holes` 1 for the MVP seam): `k = G_OPEN x A_CUT x n_holes / (V x GAS_SCALE)`; for a keep-side cut room of V = 20 (its other doors closed; the room volume is `[UNV]` until Layout publishes it) `k` = 0.0024 per step, the DECOMP band (P < 400) is reached in `ceil(ln(1000/400)/0.0024)` = 382 steps = 2292 ticks and VACUUM (P < 100) in `ceil(ln(1000/100)/0.0024)` = 960 steps = 5760 ticks (96 s); the simulated value may differ by +-2 steps (floor in D.1). V = 30: `k` = 0.0016, 1440 steps = 8640 ticks. A keep-side room open to a larger connected volume vents more slowly (`k` uses the connected volume), bounded in the worst designed case by `ceil(ln(10) x V_total x 1000 / (2 x 24 x 1000))` steps, e.g. V_total 120: 5757 steps = 34 542 ticks; see E-31 for the listed exposure. The window between the handoff (`pending` set in P3 of `T`) and the apply (P2 of `T+1`, up to `T+8` under queue truncation, or `submit_T + 13` when the completion is rejected or dropped, E-33) is not covered by the channelling gate; a snapshot there stores `pending` as NONE and holds no queued completion, so a restore loses the weld or cut and the player redoes the six seconds (the non-snapshot ticks, MSA-4) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. Closing it is an optional ASK to Save/Load (ASK S1): defer the autosave while `pending_any()` is true (one name: `pending_any()`). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-54 (auto: `last_shape_change_T()` after a weld and a cut; two 32-bit parts or a string at the serialisation boundary). *Pillar:* P5. *Decides:* advisor R19; KRN H3 (one source of truth for history is Layout's `welds[]`).

**MSA-34 — Outcome presentation grammar: one cue, no reason text, the world is the explanation.** *Rule:* REJECTED at begin uses the shared prompt grammar (no prompt, or the standard rejection cue). Every **post-channel non-DONE outcome** (a handoff `REJECTED`, `BLOCKED(PORT_ABSENT)`, `BLOCKED(SLOT_OCCUPIED)`, `NOT_TAKEN`) shares **one** cue per verb family and carries no reason text (registry: `seam_cue_vocabulary`): weld, *the bead sputters and does not hold*; seam cut, *the flame gutters and the seam holds*; install, *the module bumps against the socket and stays in your hands*. (The cut never takes the weld's sputter: the cut's bead is already laid, and the cut is the verb that fails to *separate* it.) The reason code is in the result event for DIAGNOSTIC and replay only. A distinct cue per reason would be the reason text P4 forbids; the cost is that the player cannot tell `PORT_ABSENT` from `NOT_TAKEN` by the cue alone, which is acceptable because both are rare in MVP and the world answers within one walk. Every non-DONE outcome has a world witness reachable within about 60 s: `PORT_ABSENT` leaves the lane node's hardpoint footprint visible as an empty bay (Layout keeps a MACHINE footprint after salvage, LY-20); `SLOT_OCCUPIED` shows the occupying module at the bay; `NOT_TAKEN` means a world fact changed during the channel. A presenter plays an outcome cue only if `last_result` is not empty, `last_result.T` differs from the last value it saw and lies within `OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS` 60 `[PLACEHOLDER]` of the current `T`; a restore never replays a cue (`last_result` is runtime-only and so empty after a restore) and a MIRROR join never replays a stale one. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review, three corrections to the dedupe.* (1) `last_result` is runtime-only (MSA-4), so a restore leaves it empty by construction rather than by a clearing step. *(2)* **`last_seen` is initialised on join and on restore** to the value of `last_result.T` the presenter finds at that moment (or to "none" when empty), so a client that joins 30 ticks after a result sees `last_result.T` equal to `last_seen`, not different from it, and plays nothing; the earlier text claimed a join "never plays a stale cue" while `last_seen_T` was unset on a MIRROR join, which would have played it. *(3)* **The dedupe is keyed by `(PlayerId, T)`, not by one scalar `T`,** because results are per seat, and **a presenter plays cues for the local seat only**; a crewmate's result is seen through their channel and the world, never through a cue. D.17 states the formula; AC-MSA-139 tests a join 30 ticks after a result.)* *Criterion:* AC-MSA-55 (review and manual: the cue is identical across the three non-DONE outcomes, none reads as "it worked", the witness is reachable in about 60 s), AC-MSA-56 (auto: `last_result` is consumed once). *Pillar:* P4, P1. *Decides:* advisor R12, R24; TD-13; game-designer G3, G4.

**MSA-35 — The perception boundary.** *Rule:* Assembly reads no `PERCEPTION_OUT` block (CR-12) and writes no perception state; every Assembly read has no perceived variant; Assembly's presentation never consumes `surge[]` magnitudes, `q`, `leak_bp` or `hp`. A phantom seam or a false "welded" state may exist only in the Perception Filter's corruptible presentation layer; it never reaches `welds[]`, the consequence channel or the bead geometry. Seam geometry, bead, cap plate and socket state are truth channel (an ASK to the Filter repeating LY-34), the seam's own lane valve and breaker sprites are truth channel under the MSA-23 fallback until the zero-gradient witness is confirmed (the same ASK to the Filter, F1), and `CUT_BLOCKED` is a kept truth oracle (MSA-21). No flesh or violet appears anywhere in Assembly's presentation. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-57 (lint: Assembly's source references no `PERCEPTION_OUT` block id). *Pillar:* P4. *Decides:* advisor R24, R29; LY-34.

**MSA-36 — Scope guards.** *Rule:* no hull-plate replacement (Interaction's FULL repair once the Graph rules, IDB Open Question 11), no seam repair (M7 on a SEAM is IDB Open Question 12; a seam is restored by a re-weld, never for free), no re-weld of a used socket, no discard, no unload, no rotation, mirror, free placement or resizing, no `remove_module`, no `q` upgrades or tool tiers, no cut as a weapon, no player-placed lamps.
