The only plate Assembly ever causes is the cap plate, which is Layout's presentation of `CAPPED`. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-58 (review). *Pillar:* anti-pillars. *Decides:* advisor R22.

**MSA-37 — Co-op seats `[VS-B]`.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the multi-seat rules below need a second seat and are out of the MVP gate; the per-seat data shape (a map keyed by PlayerId, ascending-PlayerId iteration, AC-MSA-07b) is still built in the MVP with one seat.)* *Rule:* up to four seats; each has its own channel record, `pending` and `last_result`. **The same socket gives `BUSY` at begin:** IA-34 makes contention one job per `(subject_ref, verb class)`, and WELD is a verb class, so a second seat's `begin_weld` on a socket already being welded is an immediate `BUSY`; two seats channelling the *same* socket cannot happen. Channels do not stack or shorten. **A double DONE is reachable only with two seats on the two different sockets of one pair** (MSA-11 lets either socket of the pair be focused, and IA-34's key is the focused subject, not the derived pair). Each pays its full `D` and both emit TOOL noise. If both reach DONE on the same tick, `assembly.step` submits two SYSTEM completions in ascending PlayerId order, the first applies, and the second's `validate` fails `SOCKET_NOT_FREE` (the pair's sockets are already `WELDED`), producing `NOT_TAKEN` for that seat (not an error, not a fault). If they reach DONE on consecutive ticks, the second seat's handoff pre-check fails first with `SOCKET_NOT_FREE` and `REJECTED` is written, or, if the first completion has been submitted but not yet applied, the second completion's `validate` fails and it resolves as `NOT_TAKEN`. **Two seats on the same seam cut:** the second `begin_seam_cut` is `BUSY` (IA-34, class WELD, ASK I1(iv)); on the two sockets of one pair the second completion fails `SEAM_NOT_ACTIVE` and resolves `NOT_TAKEN`. Optional ASK I1(x): key WELD contention on the *host socket of the derived pair*, which would turn the second-socket case into an immediate `BUSY` too and make the double-DONE case unreachable. `assembly.state` is replicated as `SHARED_SUMMARY`; a crewmate's weld in progress is visible through Interaction's replicated channel record (the human channel, P1). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-59 (auto: two seats, same tick and consecutive ticks, exactly one `DONE` and a single welded state). *Pillar:* P1. *Decides:* TD-12; IA-34.

**MSA-38 — Restore and pause.** *Rule:* a restore drops every channel (abort cause `RESTORE`, IA-36); `assembly.state` has nothing to clear, because `snapshot()` stores `pending` as NONE and `last_result` is runtime-only and so is empty after any restore (MSA-4) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; nothing is half-applied because the apply is atomic and a snapshot is taken only at a tick boundary. Completions are never replayed from a save. While the simulation is paused no tick advances (no P3, so no hook submits), the TTL and the ledger are in ticks and stop, and resume continues as if there had been no pause. For `persist` blocks, restore-then-continue equals uninterrupted play (kernel G4) at every tick where no ASSEMBLY channel record is open and no `pending` is set; the differences are asserted explicitly rather than hidden: while a channel is open the restore drops the channel record and its ledger entry (abort cause `RESTORE`, E-29), and while `pending` is set (from `submit_T` to the apply, or to the TTL, up to 13 ticks after a rejection, E-30, E-33) the completion is in the kernel queue, which no save holds, so the restore loses it (the **non-snapshot ticks**, MSA-4) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **the permitted difference is exactly one key, and the test asserts that only it differs:** PD-1, the seat's ASSEMBLY channel record and its ledger entry in `interaction.state` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: PD-2, `seats[p].pending`, is deleted: `snapshot()` stores `pending` as NONE, MSA-4, so it never differs between a stored and a restored state)*. `last_result` is not a permitted difference because it is runtime-only and outside the hash (MSA-4). A completion that was queued but not applied is not in any block, so a restore drops it with the queue; the world then differs from the uninterrupted run after the dropped weld or cut, so the equivalence claim is made per snapshot tick: for every snapshot tick `s` at which PD-1 does not exist and no seat's live `pending` is set (a non-snapshot tick otherwise, MSA-4), restore-then-continue is hash-equal to the uninterrupted run at every tick `>= s`; for a snapshot tick `s` inside PD-1 or a non-snapshot tick, the claim is only that the restored state at `s` equals the uninterrupted state at `s` with exactly PD-1 removed (the stored `pending` is NONE in both), and the script then re-drives the verb from its `begin_*`. AC-MSA-61 and S7 assert it.)* *(orchestrator ruling, QA-lead flag F-1: the earlier wording "except the handoff-to-apply tick" was contradicted by E-29.)* *Criterion:* AC-MSA-60 (auto: restore mid-channel leaves no channel, a stored `pending` of NONE, no `last_result`, no orphan, no cue), AC-MSA-61 (auto: snapshot at every tick of a weld-and-cut script, restore, continue; hash-equal except the asserted window). *Pillar:* P1, P5. *Decides:* IA-36; SL-5; KRN G4.

**MSA-39 — No presentation work inside the sim apply; budgets are spike-measured.** *Rule:* a weld or cut apply marks dirty keys and returns. The PROJECTOR (Layout -> scene), the Emitter rebuild (EM-30), the Lighting partition and Failure & Trail rebuilds run as P5 subscribers after the commit with writes disabled; if several ticks run in one render frame (catch-up, `MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME` 4) they coalesce to the latest committed state and never replay each tick's change. Budget placeholders, all named in T9, measured in the spike and none a measured fact today: whole weld apply <= 0.5 ms Release (`MSA_WELD_APPLY_BUDGET_MS`; the Graph's M2 alone <= 0.2 ms is locked and sits inside it), whole cut apply <= 0.5 ms (`MSA_CUT_APPLY_BUDGET_MS`), install apply <= 0.2 ms (`MSA_INSTALL_APPLY_BUDGET_MS`), `assembly.step` idle <= 0.02 ms (`MSA_STEP_IDLE_BUDGET_MS`; it runs 60 times a second), P5 subscribers on the weld frame <= 8 ms (`MSA_WELD_FRAME_P5_BUDGET_MS`) with the PROJECTOR <= 4 ms (`PROJECTOR_WELD_BUDGET_MS`, `[UNV]`) and no frame above 33 ms (`WELD_FRAME_HITCH_MAX_MS`), and `weld_offer` per call <= `MSA_WELD_OFFER_BUDGET_US` (value set in the spike), since the Pipeline's camera cut hides a one-to-two-frame hitch. These are co-measured with Body's own weld-projection spike (its S3). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **the first weld frame has two costs the budgets above do not cover.** (1) *First-use compile:* the first weld or cut frame is the first time the weld and cut sprites, the spark and bead materials and any shader variant they pull in are drawn, so a cold shader or pipeline compile can land on that frame. Rule: **the weld, cut and outcome-cue assets (bead pieces, hatch states, sputter, gutter, bump, spark and cursor materials) are preloaded at scene load, and a warm-up draw of each material runs behind the loading screen**, so that no pipeline compile happens on the weld frame. (2) *Event autosave:* the `ASSEMBLY_WELD` and `ASSEMBLY_CUT` events (MSA-33) ask Save/Load for a synchronous in-memory snapshot (kernel CR-18) that can land on the same frame as the P5 rebuild. Rule: Save/Load owns `event_due` and adopts no settle conjunct, so the event capture may share the weld frame; the capture is therefore INSIDE the measured hitch, and AC-MSA-63 and S3 measure the **whole frame** including a capture that fires on it (kept as written). The settle request to Save/Load is withdrawn *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G1/G3)*. S3 is run on a cold cache, in an export build, not only in the editor, because editor numbers hide both costs.)* `assembly.step` and the completion payloads are pure functions of block state, so replay and MIRROR identity hold. *Criterion:* AC-MSA-62 (lint and auto: determinism of `assembly.step`; no wall-clock or RNG), AC-MSA-63 (spike: P5 subscriber cost and frame hitch). *Pillar:* P1. *Decides:* KRN CR-10, CR-17, H9, H10; TD-15.

**MSA-40 — Every invariant is stated for every reachable state, not just the default.** *Rule:* conservation (a weld adds edges only; a cut tombstones them; no node is minted), `abs(actor)` of every actor, offsets on the 24-px grid, and the body count hold at **every** reachable state: zoomed in and out on the integer ladder (`k` in `[1, k_auto + 2]`; nothing in these rules depends on zoom, and the in-world cursor scales with the world viewport while the prompt and bar keep their HUD pixel size), with a carried module, with zero to four seats, with a CASCADE stub submitter, after a restore, and in MIRROR. Tests assert `abs` and section-local position, never raw `pos_curr` (a weld followed by a cut is not an identity on `pos_curr`, BDY-23). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-64 (auto: the invariant matrix over those states). *Pillar:* P1. *Decides:* advisor R28.

**MSA-41 — The body count is bounded by construction; Assembly adds no capacity rule.** *Rule:* the number of live bodies satisfies `N(t) = N0 - W(t) + C(t)` with `C(t) <= W(t)` (every cut needs a prior ACTIVE weld), hence `N(t) <= N0` for every reachable sequence (formula D.8). MVP worst case: home + A + B + pod = 4 bodies before the weld, 3 after it, 4 after the one cut; the ceiling is one cut because both MVP sockets go terminal (LY-13). The lint `bodies_after_worst_sequence <= min(MAX_BODIES, BODIES_LIVE_MAX)` is 4 against `MAX_BODIES` 8 and the 2D Lighting placeholder `BODIES_LIVE_MAX` 4: **the equality is a risk, not a margin**; adding any body outside Assembly (a second pod, a loose piece Web Map keeps alive) or any Vertical Slice socket-economy change **degrades cross-body lighting: surplus bodies' lights are drawn as `OTHER_BODY` stand-ins (2D Lighting cross-body isolation rule)**. It is a degradation, not a fault: the sim is unaffected, but a body beyond the cap would lose real light cast onto its own geometry. ASK LT1 (T15) asks the 2D Lighting owner to confirm `BODIES_LIVE_MAX` 4 against this zero-margin worst case or to raise it to 5. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **LT1 is closed, citing LO-24.** 2D Lighting's LO-24 and its Tuning Knobs row define a body as *live* when it is built and has a non-dormant section or hosts the avatar, state the MVP worst case as "exactly 4, zero margin accepted", and degrade any surplus body to `OTHER_BODY` stand-ins. The two documents' worst cases are reconciled in one line each: **LO-24:** live bodies at most 4, zero margin accepted; **WM-24:** at most one unwelded derelict is loaded and the pod's section is always dormant, so the bodies that are live at any moment are fewer than the bodies that are built (home, the berthed derelict, and the pod only while it hosts the avatar), which makes Assembly's count of built bodies (D.8: 4, then 3, then 4) the conservative bound. The assertion is **warn-only**: INV-6 (T7) raises the diagnostic `MSA_BODIES_LIVE_WARN` when `body_count` exceeds `BODIES_LIVE_MAX` and fails nothing; it is hard only at `MAX_BODIES` 8.)* `CAPACITY` on `can_detach` and a `MAX_SECTIONS_PER_BODY` value are deferred to Vertical Slice (Layout Open Question 8). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-65 (lint and auto: the worst MVP sequence ends at 4 bodies; the equality is reported as zero margin). *Pillar:* P5. *Decides:* advisor R7.

**MSA-42 — Do not fake a choice in the weld; the real decisions are whether, when and how clean.** *Rule:* the MVP weld is deliberately choiceless (MSA-13; Player Fantasy). The design commits to: no quality slider, no weld pattern, no rotation; the real decisions are **whether** to weld versus strip (neither line dominates; the playtest gate checks that both are taken), **when** relative to Attention, and **how clean** a later cut will be. Degenerate-play ledger, resolved: cancel-to-peek costs tool noise per accrued tick and the ledger is time-bounded (Interaction's); weld, strip by V8 salvage, cut away is an intended healthy line (each V8 costs noise and leaves open ends to cap, the cut is `LOUD`); weld-cut-weld to reset or farm is unreachable (terminal sockets, nothing minted); a RAGGED cut breaches both sides including the home side, so it is self-harm, never a weapon ("a cut never removes a threat the player is not leaving behind"); shedding an infested section after the seam has been prepared is healthy P5 play (*Amended 2026-10-27 — design review:* it is a prepared amputation, not an escape; a cut made under attack is RAGGED and breaches home, so it is the worst way out), with the rule that **a cut must not launder Attention** (the locus falls back, the level does not reset: a verify handed to the Attention owner, ASK A1); battery-banking through weld and cut is not a faucet (conservation). **First-weld scaffold (authoring sequencing, no change to `weld_offer`):** before derelict B becomes dockable, the player has already exercised V4 and V3 on `home_ship` (clearing the home-ship faults), so the first connect is not a difficulty spike on a leaking seam; `weld_offer` never reads player history (ASK D1 answered by DA OQ-DA-31: no gate needed). *Criterion:* AC-MSA-66 (review: no choice-faking element exists), AC-MSA-131 (auto: no strip line makes `weld_join` permanently impossible; *Amended 2026-10-27 — design review*), AC-MSA-69 (manual: both the weld line and the strip line are taken by at least 2 of 5 playtesters), AC-MSA-70 (manual: median time from prompt-first-appears to channel-begin <= 45 s, and from bead to first seam-breaker close 60-180 s; both are `[PLACEHOLDER]` **playtest targets for the playtest gate, not sim knobs**, and live in the playtest script rather than in any data file). *Pillar:* P5, anti-pillar, SDT honesty. *Decides:* game-designer G1, G2, G13; advisor R11.

#### G. Tables of record

**T6 — `assembly.state` schema v1.**

| Field | Type | Range / enum | Persisted | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `schema_version` | int | 1 | yes | CR-8; an unsupported version on restore is a reported failure, never a silent default |
| `seats` | map PlayerId -> record | 0-4 entries | yes | keyed by PlayerId, never peer id; no packed arrays (MSA-4) |
| `seats[p].pending.kind` | enum | `NONE`, `WELD`, `SEAM_CUT` | live only; `snapshot()` writes `NONE` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | a gate and the TTL anchor, never stored (MSA-4) |
| `seats[p].pending.target_ref` | primitive pair | socket key or `seam_id` | live only; stored as the empty ref | stable ids, no object references (H7) |
| `seats[p].pending.submit_T` | int64 | >= 0 | live only; stored as 0 | string in JSON (SL-2) in the summary replica; the TTL anchor (MSA-6) |
| `seats[p].last_result.verb_id` | enum | `WELD`, `SEAM_CUT`, `INSTALL` | **no, runtime-only** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | outside `snapshot()` and every hash scope; replicated in `SHARED_SUMMARY` |
| `seats[p].last_result.target_ref` | primitive pair | socket key, `seam_id` or hardpoint ref | no, runtime-only | stable ids, no object references (H7); presenter anchor for the outcome cue |
| `seats[p].last_result.outcome` | enum | `DONE`, `REJECTED`, `BLOCKED`, `NOT_TAKEN` | no, runtime-only | |
| `seats[p].last_result.reason` | int | 0-99 | no, runtime-only | `MSA.<CODE>` as a number; diagnostic only |
| `seats[p].last_result.T` | int64 | >= 0 | no, runtime-only | string in JSON (summary replica); the presenter dedupe key, with the PlayerId (MSA-34, D.17) |
| `cascade_deferred` | list | at most 1 entry `{seam_id, first_deferred_T}` | yes | `[VS]`-reserved (MSA-22); empty in MVP |

`default_state()` is empty seats. No derived caches in `snapshot()` (CR-8). v1 is the first version, so no migration is required in MVP. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the `last_result` rows are runtime-only (MSA-4): they are in the live block and in the `SHARED_SUMMARY` replica but not in `snapshot()`, so they are not in the canonical bytes of any hash scope. `schema_version` stays 1 because no build has shipped this block; a later change to what is persisted bumps it. Kernel ASK K3 asks the kernel to confirm a block may exclude a runtime-only field from `snapshot()`.)*

**T8 — Criterion index (pre-assigned ids; the Acceptance Criteria section expands each to Given/When/Then and keeps the id).** Tags: (auto), (lint), (review), (manual), (spike). *The Acceptance Criteria section supersedes the tags and gists in this index: AC-MSA-46 and AC-MSA-66 are absorbed into AC-MSA-01 and AC-MSA-58; AC-MSA-07, 17, 29 and 55 are split into lettered ids; 02, 09, 10, 18, 20, 37, 49, 58, 67 and 72 were re-tagged by the QA review (review or lint to auto or lint; spike to auto nightly); AC-MSA-73 to AC-MSA-128 are added there (ids 113-115 and 119 are the Game Feel benchmarks). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: AC-MSA-05 splits into 05a and 05b; AC-MSA-129 to AC-MSA-148 are added by the first design review, and the criteria section keeps the authoritative tags, in particular `[VS-B]`, which excludes a criterion from the MVP gate.)**

| Id | Tag | Gist | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| AC-MSA-01 | auto | exactly five command types registered; `remove_module` and `begin_cut` absent | MSA-1 |
| AC-MSA-02 / 03 | review / lint | no restated foreign arithmetic or value; only `assembly.state` written, all else via published mutators | MSA-2 |
| AC-MSA-04 | auto | begin, accrue, handoff, completion at default durations; completion applies at `Tb + D + 1` | MSA-3 |
| AC-MSA-05a / 05b / 06 | auto / auto / lint | schema and `pending` stored as NONE (restore-idempotent); pre-check refusal versus block-layer failure; no packed-array field *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | MSA-4 |
| AC-MSA-07 | auto | `assembly.step` deterministic, ascending PlayerId, hook order registered | MSA-5 |
| AC-MSA-08 / 09 | auto / lint | `DONE` xor `NOT_TAKEN` under backlog 1-8; payload fields, `max_age_ticks == PENDING_TTL_TICKS`, range | MSA-6 |
| AC-MSA-10 | lint | one predicate, three sites, identical modulo T2a | MSA-7 |

| AC-MSA-18 / 19 / 20 | lint / lint / review | `q` clamp and `MSA_Q_CLAMPED`; `q` reaches no player-visible path; no modifier term | MSA-13 |
| AC-MSA-21 / 22 / 23 / 24 | auto / auto / spike / auto | weld call order; exact P5 batch keys; apply budget; proof enumeration | MSA-14 |
| AC-MSA-25 / 26 / 27 | auto / auto / lint | fresh seam fully disconnected; vacuum, frozen and dead weld moves nothing; `surge[].warn` only | MSA-15 |

| AC-MSA-31 | auto | no cut prompt on the derelict side; prompt on the home side | MSA-18 |
| AC-MSA-32 / 33 | auto / lint | SEALED/RAGGED matrix; derivation reads positions only | MSA-19 |
| AC-MSA-34 / 35 | auto | cut call order; `heading_q` 0 and `abs` conserved | MSA-20 |
| AC-MSA-36 / 37 | auto / spike | `CUT_BLOCKED` at three sites; legal stance outside the hatch rectangles | MSA-21 |
| AC-MSA-38 | auto | CASCADE defers, applies when clear, abandons on expiry (stub submitter) | MSA-22 |
| AC-MSA-39 | manual | cut mode predictable from the world | MSA-23 |
| AC-MSA-40 | auto | detached body persists; no M4/LM4 call | MSA-24 |

| AC-MSA-55 / 56 | review+manual / auto | one non-DONE cue and a reachable witness; `last_result` consumed once | MSA-34 |

### States and Transitions

Three state machines are in play, none of them owned in full by Assembly: the per-seat **channel handoff** (Interaction owns the channel half, Assembly the outcome half), the **install** TAP, and the **socket and seam lifecycle** that Assembly drives but Layout and the Graph own. "Who may cause" names the only system or actor allowed to make the transition.

**S1 — Channel handoff for weld and seam cut, per seat.**

| State | Where it lives | Entry | Exit | Transitions out | Who may cause | Visible to the player |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| IDLE | `interaction.state` has no record for the seat; `assembly.state.pending.kind` = `NONE` | start; any terminal | `begin_*` applied | -> CHANNELLING | the seat (PLAYER `begin_weld` / `begin_seam_cut`, offer permitting). A `begin_*` pressed while `pending.kind != NONE` is rejected `BUSY` at site 1 and the seat stays where it is (MSA-6, MSA-7) | the prompt only if the offer holds |
| CHANNELLING | `interaction.state` record `{owner_system ASSEMBLY, begin_tick Tb, duration D, steps_done ...}` | `begin_*.apply` in P2 of `Tb` | DONE (accrued `D - resume_offset` ticks) or ABORTED | -> HANDOFF; -> ABORTED | `interaction.step` (clock, abort set, noise); the seat (cancel, movement) | linear LONG bar with needle-mark, sparks at the hand point, work-loop sound, the chest beam stays on |
| ABORTED | Interaction (an IA-15 cause plus a ledger entry, TTL `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` 600) | any abort cause | IDLE, or re-entry to CHANNELLING from the ledger within the TTL | -> IDLE; -> CHANNELLING | Interaction (world or body ended it); the seat (let go) | bar drains (cancel) or snaps empty with an "interrupted" cue (abort); the socket and the hull are unchanged (no bead exists before the weld applies; Interaction's abort frames only) |
| HANDOFF | one tick: the DONE handoff in `channel_view` | accrual tick `e = D - resume_offset` (P3 of tick `T`) | `assembly.step` in the same P3 | -> REJECTED; -> PENDING | `interaction.step` creates it; `assembly.step` consumes it | nothing (same tick); for a cut the `LOUD` is pushed now |
| REJECTED | `last_result = REJECTED(reason)`, nothing submitted | the handoff pre-check fails (MSA-7 site 2) | next `begin_*` | -> IDLE | `assembly.step` | the channel has ended, with the verb family's one post-channel cue (weld: the bead sputters and does not hold; cut: the flame gutters and the seam holds); the cause is world-visible (a crewmate in the hatch, a socket already welded, a lost dock). REJECTED at *begin* is a different thing and keeps the prompt grammar |
| PENDING | `assembly.state.pending {kind, target_ref, submit_T}`; the SYSTEM completion is in the kernel queue | `assembly.step` submits the completion | the completion's `apply` clears it; or the TTL fires | -> APPLIED; -> BLOCKED; -> NOT_TAKEN | `assembly.step` (enter); the kernel and the completion `apply` (exit) | nothing (normally one tick, 16.7 ms; up to 13 ticks after a rejected or dropped completion, E-33) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| APPLIED | Layout `welds[]` row, Graph SeamRecord, Body pose rows changed | completion `validate` OK and no DEFERRED failure | `last_result = DONE`, `pending` cleared | -> IDLE | the completion `apply` only | the world changed: bead or cap plate, hatch leaf, weep, possible camera cut |
| BLOCKED | `last_result = BLOCKED(reason)`; nothing mutated | `validate` OK but the full `can_join` returns `PORT_ABSENT` in `apply` A0 | `pending` cleared | -> IDLE | the completion `apply` only | the weld family's cue, "the bead sputters and does not hold" (`PORT_ABSENT` is reachable only by a weld) |
| NOT_TAKEN | `last_result = NOT_TAKEN` | the completion was Rejected, Dropped or `STALE` and `T - submit_T > PENDING_TTL_TICKS` | `pending` cleared | -> IDLE | `assembly.step` (TTL rule) only | the verb family's cue (weld sputter; cut "flame gutters and the seam holds") |

Transition rules. IDLE -> CHANNELLING -> HANDOFF -> {REJECTED | PENDING} -> {APPLIED | BLOCKED | NOT_TAKEN} -> IDLE; CHANNELLING -> ABORTED -> {IDLE | CHANNELLING}. PENDING resolves to exactly one of its three exits (MSA-6). A restore from any state returns the seat to IDLE with the channel dropped (`RESTORE`) and the seat records cleared. FAULTED is a kernel state, not an Assembly state: a defect inside any `apply` leaves the whole kernel in FAULTED and no Assembly state is meaningful until a player-initiated restore (MSA-31).

**S2 — Install, a TAP, one tick.**

| State | Entry | Exit | Transitions out | Who may cause | Visible |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CARRYING (Suit and Controller own it) | pickup | install or drop | -> APPLIED; -> BLOCKED; -> REJECTED; drop | the seat | carry gait; the install prompt appears on a compatible HARDPOINT |
| APPLIED | `install_module` validate OK and `check_install_module` OK | `last_result = DONE` | -> CARRYING ends (the instance dissolves into an INSTALLED node, Item DB row 8) | the install `apply` only | the module seated, the carried instance gone, the clunk |
| BLOCKED | `check_install_module` fails | `last_result = BLOCKED(SLOT_OCCUPIED)` | -> CARRYING (the instance is still carried) | the install `apply` only | the module bumps and stays in the hands |
| REJECTED | `validate` fails | none | -> CARRYING | the kernel / `validate` | no prompt, or the standard rejection cue |

**S3 — Socket and seam lifecycle as Assembly drives it** (cited; the owners are in brackets).

| Socket [Layout LY-13] | Seam [Graph LS-15/LS-16] | Trigger | Mutators | Who may cause |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `FREE` | none | the `weld` completion `apply` | M2, LM2, BM3 | Assembly only |
| `WELDED(seam_id)` | `ACTIVE`, `leak_bp` 10000, wearing in | time (Graph wear-in) | none | the Graph |
| `WELDED(seam_id)` | `ACTIVE`, `leak_bp` 0 | wear-in complete | none | the Graph |
| `CAPPED` | `CUT`, boundary SEALED | the `seam_cut` completion, `cut_mode` SEALED | M3, LM3, BM4 | Assembly only |
| `CUT_OPEN` | `CUT`, boundary RAGGED | the `seam_cut` completion, `cut_mode` RAGGED | M3, LM3, BM4 | Assembly only |
| `CAPPED` / `CUT_OPEN` | `CUT` (tombstone kept with the boundary mode) | nothing; terminal in MVP | none | none |

Illegal transitions: `FREE -> CAPPED`, `WELDED -> FREE`, `CAPPED -> WELDED` (re-weld), any state to `FREE`. A second weld on a used socket returns `SOCKET_NOT_FREE`. The body-membership consequence follows Layout: a weld merges two bodies (the absorbed body becomes `DISSOLVED`); a cut splits one body into exactly two components because the weld relation is a forest (LY-14: `SAME_BODY` is a hard reject).

| Neighbour row (quoted) | This GDD |
|---|---|

| **Graph Interactions row:** "weld plan, `q`, `L_seam`; `weld`/`cut` commands -> M2/M3; M13 as the hatch completes" / takes "`can_join` with `surge[]` and `WELD_DP_WARN`; seam ids" / "Assembly owns the commands and `q`; Graph owns the seam edges after the weld". "Seam ids" is the open return of M2 *(ASK G1)*. The LS-18 caller column also drifts and is refreshed under C8: M3 is called by Assembly's `seam_cut` (not `cut`), M5 by Assembly's `install_module` (not `install`), and M4 `discard_section` by Web Map only (MSA-24). |

| **Layout row:** takes `socket_plan`, `can_attach`, LM2, LM3, LM4 and their return values / supplies "weld quality `q`, the weld and cut commands, `boundary_mode`, the preview cursor, the atomic orchestration across Graph + Layout + Body" / "Assembly repeats: socket compatibility is Layout's verdict, `can_join` is the Graph's, **both must pass before `apply`**" | **Agrees.** Assembly does not call LM4 (MSA-24). The "preview cursor" is read as: Assembly decides *when* the cursor is offered (`weld_offer`) and Layout supplies the displayables, drawn in world space (UI Requirements). The host rule is confirmed (MSA-11). |
| **Body row:** "BM3, BM4; pure `can_rebase_attach`, `can_rebase_detach`, `actors_overlapping`, `foot_boxes`, `docked`, `body_count`, `actors_on`" / "Assembly owns the weld and cut commands, weld quality `q`, the order of mutators and the host-socket order" / "a PLAYER-origin cut calls `actors_overlapping` for both hatch rectangles and rejects (`CUT_BLOCKED` suggested, Assembly's code) and a system-origin cut should defer while it is non-empty (BDY-24); the home-ship socket is passed first". Must-repeats: the SYSTEM-origin cut defers and is preceded by the tell window, "deferral length and expiry are Assembly's"; "a PLAYER-origin weld is only OFFERED once `docked` holds" | **Agrees and repeats both must-repeats** (MSA-12, MSA-21, MSA-22). **Wording refresh owed (C7):** PLAYER/SYSTEM is read as `cause` CHANNEL/CASCADE, because every channel completion is SYSTEM-origin. Name adopted: `MSA.CUT_BLOCKED`. |
| **Save/Load row:** "Owns no block (acts through Graph/Layout/Body mutators); a weld/cut completion is an autosave **event** (name provisional)" / "A completion notification Save/Load may consume (mechanism per SL-13); Assembly never calls Save/Load" | **Agrees** on the event and on never calling Save/Load (MSA-33). **Answers "name provisional" (SL OQ-24):** the names are `ASSEMBLY_WELD` and `ASSEMBLY_CUT`, polled through `last_shape_change_T()` (D.9). **Contradicts (C2):** Assembly owns `assembly.state` (a live per-seat `pending` gate stored as NONE, `last_result` runtime-only, the `[VS]` deferral record; no world history) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; an inventory row is owed in Save/Load's block table. |
| **Controller row:** "Weld/cut channels accrue in Assembly's block; abort on movement read from `controller.state`; facing lock for the work loop" / PC-7: "Progress is sim state in the owner's block" | **Agrees** on abort on movement and the facing lock. **Contradicts (C3):** progress accrues in `interaction.state` with `owner_system` ASSEMBLY, not in Assembly's block. PC-8 carve-out (C1). |
| **Attention row:** "**Must repeat:** which events push (a weld-completion push is its decision) without duplicating Interaction's LOUD at cut completion" and E-44 "Interaction pushes one `LOUD` (IA-33), Assembly pushes nothing at MVP (no double count)" | **Answers the must-repeat** (MSA-29): Assembly pushes nothing; no `LOUD` at weld completion. **Agrees (C5 closed 2026-10-27):** Attention E-44 and AC-AT-72 now assert zero `LOUD` at weld completion. |
| **Emitter row:** "Assembly / Web Map & Transit \| calls E1 / E2 inside the atomic weld, cut, hop and load apply" | **Contradicts (C6):** weld and cut call no Emitter mutator (MSA-17). |
| **Derelict Authoring row (DA:684, current):** "Assembly owns the weld, cut and install verbs, weld quality `q` and the MSA-11 host rule; `socket_plan` and `apply` are Layout's, `can_join` is the Graph's, and the cap plate is Layout's presentation of CAPPED" / "the lane set is POWER 0 + O2 0 + hatch and the offset is (696, 384) (DA-10)" | **Repeats** the lane set and offset (MSA-11). **Agrees** on `socket_plan` (Layout), `can_join` (Graph) and the cap plate (Layout presentation). **Contradicts (C11):** the weld/cut/install apply is Assembly's command apply (MSA-14, MSA-20, MSA-27), which calls Layout's LM2/LM3; Layout owns those mutators, not the apply. |
| **Pipeline row:** "weld/detach notifications that change the avatar's body or the loaded rect" / "**Routed:** detached sections **translate without rotation** in the interior view (C2)" | **Adopted** (MSA-20): `heading_q` 0. |

**T12 — Downstream and reader systems.**

| System (status) | Data Assembly supplies | Data Assembly takes | Interface owner | Provisional notes and must-repeat |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Boarding & Salvage** (designed) | the install command; the weld offer once docked; the seam cut as the extraction service; `last_result` | the carried-module state | Boarding: the sortie loop and extraction decisions; Assembly: the commands | Boarding must repeat the install precondition (carried, compatible, at a hardpoint); the Item DB's `T_cycle` is "cut + carry + `cap_port` on the stubs + install" (Interaction's and Assembly's). Dependency strength **Hard** (the reference sortie ends in the `htr_a` install, BS-17). `scr_a` has no MVP destination: no vacant scrubber hardpoint and no `remove_module` (MSA-28; Boarding C-BS-3, EC-BS-16), so it comes home as a loose item |
| **Web Map & Transit** (designed) | `weld_offer` (gated on `docked`); weld and cut result via Layout/Graph dirty keys; `last_shape_change_T()` | `docked(a, b, frame_shift_px)` established before the offer and held while an ASSEMBLY weld channel is open; `init_world`; the detached piece persists as a dormant orphan body (record status ORPHAN); no discard in MVP (WM-23); the pod body counted in `MAX_BODIES` | Transit: docking, `init_world`, the orphan record; Body: the `docked` test; Assembly: gates the offer | BDY-27 must-repeat: the PLAYER-origin weld is only offered once `docked` holds. Web Map WM-22 holds `docked` while an ASSEMBLY weld channel is open (ASK T1 answered); WM-23 detects weld and cut by a read (`last_shape_change_T()` early-out) |
| **Attention System** (designed) | nothing pushed; `body_of` changes via Layout; the cut-away fact via Layout (locus fallback) | none | Attention (units, locus, breach machine) | C5 closed; verify ASK A1 (a cut must not launder Attention) |
| **Derelict Authoring** (designed) | `socket_plan` / `can_join` consumption | the MVP socket pair, lanes POWER 0 + O₂ 0, offset `(696, 384)`, the one-sided-hatch convention, the fault order | Authoring: content; Layout: schema | DA-10 must-repeat; ASK D1 answered (OQ-DA-31) |
| **Emitter State, 2D Lighting, Pipeline** (designed) | Layout and Body dirty keys only | none | Emitter: rebuild from `SEC/` and `BODY/` (EM-30 `body_id` partition); Lighting: partition by `BODIES_LIVE_MAX`; Pipeline: camera cut on `BODY_TRANSFER` | CONFLICT C6; the PROJECTOR is a P5 subscriber (MSA-39) |
| **Horror Entities** (Designed; *Amended 2026-10-27 — design review*) | layout changes (weld, cut) via Layout dirty keys (`SEC/`, `BODY/`); no cut verb for entities (MSA-22) | none | Horror: the path-cache recompute (HE-22) | HE-22 names Assembly as a Soft neighbour; refresh: HE-22 should cite MSA-14/MSA-20 (weld/cut apply) rather than MSA-22 (the CASCADE-cut rule) |
| **Failure & Trail** (designed) | seam and cut state through the Graph | none | Failure & Trail: renders the weep and breach | The seam weep, the hiss and the `SEAL_STRAIN` creak are its rendering of Graph descriptors |
| **Adaptive Audio & Tells** (Designed, `adaptive-audio-and-tells.md`) | the weld and cut sound moments; the CASCADE tell-window co-ownership | the 10-30 s tell | Audio: sound; Assembly: cue timing | repeat the tell window |
| **Perception Filter / Sanity** (both Designed; `perception-filter.md`, `sanity-and-hallucination-rules.md`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | none | truth-channel tags for bead, cap plate, socket geometry, `CUT_BLOCKED` | Filter | **Repeated**: PF row 11 (seam geometry, cap plate, bead, `CUT_BLOCKED`, install, weld and cut prompts never wrapped or faked) and PF row 12 (the seam lane's own valve and breaker sprites are TRUTH until G8 and FT1 publish a zero-gradient witness; the wrapped-sprite registry must hold none of their ids); Sanity SN-15 L-C and its `harm_eligible` read for `CUT_STRANDS` (ASK F2; adopted by default, user ruling D53, `[VS-B]`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. MSA-35 and the install-prompt rule stand |
| **HUD & Suit Layer, Debug Overlay** (both Designed; `hud-and-suit-layer.md`, `debug-overlay-true-values.md`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: In-World Instruments is dropped from this row; its GDD has no Assembly dependency, and the "feel the seam" read of MSA-23b is a separate ask, IW1)* | the cursor data contract via Layout; the bar via `channel_view`; the Overlay reads true `q`, `leak_bp`, seam state | the prompt-plate and linear-bar grammar | Interaction: prompt; HUD: the three verb tokens (HUD's verb-token row); Overlay: true values (the Overlay's Assembly rows) | both GDDs carry an Assembly row; no longer provisional |

**Tick-order facts the rows above depend on.** Within one tick, P2 (commands: the `begin_*` applies, the completions, install) precedes P3 (hooks). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* The Graph hooks run on separate ticks (power at `T mod 6 = 0`, atmo at 2, failure at 4, Graph LS-8); on any tick the P3 order is the resolved order, in which whichever Graph hook is due runs before `emitter.place` -> `interaction.step` -> `assembly.step` (IA-14: through the `emitter.place` chain). The weld applies in P2, so the first Graph hook that runs after it (power, atmo or failure, whichever is due next) refreshes the topology (Graph phase 1 refresh at the head of any hook) and sees the bumped `topology_version` and the new seam, so no half-state is observable. A completion submitted in `assembly.step` in P3 of `T` is dispatched in P2 of `T+1`. Body's mutators run after Layout's, which run after the Graph's, inside one `WriteScope`.

## Formulas

Assembly's arithmetic is small on purpose. The Graph owns the seam formulas (`seam_at_weld`, `A_seam_m`, the SEALED/RAGGED hole and open-end rates), Layout the weld offset and detach re-base, Body the docking invariant and `actors_overlapping`, Interaction the channel clock, ledger and subject budget. This section defines only what Assembly owns (`q`, the two offer predicates, host and mate selection, the cut-mode derivation, the pending TTL, the body-count bound, the shape-change time, the CASCADE deadline) and works each cited formula at MVP values so the numbers a reviewer sees are the numbers a test asserts. Every formula is integer arithmetic (the kernel's determinism rule: anything hashed, replicated or replayed is integer or fixed-point); the only fractional values below are display conversions marked as such. Numbering matches the cross-references in Detailed Design (D.2 to D.12 are cited there); D.13 to D.18 are additions. Every formula that Assembly owns is a registry candidate as a **formula** (name, expression, variables), not only as constants (`docs/consistency-failures.md`: register the formula). MVP data: the pair `sock_airlock_e` (home-ship, facing E, `line_px` 720, `start_px` 672) and `sock_b_w` (derelict B, facing W, `line_px` 24, `start_px` 288), `width_tiles` 2, lanes POWER 0 and O2 0, weld offset `(696, 384)` (DA-10), `GRID_24` closure. Where a value is not published by its owner it is marked `[UNV]`.

### D.1 Weld quality

The weld_quality formula is defined as:

`q = clamp( WELD_Q_DEFAULT , Q_MIN , Q_MAX )`, with `Q_MIN = max(400, ceil(LEAK_HP / 10))` and `Q_MAX = 990`

Derived: `Q_MIN` is the larger of 400 and the smallest `q` whose `hp_seam = q x 10` (`LSG D.14`) is not below `LEAK_HP` (that smallest `q` is `ceil(LEAK_HP / 10)`, so a `LEAK_HP` that is not a multiple of 10 rounds up and the integer-division ambiguity does not arise; the 400 outer floor holds across the Graph's 2000-6000 `LEAK_HP` range); `Q_MAX` is the registered 990, 3 permille under the first `q` (993) at which the Graph's weep is 0 from birth (`LSG D.2`, floor form) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: re-justified; the earlier "10 permille under the q = 1000 cliff" came from the superseded ceiling form. Q_MAX stays 990, a registered value, but a weld at Q_MAX now weeps only 2 milli flow units for about 6.3 minutes, so it is the faintest legal weld, not a still-weeping one)*. The clamp runs once, at data load, never per weld; a clamped value raises the diagnostic `MSA_Q_CLAMPED`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **this is the one place the range of `q` is stated**: `Q_MIN(LEAK_HP)` to 990, which is 400 to 990 at the default `LEAK_HP` 4000 and 600 to 990 at 6000. Every other mention (MSA-13, T9, T17, FB-8, VA-15) cites D.1.)* The rule has no other term: there is no `sum q_mods`, no per-weld input and no state read.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WELD_Q_DEFAULT` | int (permille) | 400-990, default 850 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | data file (registry candidate ★) | The one weld-quality constant; refines advisor R11 (was 400-1000) |
| `LEAK_HP` | int (bp) | 2000-6000, default 4000 | Graph tuning (cited, locked) | Below this a fluid sub-edge starts in LEAK |
| `Q_MIN` | int (permille) | 400-600 (400 at `LEAK_HP` <= 4000; 600 at 6000) | derived | Lower clamp; moves with `LEAK_HP`, never below 0 |
| `Q_MAX` | int (permille) | 990 | derived (`1000 - 10`) | Upper clamp |
| `q` | int (permille) | `Q_MIN`..990 | calculated | The value placed in the plan handed to `weld_join` and stored on the SeamRecord |

**Output Range:** 400 to 990 inclusive. Two named cliffs sit just outside it. *Below 400:* `hp_seam` falls under `LEAK_HP` and every fluid sub-edge of the seam starts in LEAK mode, a leak with no prior tell (a P2 fairness break). *At q 993 and above (1000 is the limiting case):* `A_seam_m` is 0 from the first period, the seam has no weep and the weld loses its P1 tell *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. `A_seam_m` is the Graph's (`LSG D.2`) and falls linearly with `leak_bp`, which wears in by 100 bp per 10 s period from 10000 (`leak_bp = 10000 - 100 x period`); the table is worked at registry defaults for `L_seam` 2 and states no expression. Within the range the Graph's consequences are physical and cited, not restated (below, `L_seam` 2, cable port caps 3000):

| `q` | `(500 + q/2)` permille of the lesser port | `cap_seam` (W) | `hp_seam` | `A_seam_m` at period 0 (milli flow units, `L_seam` 2) | `A_seam_m` at period 50 | weep ends | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 399 (loaded as-is, would clamp to 400) | 699 | 2097 | 3990 (below `LEAK_HP` 4000) | 11,558 | 5,778 | period 100 | leaks at birth: outside the range |
| 400 | 700 | 2100 | 4000 (not below) | 11,520 | 5,760 | period 100 | the lower bound |
| 700 | 850 | 2550 | 7000 (`HP_OK` boundary) | 2,880 | 1,440 | period 100 | legal, tight |
| 850 | 925 | 2775 | 8500 | 720 | 360 | period 100 (about 16.7 min) | the MVP default |
| 990 | 995 | 2985 | 9900 | 2 | 0 | period 38 (about 6.3 min) | the upper bound: a faint, short weep |
| 1000 (loaded as-is, would clamp to 990) | 1000 | 3000 | 10000 | 0 | 0 | none | no weep at birth: outside the range |

**Example:** the data file ships `WELD_Q_DEFAULT = 850`: no clamp, `q = 850`, `MSA_Q_CLAMPED` not raised. A modder or a bad edit ships 1000: the loader clamps to 990, raises `MSA_Q_CLAMPED` once at load, the weld produces `A_seam_m` 2 at period 0 (a faint weep that ends at period 38, about 6.3 minutes) and the Graph's descriptor keeps `intensity_pm` at least 1 while it flows (LS-14) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.

### D.2 Offer predicates

The weld_offer formula is defined as:

`weld_offer(seat, s) = ( tool_in_hand(seat) == tool_welder ) AND lit(seat, s) AND in_reach(seat, s) AND ( socket_state(s) == FREE ) AND ( |Pairs(s)| >= 1 )`, with `Pairs(s) = { (a, b) : s IN {a, b} AND host_of(a, b) AND can_attach(a, b) == OK AND docked(a, b, frame_shift_px(a, b)) }` and `host_of` the host rule of D.3 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the earlier form defined `weld_offer` through a host socket `a` that D.3 defined through the same candidate set; the focused socket `s` now seeds the candidate pairs and D.3 only chooses among them)*

Derived: the prompt `E . Weld` exists iff `weld_offer` is true. The predicate reads no node or edge accessor, calls neither `can_join_static` nor the full `can_join`, and reads no `surge[]` (MSA-9, MSA-12). The carry lockout (a seat carrying an item gets no channelled verb, PC-7, IA-28) is Interaction's selector, not a term here; a carrying seat therefore never sees the weld prompt and `begin_weld` is a `BUSY` backstop.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `tool_in_hand(seat)` | enum | tool class id | Suit (`tool_welder` is class {1, 9}, slot 2, equipped at start) | The equipped tool |
| `lit(seat, s)` | bool | 0 or 1 | Emitter State via Interaction's lit check | The socket subject is lit (lit-only channelled verbs, PC-7) |
| `in_reach(seat, s)` | bool | 0 or 1 | Interaction reach geometry, `REACH_PX` 72 | The socket subject is within reach |
| `socket_state(s)` | enum | `FREE`, `WELDED`, `CAPPED`, `CUT_OPEN` | Layout (LY-13) | Terminal lifecycle (S3) |
| `can_attach(a, b)` | enum | `OK` or one of eight reasons | Layout (LY-14) | The mating verdict |
| `frame_shift_px(a, b)` | int pair (px) | on the 24-px grid | Layout `LY D.6` (ASK L3 asks for it as a pure read) | The shift the weld would apply (D.5) |
| `docked(a, b, fs)` | bool | 0 or 1 | Body `BDY D.8` | The two bodies already sit at the weld offset with equal velocity |
| `Pairs(s)` | set of ordered socket pairs `(a, b)` | 0..`MAX_SOCKETS_PER_SECTION` x `MAX_SECTIONS` in principle; exactly 0 or 1 in MVP | calculated | The ordered pairs that contain the focused socket `s`, put in host order by D.3, and pass both `can_attach` and `docked` |
| `weld_offer` | bool | 0 or 1 | calculated | Whether the prompt exists |

**Output Range:** boolean. Pure and total: no side effect, no module-body state. In MVP `|Pairs|` is 0 or 1 by authoring (one socket pair); with several candidate pairs `[VS]` D.3 selects the lowest key pair. Per-call cost is O(1) over cached static data (`MSA_WELD_OFFER_BUDGET_US`, set in the spike).

**Example:** the seat holds the welder, stands lit within 72 px of `sock_airlock_e`; the socket is `FREE`; `sock_b_w` passes `can_attach` (facings E and W are opposite, `width_tiles` 2 and 2, `SOCKET_CLASS` 1 and 1, lane multisets equal, no `GEOM_OVERLAP` under Layout's `rects_in` test: the home `spine_corridor` east hull wall is x [672, 696) per Derelict Authoring D.13); B is docked at `frame_shift_px = (696, 384)`: `|Pairs| = 1`, offer = 1, the prompt shows. The same stance with B still 24 px short of its dock (`world(b) - world(a) = (720, 384)`): `docked` is false, `|Pairs| = 0`, offer = 0, **no prompt and no "can't weld" icon**.

The seam_cut_offer formula is defined as:

`seam_cut_offer(seat, s) = ( tool_in_hand(seat) == tool_cutter ) AND lit(seat, s) AND in_reach(seat, s) AND ( socket_state(s) == WELDED ) AND ( seam_state(seam_of(s)) == ACTIVE ) AND ( foot_section(seat) IN keep_sections(seam_of(s)) )`

Derived: `keep_sections` is the component of the weld forest that keeps its `body_id` (the one containing the body's `origin_section_id`, LY-17; in MVP the home-ship side), from Layout's `detach_preview` (ASK L1). There is deliberately **no `CUT_BLOCKED` term**: the prompt is offered, `CUT_BLOCKED` is checked at begin, handoff and completion (D.13), and the clean hatch-presence oracle stays a rejection the player can see (MSA-21).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `tool_in_hand(seat)` | enum | tool class id | Suit (`tool_cutter`, class {8}, slot 1) | The equipped tool |
| `lit`, `in_reach` | bool | 0 or 1 | as above | |
| `socket_state(s)` | enum | as above | Layout | Must be `WELDED` |
| `seam_of(s)` | seam id | `seam_id` allocated by the Graph | Layout `welds[]` | The seam the socket is part of |
| `seam_state` | enum | `ACTIVE`, `CUT` | Layout / Graph | Only an `ACTIVE` seam can be cut |
| `foot_section(seat)` | section id | a section of the seat's body | Body `BDY D.10` | The section containing the seat's foot **origin** (BDY-23) |
| `keep_sections(seam)` | set of section ids | 1..`MAX_SECTIONS` | Layout `detach_preview` (ASK L1) | Sections that keep `body_id` after the cut |

**Output Range:** boolean. **Example:** welded pair, seat standing in the home-ship airlock (a section in the home component): offer = 1. The same seat standing in derelict B's `b_dock`: `foot_section` is in the leaving component, offer = 0 and **no cut prompt exists on the derelict side** (the self-strand trap is unreachable). A `FREE` socket: offer = 0 (state must be `WELDED`); a `CAPPED` socket: offer = 0 (terminal).

### D.3 Host and mate selection

The host_and_mate formula is defined as:

`host_of(a, b) = ( kind(sec(a)) == HOME AND kind(sec(b)) == DERELICT )` ; `(socket_a, socket_b) = argmin over Pairs(s) of ( socket_key_a , socket_key_b )` in that lexicographic order, with `Pairs(s)` as in D.2; the pair is empty when `Pairs(s)` is empty *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the section variable is renamed `sec` so `s` means only a socket; the focused socket seeds `Pairs(s)`; and the tie-break over candidate pairs is stated)*

Derived: if both sections are DERELICT `[VS]`, `host_of` instead holds for the ordering whose `socket_a` is on the body with the lower `body_id`. **When the HOME section has several sockets** `[VS]`, a seat focusing a derelict socket `s` has one candidate pair per FREE HOME socket that passes `can_attach` and `docked`, and the pair with the lowest `socket_key_a` wins, then the lowest `socket_key_b`; a seat focusing a HOME socket has `socket_a = s` and the tie-break runs over the mates. In the MVP there is one pair, so no tie-break fires. The seat may stand on either side and focus either socket; Assembly derives the ordered pair, so the surviving body is always the home-ship's and the absorbed derelict body is re-based (LY-16 / LY Open Question 21: confirmed).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `kind(s)` | enum | `HOME`, `DERELICT` | Graph LS-17 | Section kind |
| `body_id` | int | 0..`MAX_BODIES` - 1 | Layout | The tie-break for a two-derelict pair `[VS]` |
| `socket_key` | string id | stable, ordered by byte comparison | Layout | The tie-break among several candidate pairs `[VS]` |
| `sec(x)` | section id | a section of the loaded set | Layout | The section a socket belongs to *(renamed from the reused `s`, re-review)* |
| `Pairs(s)` | set | as D.2 | calculated | Candidate ordered pairs containing the focused socket |

**Output Range:** one ordered pair `(socket_a, socket_b)` or the empty pair (no offer). Deterministic: the same state always selects the same pair, which replay and MIRROR rely on. **Example:** the seat stands in derelict B's `b_dock` and focuses `sock_b_w`; `kind(derelict_b)` is DERELICT, `kind(home_ship)` is HOME; the pair is `(sock_airlock_e, sock_b_w)` regardless of which socket was focused, and B is the absorbed side.

### D.4 Seam properties at weld (cited from the Graph, worked at MVP values)

The seam_at_weld formula is defined as:

`plan_seam = seam_at_weld( q , cap_a , cap_b , L_seam )`; **owned by the Graph (`LSG D.14`, registry `seam_at_weld`; `LSG D.2` for `A_seam_m`); Assembly supplies `q` (D.1) and the plan's `L_seam`, restates no expression and adds no term**

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `q` | int (permille) | `Q_MIN` to 990 (D.1) | D.1 | Supplied by Assembly |
| `L_seam` | int (tiles) | 1-12 | Layout `width_tiles`, via `socket_plan` | MVP value 2 |
| `cap_a`, `cap_b` | int (loop units per step) | per port | the two joined ports' `cap` (Item DB defs; authored for bare nodes) | POWER lane: `mod_breaker_junction` CABLE ports, 3000 each (Item DB def 12, AC-79). O2 lane: `dj_air` and `dj_b_dock` are bare DUCT_JUNCTION nodes whose `cap` no design file publishes: **GAP, symbol `cap_duct`, `[UNV]`**, ASK G6(d) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: still unpublished after three routings with no owner (Item DB Open Question 30, Graph open-question row 26, OQ-DA-33). The only O2-lane value that depends on it is `cap_seam`; `hp_seam`, `leak_bp` and `A_seam_m` do not, and are worked below. Proposed working value for the owner to confirm or replace: `cap_duct` = 1200 mGU/step, equal to the largest O2-lane port in the Item DB (`mod_o2_tank`, retuned cap 1200), so the junction is never the bottleneck of the lane it joins. It is a proposal, not a fact, and no assertion rests on it until the owner publishes the value and the registry carries it as `cap_duct_junction`.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: **owner assigned: Derelict Authoring**, which authors the bare DUCT_JUNCTION nodes `dj_air` and `dj_b_dock` (DA-19). It is asked to publish `cap_duct_junction` in the registry; the proposed 1200 mGU/step is acceptable as the first value `[PLACEHOLDER]`. Severity: the creative-director rules this RECOMMENDED, because implementation starts on the injected value; the owner must be assigned before the O2-weld Integration story starts, and it is (routed as a hard requirement in T13).)* |
| `A_SEAM_MAX`, `SEAM_LEAK_INITIAL_BP`, `SEAM_WEARIN_BP_PER_100`, `WELD_DP_WARN`, `LEAK_HP` | int | 16, 10000, 100, 300 dkPa, 4000 | Graph tuning (cited, locked) | Used only inside the Graph's expressions |

**Output Range:** the Graph's own: `cap_seam` 700 to 995 permille of the lesser port across Assembly's `q` range, `hp_seam` 4000 to 9900, `leak_bp` starts at 10000, `A_seam_m` per `LSG D.2`.

**Example (MVP POWER lane, `q` 850):** `cap_a = cap_b = 3000`; `cap_seam = floor(3000 x 925 / 1000) = 2775 W`; `hp_seam = 8500` (above `HP_OK` 7000 and `LEAK_HP` 4000); `leak_bp = 10000`; `A_seam_m` = 720 milli flow units at period 0 (the Graph's value, LSG D.2: 360 per tile over 2 tiles), 360 at period 50 and 0 at period 100 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. The O2 lane: `cap_seam = floor( min(cap_duct_a, cap_duct_b) x 925 / 1000 )`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: completed as far as the data allows. **O2 lane at `q` 850, `L_seam` 2:** `hp_seam` = 8500, `leak_bp` = 10000 and `A_seam_m` = 720 at period 0, all independent of the duct port cap and identical to the POWER lane, because `A_seam_m` depends only on `q`, `L_seam` and `leak_bp` (`LSG D.2`); **verified against LSG D.14 (2026-10-27):** `hp_seam = q x 10`, `leak_bp = SEAM_LEAK_INITIAL_BP` and `A_seam_m` per D.2 are computed per seam from `q`, `L_seam` and `leak_bp`, and only `cap_seam = floor(min(cap_a, cap_b) x (500 + q/2) / 1000)` reads the ports' caps. `cap_seam` is `floor(min(cap_duct_a, cap_duct_b) x 925 / 1000)`: with the proposed `cap_duct` 1200 `[UNV]` it would be `floor(1200 x 925 / 1000)` = 1110 mGU/step; with a hypothetical 800 it would be 740; the AC asserts the formula with the injected value, and asserts the number only once the owner publishes `cap_duct`. The O2 half of the weld Integration story is the only work gated on this: the kernel, the PROJECTOR (S3), the fault model (S6) and the power-lane weld proceed.)* Wear-in (cited): `leak_bp` falls 100 bp per 10 s period from 10000, so the weep tapers linearly (720, 360 and 0 milli at periods 0, 50 and 100 for `q` 850) and stops at period 100: `100 x 10 s = 1000 s`, about 16.7 minutes; at `q` 990 it is 2 milli until `leak_bp` falls below 6250 (period 38, about 6.3 minutes) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. Surge (cited, `LSG D.14`): home bay at 1000 dkPa against a derelict room at 150 dkPa gives `|dP|` = 850 > `WELD_DP_WARN` 300, so `surge[].warn` is true and the closed hatch creaks (`SEAL_STRAIN`); the weld is permitted and moves nothing. Assembly's cue reads `warn` as a boolean only (MSA-15).

### D.5 Weld offset prediction and the actor invariant

The frame_shift_prediction_equal formula is defined as:

`frame_shift_px = offset_a + pos_a - pos_b - offset_b_old` ; the apply asserts `LM2.frame_shift_px == predicted` (else `MSA_SHIFT_DRIFT`) ; `docked(a, b, fs) = ( vel(a) == vel(b) ) AND ( world(b) - world(a) == fs )` ; `abs(actor) = world(body) + pos_curr`

Derived: the first is Layout's `LY D.6` (attach), the third Body's `BDY D.8`; Assembly computes nothing new, it compares the value predicted in `validate` (V3) with the value LM2 returns (A3) and BM3 consumes (A4). Because `offset_a`, `pos_a` and `pos_b` all lie on the 24-px grid (`GRID_24` lint), so does the result (the 24-grid closure, `LY D.6`).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `offset_a`, `offset_b_old` | int pair (px) | on the 24-px grid; `(0, 0)` for a freshly built single-section body (LY-25) | Layout | Host section offset in the surviving body; absorbed section offset in its own body |
| `pos_a`, `pos_b` | int pair (px) | on the 24-px grid, local >= 0 | Layout socket reference points: `(line_px, start_px)` for facing E or W | The two sockets' reference points |
| `frame_shift_px` | int pair (px) | on the 24-px grid, far below `WORLD_COORD_LIMIT` 2^22 | calculated (Layout) | The absorbed body's frame origin in the host frame; every absorbed section GAINS it |
| `world(a)`, `world(b)`, `vel(a)`, `vel(b)` | int pairs | Body ranges | `body.state` | Exterior poses and velocities |
| `pos_curr` | int pair (px) | in the actor's body frame | `body.state` | The actor's position |

**Output Range:** exact integers. `delta_abs = frame_shift_px - (world(b) - world(a))` is `(0, 0)` iff the position half of `docked` holds; when it does not hold the weld still succeeds in the simulation and the absorbed hull jumps by `delta_abs` in the exterior layer only (reachable only in the residual window of E-17; the camera cut does not hide it, BDY-27, and the former fallback of dropping the `docked` term is deleted) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.

**Example (DA-10):** `offset_a = (0, 0)`, `pos_a = (720, 672)`, `pos_b = (24, 288)`, `offset_b_old = (0, 0)`: `frame_shift_px = (0 + 720 - 24 - 0, 0 + 672 - 288 - 0) = (696, 384)` (29 x 24 and 16 x 24). With `world(home) = (1000, 500)` and `world(B) = (1696, 884)`, equal velocity: `world(b) - world(a) = (696, 384)`, docked, `delta_abs = (0, 0)`. An actor on B at `pos_curr = (100, 300)` has `abs = (1696, 884) + (100, 300) = (1796, 1184)` before; BM3 gives `pos_curr = (796, 684)` in the host frame, `abs = (1000, 500) + (796, 684) = (1796, 1184)`: equal. Not docked (`world(B) = (1720, 884)`): the difference is `(720, 384)`, `delta_abs = (696, 384) - (720, 384) = (-24, 0)`, the case the offer gate keeps away from the player. (Layout's own `LY D.6` example, `(576, 96)`, is a different socket placement and is not the MVP pair.) A weld followed by a cut of the same seam returns `pos_curr -= frame_shift_px` and zeroes the sub-pixel part, so it is **not** an identity on `pos_curr`; tests assert `abs` and section-local position.

### D.6 Cut mode

The cut_mode formula is defined as:

`cut_mode(seam) = SEALED if ( for all o in openings(seam): area(o) == 0 ) AND ( for all e in fluid_sub_edges(seam): valve(e) == CLOSED ) AND ( for all c in cable_sub_edges(seam): breaker(c) == OPEN ), else RAGGED`

Derived: the three clauses are exactly the as-welded state `weld_join` leaves (`LSG D.14`: opening `area` 0, valves CLOSED, breaker OPEN). The test reads **positions only**, never `hp`, `state` or `served_pm`. It is evaluated once, in the completion `apply` (step A1), and the single result is passed identically to M3 and LM3. No payload, prompt or cue carries or names a mode.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `area(o)` | int | 0..`area_max` | Graph via `seam_view` (ASK G3) | Opening area (the hatch) |
| `valve(e)` | enum | `OPEN`, `CLOSED` | Graph via `seam_view` | PIPE and DUCT sub-edge valve position |
| `breaker(c)` | enum | `OPEN`, `CLOSED` | Graph via `seam_view` | CABLE sub-edge breaker position |
| `cut_mode` | enum | `SEALED`, `RAGGED` | calculated | The `boundary_mode` for M3 and LM3 |

**Output Range:** two values, deterministic. SEALED: nothing leaks, the socket becomes `CAPPED`, tombstones with a cap plate. RAGGED: per former opening a virtual hole `A_CUT` 24 on each side as a hull stub of `hp` 2500 (severity 4 BREACH, EXOGENOUS, exempt from the fairness gate, LS-16), each fluid port an `open_end` leaking at `OPEN_END_RATE_PM` 500 until `cap_port`; a 20-tile room reaches the DECOMP band in 382 steps (38 s) and VACUUM in 960 steps (96 s) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; the socket becomes `CUT_OPEN`. All cited from the Graph.

**Example (MVP seam: one hatch OPENING, one O2 DUCT sub-edge, one POWER CABLE sub-edge):**

| hatch `area` | O2 valve | breaker | `cut_mode` | what the player saw beforehand |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | CLOSED | OPEN | SEALED | a quiet seam: no creak, no frost, no valve hiss, no draught *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the bead's own weep hiss may still be present during wear-in and says nothing about the mode, MSA-23 item 1)* |
| 64 (open) | CLOSED | OPEN | RAGGED | the leaf standing open with a draught across the threshold |
| 0 | OPEN | OPEN | RAGGED | a hiss at the open valve's lane |
| 0 | CLOSED | CLOSED | RAGGED | the junction's own powered state |
| 64 | OPEN | CLOSED | RAGGED | all three |
| 0 | OPEN | CLOSED | RAGGED | the open valve's hiss and the junction's powered state *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| 64 | CLOSED | CLOSED | RAGGED | the open leaf with a draught and the junction's powered state *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| 64 | OPEN | OPEN | RAGGED | the open leaf with a draught and the open valve's hiss *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the matrix is now all eight combinations of hatch, valve and breaker. Two quantifier cases follow from the formula and are tested with it: a hatch at `area` 1 (partly open) is RAGGED because only `area` 0 satisfies the first clause, and a seam with two openings is SEALED only if **both** are 0, so one at `area` 0 and one at `area` 1 is RAGGED.)*

A hatch closed but a hallucinated "closed" valve that is truly open: RAGGED; the hiss is the consequence-channel witness the player could have heard (MSA-23). A hatch opened during a cancelled-and-resumed cut makes the resumed cut RAGGED because the mode is re-derived at completion.

### D.7 Channel timing and exposure

The channel_timing formula is defined as:

`T_done = Tb + D - resume_offset` ; `T_apply = T_done + 1` ; `blocks = D / GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` ; `exposure_s = ( D - resume_offset ) / TICK_RATE` ; `tool_pushes = D - resume_offset` ; constraint `D mod GRAPH_STEP_TICKS == 0`

Derived: the clock, the abort set, the ledger and the noise are Interaction's (IA-14, IA-16, IA-33); Assembly supplies only `D` and consumes `T_done`. Accrual is at `e = T - Tb` = 1 to `D - resume_offset`, so the DONE handoff is the P3 of tick `T_done` and the completion applies in the P2 of `T_apply` (MSA-3). `resume_offset = blocks_done x B` while the ledger entry is valid (`T - abort_tick <= PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS`), else 0 (`IA D.6`, cited). A cancel wastes at most `B - 1` accrued ticks (the partial block is not credited), so cancel-to-peek is never free. **Minimum resumed channel** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: `D - resume_offset` can be as small as `B` = 6 ticks (one Graph block left), which is why the BUSY gate of D.11, not a channel-length floor, guards the handoff.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Tb` | int64 tick | >= 0 | the P2 tick of `begin_*` | Begin tick (held as two 32-bit integers or a string at the serialisation boundary, SL-2) |
| `D` | int ticks | 240-480, multiple of 6 | `WELD_CHANNEL_TICKS` 360 / `SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS` 240 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Declared duration |
| `resume_offset` | int ticks | 0..`D - B` | Interaction ledger (cited) | Credited ticks from an earlier aborted channel |
| `B` = `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` | int | 6 | Graph (cited) | Block length |
| `TICK_RATE` | int | 60 | Kernel (cited) | Ticks per second |
| `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` | int | 600 | Interaction (cited) | Ledger lifetime |

**Output Range:** `blocks` 40 to 80; exposure 4.0 s (cut) to 8.0 s (weld at 480) fresh; `T_apply - Tb = D + 1` for a fresh channel. The floor 240 keeps both channels in the LONG bar class (`BAR_LONG_FROM_TICKS` 240); the ceiling 480 is set by analogy with IA I-9, not a lock on Assembly.

**Example:** a fresh weld begins in P2 of `Tb` = 437039: `D` 360, `blocks` 60, accrual on ticks 437040..437399, `T_done` 437399, `T_apply` 437400; 360 TOOL pushes, exposure 6.0 s, **no** LOUD at the completion. A fresh seam cut begins at `Tb` = 439759: `D` 240, `T_done` 439999, `T_apply` 440000, 240 TOOL pushes and one LOUD at DONE (pushed by Interaction), exposure 4.0 s. A weld cancelled by movement at `e` = 100 credits block 16 (`resume_offset` 96); a re-begin within 600 ticks has remaining `360 - 96` = 264 ticks (4.4 s), so the total accrued TOOL ticks are 99 + 264 = 363, three more than the fresh 360 (the wasted partial block).

### D.8 Body-count bound

The bodies_after_worst_sequence formula is defined as:

`N(t) = N0 - W(t) + C(t)` with `C(t) <= W(t)`, therefore `N(t) <= N0` for every reachable sequence; `bodies_after_worst_sequence = N0`; lint `N0 <= min( MAX_BODIES , BODIES_LIVE_MAX )`

Derived: a weld merges two bodies into one (the absorbed body is `DISSOLVED`), a cut splits one into two, and every cut needs a prior `ACTIVE` weld, so cuts never outnumber welds. Sockets are terminal (LY-13), so `W <= P`, the number of authored socket pairs. Assembly adds no capacity rule; `can_detach`'s `CAPACITY` reason and a `MAX_SECTIONS_PER_BODY` value are deferred to Vertical Slice (Layout Open Question 8).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `N0` | int | 1..`MAX_BODIES` | built at load (Web Map `init_world`, Layout) | Live bodies before any Assembly command |
| `W(t)`, `C(t)` | int | 0..`P`; 0..`W(t)` | count of Layout `welds[]` rows ever welded / ever cut | Welds and cuts applied by tick `t` |
| `P` | int | MVP 1 | authored socket pairs | Possible welds |
| `MAX_BODIES` | int | 8 | Layout (cited, locked) | Hard cap |
| `BODIES_LIVE_MAX` | int | 4 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | 2D Lighting (cited) | Cross-body lighting cap |

**Output Range:** MVP `N(t)` is 4 before the weld, 3 after it, 4 after the one cut. **Zero margin:** the worst case equals `BODIES_LIVE_MAX` exactly. Any body added outside Assembly (a second pod, a loose piece Web Map keeps alive) or any Vertical Slice socket-economy change exceeds it, which degrades cross-body lighting (stand-ins) without breaking the sim (MSA-41, ASK LT1). **Example:** home + A + B + pod = 4; weld (B dissolves into home): `4 - 1 + 0` = 3; cut: `4 - 1 + 1` = 4; a second weld attempt returns `SOCKET_NOT_FREE`, so `N` stays 4 and `4 <= min(8, 4)` holds with margin 0.

### D.9 Shape-change time and the autosave event

The last_shape_change_T formula is defined as:

`last_shape_change_T = max over welds[] of max( welded_at_T , cut_at_T )`, or `NONE` when `welds[]` is empty ; the cited consumer is Save/Load's F-1: `event_due = (pending_events != empty) AND ( T - last_save_T ) >= AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS AND NOT (ragged_breach_open() AND ASSEMBLY_CUT IN kinds(pending_events))` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: the last conjunct is the ruled withhold, X7 / D54)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G2 (the creative-director adopts the three fixes together): the settle conjunct is NOT part of the formula (withdrawn 2026-10-28, G1: Save/Load owns `event_due` and has none); the withhold is keyed on the pending set (F-1), not on `event_kind` (the newest timestamp in `welds[]`, so a later weld bypassed it), and `ragged_breach_open()` is the venting-or-uncapped-port read defined in the table below *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. The expression above is Assembly's statement of what it publishes; the formula that consumes it is Save/Load's `event_due`, which is the owner; Assembly conforms (G1), cross-GDD note and registry request; Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*

Derived: Assembly publishes the pure read; Save/Load polls it at tick boundaries (SL-13) and Layout's `S/<seam_id>` key in the P5 batch is the notification. The event names are fixed as `ASSEMBLY_WELD` and `ASSEMBLY_CUT` (Save/Load Open Question 24, answered 2026-10-27); the MVP ships both events enabled (user ruling D17), alongside interval, QUIT and initial saves. Assembly never calls Save/Load. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: `event_pending` stays latched while the withhold holds, so the save is taken once the keep-side room has vented (band VACUUM) and its keep-side ports are capped or the port ceiling has passed *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; the interval save treats the open RAGGED window as a soft-gate row, bounded by `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS`.)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `welded_at_T`, `cut_at_T` | int64 tick | 0..2^63 - 1; `cut_at_T` unset until cut | Layout `welds[]` | Apply ticks recorded by LM2 and LM3 |
| `last_shape_change_T` | int64 tick or NONE | monotonic non-decreasing in a run | calculated | Never a JSON number: two 32-bit parts or a string at the serialisation boundary (SL-2) |
| `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS` | int | 1800 | Save/Load (cited) | Coalescing floor |
| `last_save_T`, `event_pending` | int64, bool | | Save/Load (cited) | `event_pending = (pending_events != empty)`; `pending_events` is a set of (kind, tick) entries (F-1) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |
| `event_kind` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | enum | `ASSEMBLY_WELD` iff the maximum is a `welded_at_T`; `ASSEMBLY_CUT` iff it is a `cut_at_T` | calculated | Names the event only; the withhold reads the pending-set kinds (F-1), not this single value *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review; Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |
| `ragged_breach_open()` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings; Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | bool | | Assembly pure read (MSA-33) | `EXISTS seam s in welds[] with state CUT and boundary RAGGED such that venting(s) OR ports_open(s)`; `venting(s) = EXISTS side IN seam_view(s).cut_sides, side.section_id IN keep_sections(s), EXISTS h IN side.cut_holes: h.a_hole > 0 AND compartment(h.cid).band_p != VACUUM`; `ports_open(s) = (SUM over keep sides of side.uncapped_open_ends) > 0 AND T - cut_at_T < RAGGED_WITHHOLD_MAX_TICKS`. Keep side = the sections that keep `body_id` after the cut (`keep_sections`, Layout `detach_preview`); the leaving orphan never counts. False for a SEALED cut, a seam with no `cut_sides`, a cut whose keep-side room is already VACUUM, or a closed hole (`a_hole` 0, Vertical Slice). Numbers (V = 20, one hole, `P0` 1000 dkPa, `k` = 2 x 24 / (20 x 1000) = 0.0024 per step): DECOMP band (P < 400) at `ceil(0.91629 / 0.0024)` = 382 steps = 2292 ticks; VACUUM (P < 100) at `ceil(2.302585 / 0.0024)` = 960 steps = 5760 ticks (96 s), +-2 steps; V = 30: `k` 0.0016, 1440 steps = 8640 ticks |
| `RAGGED_WITHHOLD_MAX_TICKS` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review; Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | int ticks | 2400 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, safe 2400-7200 | data file (registry candidate ★; Assembly's) | Ceiling on the PORT clause only: `ports_open` is false from `cut_at_T + RAGGED_WITHHOLD_MAX_TICKS` on, capped or not. It does not bound the decompression: 2292 ticks is the time to the DECOMP band, not to a vented room, and the hull-hole clause ends on the Graph band (VACUUM), not on a timer. At most `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` 7200; no boot lint for decompression (state-driven) |

**Output Range:** `NONE` or a tick. **Example:** the weld applies at `T` = 437400 (`welded_at_T`), `last_save_T` = 432000: `last_shape_change_T` = 437400, elapsed = 437400 - 432000 = 5400 >= 1800, so `event_due` is true at the poll of `T` = 437400 itself (no settle) and the capture is taken on the retry grid; the capture's frame cost is measured inside AC-MSA-63 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. The cut applies at 440000: `last_shape_change_T` = 440000. With `welds[]` empty (no weld ever): `NONE`, no event. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: **RAGGED worked example.** The weld's event save is written at `last_save_T` = 437400. Fixture: keep-side cut room V = 20, one hole, its other doors closed, `P0` 1000 (`k` = 0.0024). The RAGGED cut applies at `T` = 440000 with two uncapped keep-side stubs: `last_shape_change_T` = 440000, `event_kind` = `ASSEMBLY_CUT`, elapsed = 440000 - 437400 = 2600 >= 1800, but `ragged_breach_open()` is true (venting and `ports_open`), so `event_due` is false and `event_pending` stays latched. The player caps both stubs by `T` = 441800: `ports_open` is now false, but `venting` is still true (the hull hole is open; `band_p` reaches DECOMP at step 382 = `T` 442292 and VACUUM at step 960 = 5760 ticks after the cut), so `ragged_breach_open()` STAYS true. At `T` = 445760 (= 440000 + 5760, within one atmosphere step of 6 ticks) `band_p` reads VACUUM: `ragged_breach_open()` is false, elapsed = 445760 - 437400 = 8360 >= 1800, so `event_due` is true and the capture is written on the next retry-grid tick (at most 300 ticks later). The interval save that would have fallen due at 437400 + 10800 = 448200 is outside the window and is not deferred by this cut (it is re-anchored by the event capture anyway). Variant: both stubs never capped: `ports_open` lapses at 440000 + 2400 = 442400, `venting` still holds to 445760; the same capture time. Variant: V = 30 room, VACUUM at 440000 + 8640 = 448640; the interval save due at 448200 is deferred as a soft-gate input (`waited` = 448640 - 448200 = 440 < 7200, so NOT RISKY) and is written after 448640. A SEALED cut at the same `T` has no `cut_sides`: false, `event_due` true at once. (Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: recomputed with no settle conjunct; figures equal Save/Load F-1 and AC-SL-144.))*

### D.10 CASCADE deferral deadline `[VS]`

The cascade_defer_deadline formula is defined as:

`deadline_T = first_deferred_T + CASCADE_CUT_DEFER_MAX_TICKS` ; at each tick `T`: if `actors_overlapping(rects)` is empty then APPLY, else if `T >= deadline_T` then ABANDON, else DEFER

Derived: `seam_cut {cause CASCADE}` is specified and tested through a stub submitter; no MVP system submits one (MSA-22). It is preceded by the P2 tell window and by the Graph's `W_MIN_WARNING_S` 10 s gate (ENDOGENOUS transitions only), and **abandons, never forces**, on expiry (BDY-24).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `first_deferred_T` | int64 tick | | `assembly.state.cascade_deferred` (reserved, empty in MVP) | Tick of the first deferral |
| `CASCADE_CUT_DEFER_MAX_TICKS` | int ticks | 60-1800, multiple of 6 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, default 600 `[VS]` | data file (registry candidate ★) | Expiry |
| `W_MIN_WARNING_S` | int s | 10 | Graph (cited) | The fairness gate, a separate and independent knob |

**Output Range:** three outcomes (apply, defer, abandon). The equality of 600 ticks with `W_MIN_WARNING_S` x `TICK_RATE` (10 s x 60) is a coincidence of values, not a contract; no lint ties them. **Example:** `first_deferred_T` = 1000, default 600, `deadline_T` = 1600: the crewmate leaves the rectangles at 1210, the cut applies at 1210 (if the 10 s warning has elapsed); a crewmate who stays through 1600 gets ABANDON at 1600, the seam stays welded, the cut never fires.

### D.11 Pending TTL

The pending_ttl_ok formula is defined as:

`pending_ttl_ok = ( PENDING_TTL_TICKS >= ceil( QUEUE_CAPACITY / MAX_CMDS_PER_TICK ) ) AND ( PENDING_TTL_TICKS <= MAX_AGE_TICKS_ACTION - 1 )` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* ; `earliest_NOT_TAKEN_T = submit_T + PENDING_TTL_TICKS + 1`

Derived: the two clauses are, in order, the kernel's never-spuriously-stale invariant (`KRN D.7`: `max_age_ticks(any type) >= ceil(QUEUE_CAPACITY / MAX_CMDS_PER_TICK)`, a boot assertion) and Assembly's ceiling that a completion never outlives a begin's own age window. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the former third clause, `PENDING_TTL_TICKS + 1 < min(channels)`, is deleted. It was vacuous, because clause 2 caps the TTL at 119 and the channel floor is 240, and its premise was false under `resume_offset`: a resumed channel can be as short as one Graph block, 6 ticks (D.7), so "the next DONE is at least one whole channel after the next begin" does not hold. **The single guarantee that a new handoff never meets an unresolved `pending` is the `pending.kind != NONE` BUSY gate at site 1** (MSA-7): a `begin_*` is refused for as long as `pending` is set, so a new channel can only start after the TTL has resolved, whatever its length.)* Both completion types set `max_age_ticks = PENDING_TTL_TICKS`, so the kernel's `STALE` rejection and Assembly's `NOT_TAKEN` land in the same tick order: P2 rejects at `submit_T + PENDING_TTL_TICKS + 1`, then the P3 of that tick writes `NOT_TAKEN`. The result is that `DONE` and `NOT_TAKEN` are mutually exclusive (MSA-6).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PENDING_TTL_TICKS` | int ticks | 8-119, default 12 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | data file (registry candidate ★) | Lifetime of a submitted completion |
| `QUEUE_CAPACITY` | int | 1024 | Kernel (cited, locked) | Command queue size |
| `MAX_CMDS_PER_TICK` | int | 128 | Kernel (cited, locked) | Dispatch cap per tick |
| `MAX_AGE_TICKS_ACTION` | int ticks | 120 | Kernel (cited, locked) | The age bound of an action command (a begin) |
| `submit_T` | int64 tick | | `assembly.state.pending.submit_T` | When `assembly.step` submitted the completion |

**Output Range:** boolean at boot (a failing value fails the data lint); the default gives `earliest_NOT_TAKEN_T = submit_T + 13`, about 0.22 s after submission. **Example:** `PENDING_TTL_TICKS` 12: `12 >= ceil(1024/128)` = 8 yes; `12 <= 119` yes: valid. 7: fails the first clause (a legitimate completion held 8 ticks by queue truncation would be `STALE`). 119: valid, `earliest_NOT_TAKEN_T` = `submit_T + 120`. 240: fails the second clause (240 > 119) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. Under an injected backlog of 1 to 8 ticks the completion applies exactly once and `NOT_TAKEN` is never written.

### D.12 Weld notification batch

The weld_batch_key_budget formula is defined as:

`keys_block = base_block + n_moved + n_actors + n_subject_deltas <= MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` ; checked per block, for each of `graph.state`, `layout.state`, `body.state`, `interaction.state`, `assembly.state`

Derived: the kernel batches one notification per block (CR-10). The Graph's batch is fixed by its owner (`T`, `S/<seam_id>`, `SEC/<a>`, `SEC/<b>`, nothing else); Layout adds `SEC/` and `BODY/` keys for moved sections; Body adds pose and actor keys; Interaction adds only subjects whose registration changed (ASK I1(vii)). Exceeding `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` collapses a block's batch to `ALL` and costs a full rebuild, so the budget is checked, not assumed.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `base_block` | int | graph 4; layout 5; body 2; interaction 0; assembly 1 per seat that changed | the owners' key sets | Fixed keys |
| `n_moved` | int | MVP 1 (derelict B); worst case <= `MAX_SECTIONS` 24 | Layout | Sections whose offset changed |
| `n_actors` | int | 0..`MAX_ACTORS` 8 | Body | Actors transferred by BM3 or BM4 |
| `n_subject_deltas` | int | MVP <= 4 (door faces) | Interaction `sync_section` | Subjects whose registered state changed |
| `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` | int | 256 | Kernel (cited, locked) | Collapse threshold |

**Output Range:** MVP weld: `graph.state` 4; `layout.state` <= 6 (the moved section's `SEC/` key coincides with `SEC/<b>`, so 5 is the likely union and 6 the bound); `body.state` <= 3 with one avatar (2 pose rows + 1 actor; <= 10 only at the `MAX_ACTORS` 8 cap); `interaction.state` <= 4; `assembly.state` <= 1 (one key per changed seat; only the welder's seat changes in an MVP weld). Worst case `layout.state` 5 + 24 = 29. All well under 256. **The one failure mode** is `sync_section` marking every subject of both sections (about 98 + 98 fixtures plus loose items and stubs, up to about 190 in the three-section case), which is why the ask is "mark only changed subjects". **Example:** home + B weld with one avatar on B: graph 4, layout 6, body 2 + 1 = 3, interaction 4, assembly 1: five batches, the largest 6 keys. Conservation and fresh-seam disconnection on the apply tick are asserted by the Graph itself (LS-24) and cross-checked by AC-MSA-25 and AC-MSA-67; they are not re-owned here.

### D.13 Hatch-cap check (`CUT_BLOCKED`)

The cut_blocked formula is defined as:

`rects(seam) = { hatch_rect_a , hatch_rect_b }` with each of size `( WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH ) x ( width_tiles x TILE_PX )` in the body frame ; `cut_blocked = ( actors_overlapping( body_id , rects(seam) ) != empty )` with `ov( box , r ) = max(0, min(x1) - max(x0)) x max(0, min(y1) - max(y0)) > 0`

Derived: `actors_overlapping` is Body's pure read (`BDY D.11`, cited); Assembly supplies the two rectangles from Layout's hatch geometry (LY-15) and the verdict handling: a `CHANNEL` cut rejects `MSA.CUT_BLOCKED` at begin, handoff and completion, a `CASCADE` cut defers (D.10). The list includes the cutter.
