The geometry guarantee that the cutter's own foot box can stand outside both rectangles within reach is a Layout and Authoring requirement (`SOCKET_STANCE_BAND_EMPTY`, ASK L2), not an Assembly term.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH` | int (px) | 24 | art bible (registry `WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH`, value 24; Layout's hatch geometry consumes it) | Hatch plate depth |
| `TILE_PX` | int (px) | 48 | Pipeline (cited) | Tile size |
| `width_tiles` | int | 1-12; MVP 2 | Layout | Seam width: the plate is `width_tiles x 48` = 96 px long |
| `foot_box(a)` | half-open rect | 16 x 8 px (`FOOT_BOX_W` x `FOOT_BOX_H`) | Body `BDY D.2` | The actor's footprint at `pos_curr` |
| `REACH_PX` | int (px) | 72 | Controller (cited) | The interaction reach |

**Output Range:** boolean; the list is in ascending `actor_id`. **Example (MVP pair, derived from DA-10 and `LY D.6`; rectangle coordinates are `[UNV]` until Layout publishes the hatch rectangles):** `rect_a` = `[696, 720) x [672, 768)` (the home airlock's east hull plate), `rect_b` = `[720, 744) x [672, 768)` (B's west hull plate after the weld). A crewmate at `pos = (700, 700)` has box `[692, 708) x [700, 708)`; overlap with `rect_a` is `(708 - 696) x 8 = 96 > 0`: blocked. A cutter standing at `pos = (680, 720)` has box `[672, 688) x [720, 728)`: no overlap with either rectangle (8 px of clearance, one foot-box half-width, the margin ASK L2 asks Layout to guarantee) and within `REACH_PX` 72 of the hatch face (16 px away): not blocked. One foot-box further east, `pos = (696, 720)`, box `[688, 704)`: overlap with `rect_a` is `8 x 8 = 64`, blocked; this is exactly what AC-MSA-37 probes.

### D.14 Subject budget at the seam

The subject_budget_at_seam formula is defined as:

`S_total_after = S_total_before + n_subject_deltas` with the Interaction-owned lint `F + L_seed + C <= INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET`, `P <= PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE`, `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET + PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE <= 144`, `S_total <= MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS`; **owned by Interaction (`IA D.10`, I-7); Assembly supplies only `n_subject_deltas` (door faces added or removed by the weld or cut)**

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `n_subject_deltas` | int | weld: <= +4; cut: <= -4 plus stubs <= `C` | Interaction `sync_section` | Door faces registered by the weld; removed (and RAGGED stubs created) by the cut |
| `F`, `L_seed`, `C`, `P`, `A`, `H` | int | 0..120, 0..20, 0..18, 0..24, <= 4, <= 2 (`HORROR_SUBJECTS_MAX`) | Interaction (cited) | Fixtures, seeded loose items, cap stubs, player drops, actors, horrors |
| `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET`, `PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE` | int | 120, 24 | Interaction (cited) | The content lint |
| `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS` | int | 192 | Emitter State (cited) | The hard cap |

**Output Range:** a content lint, not runtime state. Over the hard cap a subject fails dark, which for a seam door reads as a hallucinated dead door (a P4 breach by accident). **Example (Interaction's registered figures, `legal_loaded_sets`):** S3 (home + B welded) `S_int` 113 + `P` 24 + `A` 4 + `H` 2 = 143, 49 below 192 *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; the seam's door faces are inside the registered S3 count. S4 `{home+B, A}`: `S_int` 145 + 30 = 175, 17 below 192 (every registered neighbourhood's `F + L_seed + C` is within `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` 120) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*. Which sections load is the decision of Web Map and Authoring, not the weld.

### D.15 Apply budget split

The apply_budget_split formula is defined as:

`weld_apply_ms = m2 + lm2 + bm3 + 2 x sync + own + verifier_release <= MSA_WELD_APPLY_BUDGET_MS` ; `cut_apply_ms` has the same shape with `m3 + lm3 + bm4`; `install_apply_ms = check + m5 + release + clear + sync + own <= MSA_INSTALL_APPLY_BUDGET_MS`

Derived: a documentation formula. The spike reports each term separately so that a failed budget points at an owner. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **the 0.5 ms split is an allocation target, not a measurement, and it is optimistic for GDScript.** The example below implies well under a microsecond per synced subject (0.10 ms for two sections of roughly a hundred subjects each), which is native-code speed; an interpreted GDScript loop with dictionary access is plausibly 3 to 5 times slower, so a spike that fails 0.5 ms would be a false negative about the design. Rules: (1) a micro-benchmark S0 runs first and measures the per-call cost of a representative mutator, a `sync_section` over 100 subjects and the release verifier subset in GDScript on RM-DESKTOP and RM-DECK (R12), and the term budgets and `MSA_*_APPLY_BUDGET_MS` are **re-derived from S0** before S1 is judged; (2) the S1 fallback (splitting the apply across ticks behind a Graph-held "welding" state) triggers **only if the whole weld tick (P2 plus P3) exceeds `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4**, the kernel's logging threshold, never merely because the 0.5 ms allocation is missed; (3) the per-term budgets stay as gating criteria on the named reference machines, pending spike measurement on RM-DESKTOP / RM-DECK (Open Question 6, closed by R12).)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the per-term budgets are **reported, not gated**; the one gate is the whole weld tick under `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4 (AC-MSA-23). A re-derived budget is frozen by a committed change before the run that judges it, and is capped at 2.0 ms, the knob's safe-range ceiling, unless the user signs off a higher figure.)* The Graph's M2 alone is locked at <= 0.2 ms Release and sits inside the weld term.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `m2`, `m3`, `m5` | float ms | `m2` <= 0.2 (locked) | Graph | Mutator cost |
| `lm2`, `lm3` | float ms | allocation targets, `[UNV]` | Layout | Mutator cost |
| `bm3`, `bm4` | float ms | `[UNV]`; up to `MAX_ACTORS` 8 transfers | Body | Mutator cost |
| `sync` | float ms | `[UNV]` | Interaction `sync_section` | One section re-registration |
| `own` | float ms | `[UNV]` | `assembly.state` writes | Assembly's own work |
| `verifier_release` | float ms | <= 0.05 (INV-1, INV-2, INV-4, INV-5, INV-7; O(seams <= 48 + bodies <= 8 + actors <= 8)) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | Assembly | Release-build verifier subset (MSA-32) |
| `MSA_WELD_APPLY_BUDGET_MS`, `MSA_CUT_APPLY_BUDGET_MS`, `MSA_INSTALL_APPLY_BUDGET_MS` | float ms | 0.5, 0.5, 0.2 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | T9 | Budgets |

**Output Range:** the sum must not exceed its budget, and the whole weld tick (P2 plus P3) stays under `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4, a logging threshold and not a cut-off. **Example (allocation targets, not measurements):** `m2` 0.20 + `lm2` 0.05 + `bm3` 0.05 + `2 x sync` 0.10 + `own` 0.01 + `verifier_release` 0.05 = 0.46 ms <= 0.5. The P5 subscribers (PROJECTOR, Emitter, Lighting, Trail) are outside the apply and have their own budget (`MSA_WELD_FRAME_P5_BUDGET_MS` 8, PROJECTOR `PROJECTOR_WELD_BUDGET_MS` 4 `[UNV]`, no frame above `WELD_FRAME_HITCH_MAX_MS` 33).

### D.16 Fault loss window

The fault_loss_window formula is defined as:

`fault_loss_ticks = T_fault - last_save_T` ; `fault_loss_ticks <= AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS + AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` (10800 + 7200 = 18000 ticks, 5 minutes: the interval autosave is soft-gated, for example while `CHANNELLING` (SL-5), and a soft-blocked interval save may defer up to `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` 7200 before it is written anyway, so this sum is the ceiling); enabling the `ASSEMBLY_WELD` / `ASSEMBLY_CUT` events can only shorten the window, never lengthen it. The name is distinct from Save/Load's registered `loss_ticks` (`T_dead - T_g`, the death-rule loss), which measures a different quantity

Derived: a defect inside an Assembly `apply` moves the kernel `RUNNING -> FAULTED` (CR-9), and the only exit is a player-initiated restore of the last autosave. This is the price of "no rollback" stated as a number so a reviewer can accept or reject it. It is **not** the death rule (SL-7); it shares only the loading path.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` | int ticks | 10800 | Save/Load (cited) | Interval autosave |
| `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS` | int ticks | 1800 | Save/Load (cited) | Event coalescing floor (events can only add saves at least this far apart) |
| `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` | int ticks | 7200 | Save/Load (registry) | Longest wait of a soft-blocked interval save; after it the save is written anyway and tagged RISKY |
| `T_fault`, `last_save_T` | int64 ticks | | kernel; Save/Load | Tick of the defect; tick of the last autosave |

**Output Range:** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the figures are relabelled. 10800 was called "typical" but it is a maximum: a defect lands uniformly inside the autosave interval, so the **mean** loss is about half of it.)* 0 to 18000 ticks (5 minutes of play) at the ceiling when a soft gate defers the interval save to `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS`; **maximum 10800 ticks (3 minutes) and mean about 5400 ticks (1.5 minutes)** when no soft gate defers it; with events enabled the window shrinks toward the event save but is not computed here. **Example:** a defect in A3 of a weld 2 minutes (7200 ticks) after the last interval autosave: the player loses up to 7200 ticks of play, within the 10800 maximum and the 18000 ceiling (*Amended 2026-10-27 — design review:* the soft-gate fixture of AC-MSA-148 deliberately lands a fault at `fault_loss_ticks` = 17999, the largest value the formula allows, so the 18000 path is exercised and not only the 7200 case); the existing save file is never overwritten while FAULTED. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions) (D55): the window, mean about 5400, maximum 10800, ceiling 18000 ticks, is accepted. With the weld and cut event saves shipping in the MVP (D17) the window for a defect after a completed weld or cut shrinks toward the event save (the minimum gap is `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS` 1800), and the RAGGED withhold and soft gate of MSA-33 cannot raise the ceiling because the soft gate is bounded by `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` 7200.)*

### D.17 Outcome cue gate

The outcome_cue_gate formula is defined as:

`cue_plays(p) = ( p == local_seat ) AND ( last_result[p] != NONE ) AND ( ( p , last_result[p].T ) != last_seen ) AND ( T_now - last_result[p].T <= OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS )` ; `last_seen` is initialised on join and on restore to the `(p , last_result[p].T)` found at that moment *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the `last_result[p] != NONE` conjunct is evaluated first, so an empty `last_result` after a restore or a join is never subtracted from; without it the result depended on how GDScript treats the empty record and on evaluation order) (Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: keyed by `(PlayerId, T)`, local seat only, initialised on join and restore; the registry expression `cue_plays = (last_result.T != last_seen_T) AND (T_now - last_result.T <= OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS)` is superseded, see the registry request)*

Derived: presentation consumes `last_result` once. `last_result` is runtime-only (MSA-4), so a restore leaves it empty and never replays a cue; a MIRROR join never plays a stale one **because `last_seen` is initialised to the value found at join** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, not because of any clearing step: with `last_seen` unset the old formula would have played a result that was 30 ticks old at join. The outcome is presented by the verb family's one cue (MSA-34); the DONE outcome has its own completion cues (the bead locks, the burn-through).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `last_result[p].T` | int64 tick | >= 0 | `assembly.state` (runtime-only) | Presenter dedupe key, with `p` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `last_seen` | pair `(PlayerId, int64 tick)` | | presenter-local, not sim state | The last `(p, T)` the presenter acted on; initialised on join and restore *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `local_seat` | PlayerId | | the presenter's own seat binding | Cues play for the local seat only *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `T_now` | int64 tick | | kernel | Current tick |
| `OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS` | int ticks | 12-180, default 60 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | data file | Staleness bound |

**Output Range:** boolean per presenter per `last_result` change. **Example:** a `NOT_TAKEN` is written at `last_result.T` = 437412; the presenter last saw 437000; the frame at 437420 has `T_now - last_result.T` = 8 <= 60: the cue plays once. A frame at 437500 after a hitch: 88 > 60, no cue (a stale cue after a hitch would read as a new failure). After a restore `last_result` is empty and no cue plays: the `!= NONE` conjunct is false and `T_now - last_result[p].T` is never evaluated *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. **Join example** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: a MIRROR client joins at tick 437442 and finds `last_result[1].T` = 437412 (30 ticks old, inside the 60-tick window); `last_seen` is initialised to `(1, 437412)`, so `cue_plays` is false and nothing plays; the next result at 437600 differs and plays once. A result of a crewmate (`p` = 2) never plays a cue on seat 1.

### D.18 Welded-body capacity bounds

The welded_body_capacity formula is defined as:

`C_welded = sum over sections s in body of compartments(s) <= 12` (the Graph's LS-26 bound, read as the welded body, advisor R26) ; `nodes_loaded <= MAX_GRAPH_NODES` ; `edges_loaded + plan_edges <= MAX_GRAPH_EDGES`

Derived: Assembly reads LS-26's "<= 12 compartments at the weld" as the **welded body's** compartment count (advisor R26). Whether LS-26 also bounds the loaded set `{home+B, A}` is the Graph's call (ASK G4). `CAPACITY` is a `can_join_static` reason; at MVP scale it is not at risk.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `compartments(s)` | int | per template | Layout / Authoring | Home 5, derelict A 4, derelict B 5 |
| `MAX_GRAPH_NODES`, `MAX_GRAPH_EDGES` | int | 4096, 8192 | Graph (cited, locked) | Capacity |
| `plan_edges` | int | MVP 3 (1 OPENING + 1 CABLE + 1 DUCT), provisional on the Graph's plan shape | `socket_plan` | Edges the weld adds (nothing else) |

**Output Range:** boolean lint. **Example:** home 5 + B 5 = `C_welded` 10 <= 12; the loaded set including A is 14 (ASK G4). Graph load: about 105 nodes of 4096 and a few hundred edges of 8192 (the Graph's own estimate), plus 3 edges for the weld.

## Edge Cases

Grouped by theme. Each case states the exact outcome and the rule that decides it. "Site 1/2/3" are the three call sites of the one predicate (MSA-7); "the sputter" is the weld family's one non-DONE cue and "the gutter" the cut family's (MSA-34). Ids `E-n` are stable references for the Acceptance Criteria.

### Zero, maximum and out-of-range values

- **E-1. If the data file ships `WELD_Q_DEFAULT` 1000 or 399**: the loader clamps it to 990 or 400 at load, raises `MSA_Q_CLAMPED` once, and every weld uses the clamped value (D.1). Playing on a q-1000 weld (or any q of 993 or more) would delete the weld's weep (the P1 tell) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; playing on a q-399 weld would leak at birth with no tell (a P2 break). The clamp runs at load, never per weld, so no weld can disagree with another.
- **E-2. If `WELD_CHANNEL_TICKS` or `SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS` is not a multiple of `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6, or is outside 240-480**: the data lint fails at boot and the game does not start with that file (IA I-2 and the analogy to I-9). A channel that does not span whole Graph blocks would credit a partial block, and a duration below 240 would leave the LONG bar class.
- **E-3. If `PENDING_TTL_TICKS` is outside 8-119**: `pending_ttl_ok` (D.11) is false and the boot lint fails. Below 8 a legitimate completion held back by the kernel's per-tick truncation would be `STALE`; at 120 or above a completion could outlive a begin's own age window *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.
- **E-4. If a `plan` would take the welded body above 12 compartments or the Graph above `MAX_GRAPH_NODES` 4096 or `MAX_GRAPH_EDGES` 8192**: `can_join_static` returns `CAPACITY` at site 1 and the player gets the standard rejection cue (structural reasons are IMMEDIATE). The MVP welded body is 10 compartments and about 105 of 4096 nodes, so this is an authoring defect in MVP, not a play outcome (D.18).
- **E-5. If a `begin_*` is pressed while `assembly.state.pending.kind != NONE` for that seat**: it is rejected `BUSY` at site 1 and nothing is queued or opened. This BUSY gate is the single guarantee (D.11) that makes it impossible for a new handoff to meet an unresolved `pending`: the completion of the previous channel has either applied or resolved to `NOT_TAKEN` within 13 ticks, and the gate holds on a resumed begin too, whose channel can be as short as 6 ticks (D.7) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.
- **E-6. If the simulation runs with zero seats (a headless test, a menu state)**: `assembly.step` iterates an empty map, submits nothing and costs the idle budget only; `assembly.state` is `default_state()`. No Assembly path assumes a seat exists.

### Simultaneous and ordering cases

- **E-7 `[VS-B]` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. If two seats stand at the two different sockets of the one pair and both reach DONE on the same tick**: `assembly.step` submits two `weld` completions in ascending PlayerId order; the first applies; the second's `validate` fails `SOCKET_NOT_FREE` (both sockets are already `WELDED`), the command is Rejected, `pending` stays set and the second seat's outcome resolves as `NOT_TAKEN` at `submit_T + 13` with the weld sputter. Never a fault. The same socket cannot be contended at all: IA-34 gives the second seat `BUSY` at begin (one job per subject and verb class), so a double DONE needs the two *different* sockets (MSA-11 allows either to be focused); optional ASK I1(x) would close even that.
- **E-8 `[VS-B]` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. If the two seats' DONEs fall on consecutive ticks**: the second seat's handoff pre-check (site 2) sees the sockets `WELDED` and writes `REJECTED(SOCKET_NOT_FREE)`, submitting nothing, with the weld sputter. If the first completion is still queued (backlog), both completions are submitted and the first-queued applies, the other becomes `NOT_TAKEN` as in E-7. The welded state is single in every ordering (AC-MSA-59).
- **E-9. If a weld or cut applies in the same tick as a Graph hook tick (power `T mod 6 = 0`, atmo 2, failure 4)** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*: the apply runs in P2 and the due Graph hook in P3, so the first Graph hook that runs after the apply refreshes the topology (Graph phase 1 refresh at the head of any hook) and sees the bumped `topology_version` and the new seam; no half-state is observable. Assembly neither registers nor orders against the Graph's hooks.
- **E-10. If an earlier command in the same P2 queue changes the target (a crewmate's salvage completion removes `j_b_main`)**: P2 order is `(queued_tick, host_order)`, so the earlier command applies first; the weld's `validate` then sees the new state, and the full `can_join` in step A0 reads `PORT_ABSENT`. The result is `BLOCKED(PORT_ABSENT)`: both orderings are legal and deterministic.
- **E-11. If a breach opens in either section during the weld channel**: the channel does not abort (the abort set is closed, IA-15) and the weld still applies; the new seam opens with hatch area 0, so nothing crosses until the player opens the hatch, and the full `can_join` at apply step A0 reports the larger `surge[]` (no Assembly presenter reads it; the creak is the Graph's `SEAL_STRAIN` descriptor, VA-16), never a number. The player who wants to patch first simply moves: the ledger holds the progress for `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` 600.
- **E-12 `[VS-B]` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. If a crewmate stands on the absorbed (derelict B) side in an unrelated channel when a weld applies** (the single-player form is the welder's own case, E-56): BM3 transfers every actor on the absorbed body, which counts as a body transfer, so the Controller clears their `channel_ref` and Interaction aborts their channel with cause `BODY`, crediting any finished blocks (IA-14 row 1). No special case in Assembly; the welder on B sees no displacement when `docked` held.
- **E-13. If a crewmate (or any other actor, in the MVP a Body double) steps into a hatch rectangle during a seam-cut channel** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (`[VS-B]` as co-op play; the double-based form gates the MVP, AC-MSA-36): the channel continues (a hazard is not an abort cause) and, at DONE, site 2 finds `actors_overlapping` non-empty and writes `REJECTED(CUT_BLOCKED)` with the gutter; the `LOUD` already went out at DONE, the seam stays welded and nothing is submitted. If the crewmate enters in the single tick between the handoff (P3 of `T_done`) and the completion (P2 of `T_apply`), site 3 rejects instead and the seat resolves as `NOT_TAKEN` 13 ticks after submission, with the same cue. "The ship will not close a seam on a crewmate" holds at all three sites.
- **E-14. If the cutter's own foot box overlaps a hatch rectangle**: the cut prompt is still offered (`seam_cut_offer` has no `CUT_BLOCKED` term, D.2) and begin is rejected `CUT_BLOCKED` with the standard rejection cue; stepping back out of the rectangle restores a valid begin. This is why Layout and Authoring must guarantee a legal stance at least one foot-box half-extent outside both rectangles within `REACH_PX` 72 (ASK L2, AC-MSA-37); without it the cut would be permanently self-blocked.

### Target and state changes during the channel

- **E-15. If the target lane node is salvaged before or during the weld channel (`j_main` or `j_b_main`, both def-backed; the bare `dj_*` nodes cannot be salvaged, DA-19)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: in MVP content this is meant to be unreachable, because ASK D2 authors the seam-lane node modules non-salvageable (MSA-8); the case stays specified for the Vertical Slice, for a defect and for the test fixture of AC-MSA-11: begin and the handoff pass (module-body state is never read there), the completion `validate` passes, and step A0 reads `PORT_ABSENT`: `last_result = BLOCKED(PORT_ABSENT)`, nothing else mutates, no `weld_join` call, the weld sputters. The cost is the six seconds; the world witness is the empty bay footprint at the lane node (LY-20), reachable within one walk.
- **E-16. If the weld is attempted onto a vacuum, frozen or dead section**: the weld is allowed and moves nothing (fresh seam: hatch area 0, valves closed, breaker open). The closed hatch creaks (`SEAL_STRAIN`) or frosts at the rim; the player decides when to open it. The weld is never refused for a hazard, only for structure (P1: the hazard is told by the hatch, not the prompt).
- **E-17. If `docked` stops holding during the 360-tick channel**: with Web Map & Transit's hold (ASK T1) this cannot happen. Without it, the handoff pre-check (site 2) re-tests `docked` and writes `REJECTED(NOT_DOCKED)` with the sputter, costing the six seconds. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the camera-cut fallback is deleted, Body's request accepted; the offer never drops the `docked` term.)* In the residual window between the site-2 re-test and the apply, a loss of `docked` still lets the weld apply (site 3 reads no pose) and the absorbed hull jumps by `delta_abs` in the exterior layer; Transit's hold (WM-22) makes that window unreachable, and the camera cut does not hide it (BDY-27). Site 3 deliberately does not re-test `docked` (BDY-27: weld legality takes no pose input).
- **E-18. If the target section is dormant (`SECTION_NOT_LOADED`)**: the prompt may still show (the offer reads no join structure) and begin is rejected with the standard rejection cue; no channel opens and no time is spent. Dormancy is a section flag, not module-body state, so it is IMMEDIATE.
- **E-19. If a weld is attempted while the seat carries a module**: no weld prompt exists (the carry lockout, PC-7 / IA-28, is Interaction's selector) and a forced `begin_weld` is `BUSY`. The player puts the module down or installs it; a torch needs two hands.
- **E-20. If the seam hatch is opened during a cancelled-and-resumed cut**: the ledger keeps the progress, but the mode is re-derived at completion, so the resumed cut is RAGGED (D.6). The open leaf and the draught are the witness; the player who wants a clean cut closes the hatch first.
- **E-21. If a RAGGED cut applies**: the Graph creates `A_CUT` 24 holes on both sides (including the home side), hull stubs `hp` 2500 and `open_end` ports; `sync_section` must create `stubs[]` entries for every uncapped `open_end` or the O2-lane port hiss could never be capped *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, which would break P2 fairness (ASK I1(vii)). The cut is EXOGENOUS and player-caused, so it is exempt from `W_MIN_WARNING_S`; its tell was the world witnesses before the press. A 20-tile keep-side room falls to the DECOMP band in 382 steps (38 s) and to VACUUM in 960 steps (96 s) (LS-16, D.1) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. Capping a stub (V8's follow-up `begin_cap`, M12) stops that port's hiss only; it does not touch the hull hole. The hull hole has no subject in the MVP, so the player's answers are to leave and shut the door behind them (a closed OPENING isolates the room and the rest of the ship stops venting) or to accept the loss of the room until Vertical Slice content closes the hole.
- **E-22 `[VS-B]` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. If a keep-side cut leaves a crewmate on the leaving piece**: they are stranded on the detached body, which drifts without rotation. Counterplay (MSA-18): the 4 s lit, loud channel is the tell, and a crewmate who opens the hatch from the far side and stands in the passage before completion makes the cut `MSA.CUT_BLOCKED` (AC-MSA-123); a crewmate who only opens the hatch makes the cut RAGGED. If no one is in the passage the cut proceeds and there is no blanket far-side gate in MVP and Vertical Slice A (Q4: "death is a bitch; it happens"; in solo play a gate is vacuous). Recovery is Web Map & Transit's (ASK T1, ASK T2). Vertical Slice B gates it as `CUT_STRANDS` by default (user ruling D53): a keep-side cut with another seat in the leaving component is rejected `MSA.CUT_STRANDS` at handoff and completion unless the cutter's `harm_eligible` bit is set (MSA-18, Open Question 2) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.
- **E-53. If derelict B is welded into the home body** *(added after E-52 and kept last in this group; id kept stable for AC-MSA-122)*: the Graph's section record keeps `kind` DERELICT and so `rate_pm` 2000 (LS-17, LS-25: the rate is keyed on `kind`, and `weld_join`, LS-15, writes no section field). Endogenous wear and overload on B's nodes and edges therefore run at twice the home-ship rate after the weld. Every endogenous transition still passes the `W_MIN_WARNING_S` gate, so the tell is kept. Assembly writes no section field and adds no rule (MSA-2). Whether a graft keeps derelict compression is the Graph's call (ASK G7). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **G7 is closed and 2x graft wear is confirmed as intended.** Graph LS-15 and LS-17 state that a welded section keeps its `kind` and `rate_pm` and that "a cut restores nothing"; Assembly keeps that: the graft is the ship's weak point, which is the motive to shed a worn graft (Player Fantasy, the cut anchor). **Observable in a session:** the Graph's own D.15 table gives, for a DERELICT section at `rate_pm` 2000, FUEL-DRIP at 5.3 minutes and HULL-FATIGUE at 6.2 minutes (against 10.5 and 12.5 at home), so an endogenous event on a grafted section is observable well inside a 30 to 60 minute session; AC-MSA-132 asserts the ratio on the Graph's fixtures.)*

- **E-54. If the welder is swapped for another tool, or the seat is staggered or DOWNED, during a weld or cut channel** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; new)*: staggering and DOWNED end the channel with abort cause `BODY` (IA-15 lists "stagger, downed, body transfer, seat rebind" under `BODY`) and Interaction credits any finished blocks to the ledger; a tool swap makes `weld_offer` or `seam_cut_offer` false, so IA-14 row 6 aborts the channel with `NOT_ELIGIBLE` within `LIT_RECHECK_TICKS` 6 ticks (the `eligible` re-test of ASK I1(viii), answered). If neither fires before DONE (the swap lands in the last block), the handoff pre-check (site 2) finds `NO_TOOL` and writes `REJECTED(NO_TOOL)` with the family cue and submits nothing. A DOWNED seat never reaches DONE. No `pending`, no mutation. AC-MSA-135.
- **E-55 `[VS-B]`. If a seat disconnects or is unbound while its `pending` is set** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; new)*: the SYSTEM completion is already in the kernel queue and is a fact of the world, not of the seat, so it applies as normal if it is still valid (the actor field names the PlayerId that began it; Body's actor for that seat may be despawned, in which case BM3 transfers the remaining actors and the welder is not among them); the seat's record in `assembly.state` is removed with the seat after the TTL resolves, and `last_result` is dropped with it (nobody presents it). A seat that is rebound to a new peer keeps the PlayerId (never the transport peer id, MSA-4), so its `pending` and the cue gate continue. No fault, no orphan record. AC-MSA-136.
- **E-56. If the welder stands on the derelict side (the absorbed body) when the weld applies** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; new)*: the welder is on B for the channel (either socket may be focused, MSA-11). The channel record is already consumed at DONE and the Controller's `channel_ref` cleared (IA-14 row 9) before the completion applies, so BM3 transferring the welder to the surviving body is not an abort (E-12 is the case of a crewmate whose own channel is still running); the welder's `abs` is conserved and, with `docked` holding, nothing moves on screen; if the Pipeline's `BODY_TRANSFER` camera cut fires (fallback only) it is hidden by the DONE bar's 12-tick hold. `last_result = DONE` is written for the welder's seat as usual. AC-MSA-137.
- **E-57. If a cut detaches the pod, or sections that hold loose items** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; new)*: **the pod cannot be detached by a cut**: it is its own persistent Layout body (WM-5), is never welded, and a cut splits only the welded component into two (LY-14, forest). **Items and fixtures in the leaving component stay in their section records** and travel with the piece (section-local positions are unchanged); the piece becomes a dormant orphan (WM-23) and its contents are unreachable in MVP but are neither destroyed nor duplicated (conservation, AC-MSA-111), and carried items stay with their carrier. The extraction line (strip by V8 salvage before the cut) is how a player keeps what the piece holds. The piece with an avatar on it is the keep-side rule's job to prevent (MSA-18). AC-MSA-138.

### Degenerate strategies

- **E-23. If the player cancels and restarts a channel repeatedly to "peek" at whether it would work**: the offer is state-independent for module-body state, so the peek reveals nothing; each start costs TOOL noise per accrued tick, up to `B - 1` = 5 ticks of each cancelled block are wasted (D.7), and the ledger lets the work resume only inside `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` 600. There is no free information and no free progress.
- **E-24. If the player welds, strips derelict B by V8 salvage, then cuts it away**: this is an intended healthy line (the "extraction" fantasy). Each salvage costs noise and leaves open ends to cap (Interaction's), each module is salvage-once (IDB-17), the cut is `LOUD`, and nothing is minted (conservation).
- **E-25. If the player tries to weld, cut and weld again to reset a damaged seam or farm a section**: the second weld returns `SOCKET_NOT_FREE` with no prompt; sockets go `FREE -> WELDED -> CAPPED | CUT_OPEN` and never back (LY-13). The playtest gate must not require a second weld (AC-MSA-41).
- **E-26. If the player cuts a section holding a hostile "to kill it"**: a RAGGED cut breaches both sides including the home side, so it is self-harm; there is no force or knockback (BDY-28). A cut removes only a threat the player is leaving behind. Shedding an infested section after the seam has been prepared is healthy P5 play, and a cut made under attack is RAGGED and breaches home, so it is not an escape *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: "escape" removed, the cut is a prepared amputation)*, with the Attention owner's rule that a cut moves the locus and does not reset the meter (ASK A1).
- **E-27. If the player stands on the derelict side of a welded seam with the cutter**: no cut prompt exists, because the seat's foot-origin section is not in the component that keeps its body (`seam_cut_offer`, D.2). The self-strand trap (alone on a drifting, sealed body with no free socket and no recovery path in MVP) is unreachable from a prompt; a forced `begin_seam_cut` is rejected `NOT_ELIGIBLE`.
- **E-28. If the player presses the install prompt repeatedly on a bay**: each press on an occupied slot resolves `BLOCKED(SLOT_OCCUPIED)` with no time cost, so a row of bays can be scanned by pressing. The residual one-bit oracle is accepted (Q6: no lockout; the carry to the bay is already the price; revisit at playtest, Open Questions). The opposite direction (a bay that looks occupied but is empty) installs the module irreversibly, so the probe is asymmetric but not a faucet.

### Save, restore, pause and faults

- **E-29. If a restore (load) happens mid-channel**: `interaction.state.post_restore` drops the channel (abort cause `RESTORE`); `assembly.state` has nothing to clear, because the stored `pending` is NONE (MSA-4) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; `last_result` is runtime-only and so is empty by construction *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; no orphan `pending`, no cue, no half-bead. The world is as of the save.
- **E-30. If a snapshot is taken while `pending` is set** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: that window is not one tick. It runs from the handoff (P3 of `T`, `submit_T`) to the apply (P2 of `T+1`, up to `T+8` under queue truncation) or, when the completion is rejected or dropped, to `submit_T + PENDING_TTL_TICKS + 1` = 13 ticks (E-33). The live `pending` is set while the channel record is already consumed, and the target may already be `WELDED` if a rival completion applied first (INV-10 accepts both on live state, T7); the snapshot stores `pending` as NONE and holds no queued completion, so the restore loses the weld or cut, no weld happened, and the player redoes the six seconds. Restore-then-continue is equal to uninterrupted play wherever MSA-38 says it is (no open ASSEMBLY channel record and no `pending`); the window is the **declared non-snapshot ticks** of MSA-4 (the one asserted difference, PD-1, is a dropped channel record, E-29), asserted explicitly by AC-MSA-61, and **AC-MSA-129 saves at every tick of the window and requires a clean restore** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. ASK S1(a) (`pending_any()`) closes it.
- **E-31. If an autosave comes due during a self-inflicted RAGGED decompression**: it is not written *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the earlier "it may be written" is superseded by the withhold plus soft-gate rule)*. SL-5's soft row (a) counts only ENDOGENOUS descriptors and a RAGGED cut's stubs are EXOGENOUS (LS-16), so the Graph never defers it; the rule is Assembly's `ragged_breach_open()` (the keep-side cut room still venting - hull hole open and `band_p` not VACUUM - or a keep-side port uncapped within `RAGGED_WITHHOLD_MAX_TICKS` 2400 of `cut_at_T`) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, which Save/Load treats as a soft-gate row for the interval save (SL-5 soft row (d)) and as a withhold in `event_due` for the event save (MSA-33, D.9, ASK S1(b)); the hard DOWNED/DEAD gate also applies. A Save & Quit inside the window is written RISKY (cause `ASSEMBLY_BREACH`). After the window the next save is taken normally. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* Listed exposure bound: a keep-side room open to a larger connected volume can keep the read true past `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` 7200 (V_total 120: 34 542 ticks); the interval save then fires RISKY with cause `ASSEMBLY_BREACH` (SL-5 row (d)); the event save stays latched until the room has vented. This is the intended price of venting the ship, not a defect.
- **E-32. If the game is paused between the DONE tick and the apply**: nothing advances (no P2, no P3, no hook submits; queued commands wait for the first RUNNING tick, CR-16), the TTL and the ledger are in ticks and stop; resume continues as if there had been no pause.
- **E-33. If the completion is rejected at admission (`QUEUE_FULL`) or in P2** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: `pending` stays set (and the INV-10 assertion of the debug build stays silent, because the target is allowed to be `WELDED` or `CUT` in this window, T7), no mutation happened, and `assembly.step` writes `NOT_TAKEN` at `submit_T + PENDING_TTL_TICKS + 1`, 13 ticks after submission (superseding the "NOT_TAKEN at T+1" of the earlier proposals); the player gets the family's cue, the world is unchanged and they re-hold E. The queue depth is far above any MVP traffic, so this is a defence.
- **E-34. If a completion is held in the queue for k ticks, 1 <= k <= 8 (per-tick truncation)**: `pending` stays until the apply clears it; `DONE` is written exactly once and `NOT_TAKEN` never, because the kernel's `STALE` bound and the TTL coincide (D.11).
- **E-35. If a mutator called by an Assembly `apply` returns a `FAULT_CODE`, a release postcondition fails (`MSA_SHIFT_DRIFT`, `MSA_VERDICT_DRIFT`), or the release verifier reports INV-1, INV-2, INV-4, INV-5 or INV-7 after an apply** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: GDScript has no throw, so the old wording "makes an apply throw" is withdrawn (MSA-31); the apply calls `ctx.fault(code, detail)` (kernel CR-19) on the first non-`OK` status and returns `ctx.complete()`, and the kernel moves to FAULTED, the faulting command is `Faulted(code)`, the rest of the queue drops with `Dropped(FAULTED)`, no autosave is written over an existing save, and the only exit is a player-initiated restore of the last autosave; the loss window (`fault_loss_ticks`) is about 5400 ticks (1.5 minutes) on average, at most 10800 (3 minutes) when no soft gate defers the interval save, and at most 18000 (5 minutes) at the ceiling (`AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` + `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS`, D.16; "typical" relabelled mean and maximum). This is not Save/Load's death rule (SL-7).
- **E-36. If a restore loads a save in which `welds[]` has an `ACTIVE` row but the Graph has no matching SeamRecord**: INV-1 fails in `assembly.state`'s `post_restore`, the kernel goes LOADING -> FAULTED naming `assembly.state` and INV-1; the save file is untouched; the game never plays on the inconsistent world.

### Hallucination interplay (MVP class (b); classes (a), (c), (d) are Vertical Slice and the rules are written now)

- **E-37. If the seam valve renders CLOSED while it is truly OPEN**: the cut is RAGGED, because the mode is derived from true positions. **Under the MSA-23 fallback this render cannot happen at the seam**: the seam's own valve and breaker sprites are TRUTH and the Filter may not wrap them until the Graph and Failure & Trail confirm a zero-gradient lane witness; the case stays specified so the derivation is proven independent of perception (a Perception stub that wraps the sprite anyway, AC-MSA-104, is a Filter defect caught by AC-MSA-126). Once the fallback lifts, the lie is falsifiable beforehand by the witness at the lane (the hiss under a gradient, the zero-gradient lane witness the owners confirmed); a player who looked got a clean cut or a decision, and one who did not took a breach that had a witness (MSA-23).
- **E-38. If the hardpoint renders EMPTY while a module is seated**: the install prompt still appears, because it depends only on what the player carries; the press resolves `BLOCKED(SLOT_OCCUPIED)` with the install bump and the module stays in the hands. The bay's footprint (truth channel) shows the occupant on a closer look. The cost is a walk at carry gait, not a free oracle.
- **E-39. If the hardpoint renders OCCUPIED while it is truly empty**: the prompt appears, the install succeeds and the module is consumed in a bay the player meant to avoid (irreversible: no `remove_module`). Falsify beforehand with the bay footprint.
- **E-40. If a hallucinated "welded" seam is drawn over a socket pair that is truly `FREE`** (Perception Filter, VS): the weld offer and every verdict read true Layout state, the geometry of the real seam is never wrapped, and the prompt `E . Weld` appearing over a "welded" seam is itself the contradiction that falsifies the lie. The Filter may not suppress or fake the prompt (ASK F1).
- **E-41. If a crewmate in the hatch renders as a horror (class (c), VS-B)**: `CUT_BLOCKED` reads true actor presence, not perceived identity, so a deluded player cannot seal a crewmate into a cap; the kept oracle costs one attempt and no time and the Filter must never wrap it.
- **E-42. If the module the player carried home is a phantom (class (d), VS-A)**: the install offer is computed from the true carried def, so no prompt appears at a bay it does not fit; the trip home is one fallback reveal and the cheaper field checks are other owners'.

### Co-op, MIRROR, zoom and engine-facing cases

- **E-43 `[VS-B]` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. If a MIRROR client joins while a `last_result` is pending presentation**: `last_result` arrives through `SHARED_SUMMARY`; the presenter acts only if `last_result[p]` is not empty, `(PlayerId, last_result.T)` differs from its `last_seen` (initialised to the value found at the join) and the result lies within `OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS` 60 of now (D.17), so a join never replays a stale cue *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. `assembly.step` does not run on a MIRROR (no authoritative tick); the crewmate's weld in progress is visible through Interaction's replicated channel record (the human channel, P1).
- **E-44 `[VS-B]` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. If a crewmate stands in the hatch to veto a cut (griefing, VS-B)**: the cut is rejected `CUT_BLOCKED` and nothing else happens; the cost to the griefer is standing in a doorway. Recorded for Vertical Slice B; the ship never closes a seam on anyone.
- **E-45. If the player zooms in or out on the integer ladder during a channel**: nothing in the rules depends on zoom; the in-world cursor and bead scale with the world viewport, the prompt plate and linear bar keep their HUD pixel size, offsets stay on the 24-px grid and `abs` is unchanged (R28). Tests run at `k` = 1 and `k_auto + 2` (AC-MSA-64).
- **E-46. If several ticks run in one render frame (catch-up, `MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME` 4) across a weld** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (the cap is only true if the tick-driver ADR and the boot assertion of T13 hold, AC-MSA-141): the apply runs in its own tick; the P5 subscribers (PROJECTOR, Emitter, Lighting, Trail) coalesce to the latest committed state and never replay each tick's change, so the weld frame costs one rebuild.
- **E-47. If the weld happens on a moving body (`vel` up to `VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK` 16)**: `docked` is relative (equal velocity and the offset), BM3 conserves each actor's `abs`, the survivor's velocity is unchanged and no force is applied (BDY-28). Moving bodies are exercised only by the spike (AC-MSA-72); MVP play welds a docked pair.
- **E-48. If a weld is followed later by a cut of the same seam**: `pos_curr` is not restored to its pre-weld value (the cut subtracts `frame_shift_px` and zeroes the sub-pixel part); `abs` and section-local position are conserved, which is what tests assert (BDY-23).
- **E-49. If `sync_section` returns with `IA_SUBJECT_OVERFLOW` counted on the weld tick**: the weld stays applied (the call is total and fails dark by design); the defect counter rises and the content lint is the fix. For a seam door this reads as a hallucinated dead door, which is why the lint, not the runtime, is the defence (MSA-16, D.14).
- **E-50. If `assembly.step` and `interaction.step` are registered in the wrong order or one is unknown**: boot fails (CR-3: an unknown hook id or a cycle) rather than reading the DONE handoff a tick late; the `after` edge is covered by a test and printable by the Debug Overlay.
- **E-51. If a glowstick (Chemlight) lights the socket instead of the lamp**: whether it satisfies `lit` for the channel is the Lighting and Interaction owners' rule; if it does, the weld proceeds with the lamp pointed elsewhere, a legitimate "weld by glowstick" line that needs no Assembly special case. The sparks are non-emissive and never the light source.
- **E-52. If the detached piece after a cut holds no actor**: it persists as an orphan Layout body with a Body pose row (Body BDY-4); Assembly neither unloads nor discards it (Web Map & Transit's, MSA-24). It counts toward `MAX_BODIES`, which is why D.8 includes it.

## Dependencies

Dependencies are bidirectional: every row below names what the counterparty must repeat in its own GDD, and the CONFLICT and ASK rows are refreshes owed to neighbour owners. This GDD never edits a neighbour; each row quotes the locked text and proposes the replacement. Status values: **designed** (file carries "Designed"), **designed, asks open**, **undesigned-provisional** (no file; the contract is stated here and the named GDD must repeat it).

**Index edge check.** The index lists seven dependencies for this system (kernel, Graph, Item DB, Interaction, Layout, Body, Save/Load). This GDD adds soft edges the index lacks: Player Controller & Input (PC-7, PC-8), Suit & Equipment (`release_carried`), Attention (no push; a conflict row), Derelict Authoring (content), and a Hard edge for the *offer* only to Web Map & Transit (`docked`). A refresh is owed to the index owner (the index is not edited here).

**T13 — Dependency table.**

| System | Direction | Strength | Interface | Status | Must-repeat by the counterparty |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Command & Simulation Layer | Assembly depends on | **Hard** | CR-3 hook, CR-4/CR-5 command and reasons, CR-8 block, CR-9 atomic apply and failure model, CR-10 batch | designed | kernel row 5 (the stress test); wording refresh C9 |

| AX1 *(new, same)* | Player Controller & Input, Interaction, HUD | a **toggle-hold** setting: a hold verb (the 6 s weld, the 4 s cut, and the veto's hatch hold) can be started and ended by a press instead of held, and every audio-first witness has a TRUTH visual equivalent (UI Requirements, AC-MSA-133 and 134); `design/accessibility-requirements.md` does not exist yet and must be created before the weld story (Default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20): X10, tier standard; the toggle-hold is Assembly's two-tap alternative; the file is authored with `/ux-design accessibility`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | the weld and cut are inaccessible to players who cannot hold a key for 6 seconds |

**What the dependency sets do not do.** There is no circular hard dependency: the Graph is pure data and Assembly mutates it only through the command layer (index soft cycle "Life-Support Graph <-> Modular Ship Assembly"), Layout and Body read nothing from Assembly, and Interaction reads one Assembly view (`weld_offer`, `seam_cut_offer`) injected as a lookup while Assembly calls Interaction's mutators, which is a mutual reference at data level but acyclic by restore and hook order (`assembly.step` after `interaction.step`).

## Tuning Knobs

Every number below is data-driven (a data file, never hardcoded). Assembly owns few knobs on purpose: the Graph owns what a weld does to the world, Interaction what the channel costs the web, Layout where sockets sit. **★ marks a registry candidate** (a cross-system fact another GDD reads or must obey); every new name is `[PLACEHOLDER]` until the playtest gate or the spike sets it. Knobs owned elsewhere are in T18 with a pointer to their source of truth and are never restated with a new value (a different value is a CONFLICT, not a tweak here). Tick values are 60 per second; ticks that must span whole Graph blocks are multiples of `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6.

**T17 — Assembly-owned knobs.**

| Knob | Default | Unit | Safe range | Too high | Too low | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WELD_CHANNEL_TICKS` ★ | 360 (6 s) | ticks, multiple of 6 | 240-480 | over 8 s: standing lit and still becomes tedium and a punishment, breaks the IA I-9 analogy (a LONG bar is at most 2 x `BAR_LONG_FROM_TICKS`) | under 240: leaves the LONG bar class; the weld becomes a tap and loses its P3 exposure | Interaction's TOOL noise per accrued tick (`U_TOOL`, Attention units; `NOISE_PER_TICK` is the Controller's walk/run/carry table and has no TOOL entry), `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` 600, D.7, D.11 (no channel-floor term any more; the BUSY gate is the guarantee) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS` ★ | 240 (4 s) | ticks, multiple of 6 | 240-480 | over 8 s: a prepared amputation becomes tedious and the P5 shed-a-graft verb stops being worth its noise *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: "panicked escape cut" and "P5 exit verb" deleted, the cut is not an escape verb)* | under 240: leaves the LONG class; the destructive verb becomes cheaper than the careful one | one `LOUD` at DONE (Interaction), D.7; shares 240 with `SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS` by coincidence, a distinct name; deliberately faster than the weld, revisit if players use the cut as a free panic button |
| `WELD_Q_DEFAULT` ★ | 850 | permille | `Q_MIN` to 990, stated once in D.1 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: 400 to 990 at the default `LEAK_HP` 4000)* (clamped at load to `[Q_MIN, 990]`, `MSA_Q_CLAMPED`; the effective floor is `Q_MIN` = `max(400, ceil(LEAK_HP / 10))` of D.1, so it rises to 600 at `LEAK_HP` 6000 and the data lint keeps 400 as the outer floor at `LEAK_HP` 2000) | 993 or more: `A_seam_m` 0 from birth, no weep, the weld's P1 tell vanishes (upper cliff; D.1 clamps to 990) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | below 400: `hp_seam` under `LEAK_HP`, every weld leaks at birth with no tell (lower cliff) | Graph `seam_at_weld`, `A_SEAM_MAX`, `LEAK_HP`, `SEAM_LEAK_INITIAL_BP`; never displayed; no modifier slot |
| `PENDING_TTL_TICKS` ★ | 12 | ticks | 8-119 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | up to 119: the cue for a dropped completion arrives late; at 120 or more a completion could outlive a begin's own age window | under 8: violates the kernel's never-stale boot invariant; a legitimate completion could be `STALE`, giving a false `NOT_TAKEN` followed by a late `DONE` | `QUEUE_CAPACITY`, `MAX_CMDS_PER_TICK`, `MAX_AGE_TICKS_ACTION`, D.11 `pending_ttl_ok` |
| `RAGGED_WITHHOLD_MAX_TICKS` ★ *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review; Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | 2400 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | ticks | 2400-7200 (ceiling on the PORT clause only; at most `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` 7200; no boot lint for decompression, which is state-driven) | over 7200: an event save is held longer than the interval save is willing to defer | under the time to cap every keep-side stub (n x `CAP_CHANNEL_TICKS` 90 + walking): a save lands while the player is still capping | `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS`, `CAP_CHANNEL_TICKS`, LS-16, D.9 |
| `OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS` ★ | 60 | ticks | 12-180 | a stale failure cue plays after a hitch and reads as a new failure | slow presenters miss the cue | `last_result.T`, D.17, Adaptive Audio and HUD presenters |
| `CASCADE_CUT_DEFER_MAX_TICKS` ★ `[VS]` | 600 | ticks, multiple of 6 | 60-1800 | a seam condemned for too long while a crewmate stands in the hatch | abandons before a crewmate can clear the hatch | `W_MIN_WARNING_S` (independent), the 10-30 s tell window, D.10; unused in MVP (stub submitter only) |
| `SOCKET_STANCE_CLEARANCE_PX` ★ (asked of Layout and Authoring) | 8 (one foot-box half-extent) | px | 8-24 | the stance band between the hatch rectangles and `REACH_PX` 72 may be empty, so the cut is unreachable | under 8: the cutter's own foot box can overlap a hatch rectangle and block their own cut at all three sites | `FOOT_BOX_W` 16, `REACH_PX` 72, `WELD_SOCKET` anchor placement, lint `SOCKET_STANCE_BAND_EMPTY`, AC-MSA-37 |
| `VETO_REACTION_TICKS` `[VS-B]` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | 90 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | ticks | 30-180 | the veto radius `R_veto` shrinks toward zero (at 210 ticks and a 30-tick door tap, 0 px), so the counterplay of MSA-18 is unusable | under 30: `R_veto` overstates what a startled player can do, and the Vertical Slice B tuning of the ruled `CUT_STRANDS` gate is made on a flattering number *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | `SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS`, `V_WALK`, Interaction's door-tap time, `R_veto = (D - reaction_ticks - door_open_ticks) x V_WALK` (MSA-18); a manual-AC parameter of AC-MSA-123 |
| `CUT_WITNESS_LOOKBACK_S` | 60 | s | 15-180 | the manual AC asks players to remember a seam they looked at too long ago | the AC becomes a reflex test | AC-MSA-39 only; a manual-AC parameter, not a sim knob, unrelated to `OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS` |
| `VS_WELD_CYCLES_MIN` | 3 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | weld cycles | 2-6 | the Vertical Slice content bill grows with sockets (Layout's three options) | 1 is the MVP economy and fails the gate; the P5 loop stays unrepeatable | AC-MSA-125 only; a Vertical Slice gate parameter, not a sim knob; Layout's `SOCKET_ECONOMY_LOW` lint (CD-GDD-ALIGN) |
| `MSA_WELD_APPLY_BUDGET_MS` | 0.5 | ms (Release) | 0.2-2.0 | hides regressions | spike fails on noise | Graph M2 <= 0.2 locked and inside it; D.15 |
| `MSA_CUT_APPLY_BUDGET_MS` | 0.5 | ms (Release) | 0.2-2.0 | hides regressions | spike fails on noise | adds mode derivation and stub creation; D.15 |
| `MSA_INSTALL_APPLY_BUDGET_MS` | 0.2 | ms (Release) | 0.1-1.0 | hides regressions | spike fails on noise | one node, one subject; D.15 |
| `MSA_STEP_IDLE_BUDGET_MS` | 0.02 | ms (Release) | 0.005-0.1 | `assembly.step` runs 60 times a second, so 0.02 ms costs 1.2 ms per second of play | false failures | seat count 0-4 |
| `MSA_WELD_FRAME_P5_BUDGET_MS` | 8 | ms | 4-16 | the camera cut hides at most a one-to-two-frame hitch | spike fails on the first rebuild | PROJECTOR, Emitter, Lighting, Trail rebuilds (D.15) |
| `PROJECTOR_WELD_BUDGET_MS` `[UNV]` | 4 | ms | 1-8 | hitch visible | spike fails | Layout ADR-LAY-03 (PROJECTOR shape); Pipeline C2 / C5 camera cut |
| `WELD_FRAME_HITCH_MAX_MS` | 33 | ms | 17-50 | the seam visibly stutters | none relevant | Pipeline camera cut at `BODY_TRANSFER` |
| `MSA_WELD_OFFER_BUDGET_US` | 5 `[UNV]`, frozen before the measurement *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | microseconds per call | 2-50 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | the hot-path read, evaluated every tick for a focused socket, becomes a frame cost | false failures | cached static half, O(1) `docked`; AC-MSA-17 |

*Not tabled, by decision.* `Q_MIN` is derived from `LEAK_HP` (D.1), not a knob. `MSA_COMPLETION_MAX_AGE_TICKS` 2 is rejected (it violates the kernel's `max_age_ticks >= 8` boot invariant). `INSTALL_BLOCKED_LOCKOUT_TICKS` is not adopted (Q6; the residual oracle is an Open Question). The keep-side cut rule and the hatch-cap rule are **rules** (MSA-18, MSA-21), not toggles: a switch that re-enables the self-strand trap is not a tuning choice. There is no `q` modifier, no weld-skill knob and no tool-tier knob (anti-pillar).

**Interactions worth tuning together.**

- `WELD_CHANNEL_TICKS` x Interaction's TOOL units is the P3 price of the first graft: lengthening the channel raises Attention exposure linearly while a movement cancel wastes at most 5 ticks (D.7), so the knob has no cliff, only a slope. Tune it against the playtest signal "the player hesitates, then commits", not against a time target.
- `PENDING_TTL_TICKS` must be re-checked (D.11) whenever `MAX_AGE_TICKS_ACTION`, `QUEUE_CAPACITY`, `MAX_CMDS_PER_TICK` or either channel duration is retuned.
- `WELD_Q_DEFAULT` stays fixed in MVP; its range exists so the Graph's q-dependent paths can be tested at 400 and 990, not so that play varies it.
- `BODIES_LIVE_MAX` and `MAX_BODIES` bound D.8; any change to the socket economy (Layout Open Question 1) re-opens the zero margin.

**Playtest targets (not knobs).** Median time from the weld prompt first appearing to the channel beginning at most 45 s; median time from the bead to the first seam-breaker close 60-180 s; pooled 17 of 20 judgements (5 players x 4 prompts) correct when asked beforehand whether a cut will be clean (the pooled rule of Validation targets, which supersedes the earlier "4 of 5 players" *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*); both the weld line and the strip line taken by at least 2 of 5 players. All `[PLACEHOLDER]`, owned by the playtest script, and listed again in the Acceptance Criteria.

## Visual/Audio Requirements

*Source: the art-director's proposal (VA-1 to VA-28), reconciled with MSA-1 to MSA-42 and the checkpoint rulings C-P1 to C-P20. ART = `design/art/art-bible.md`; IA = `interaction-system.md`; FT = `failure-and-trail-system.md`; LY = `ship-section-and-layout-data-model.md`; BDY = `ship-body-and-interior-physics.md`; PIPE = `pixel-perfect-render-pipeline-camera.md`; LSG = `life-support-graph.md`. Priority: **P** = MVP, **V** = Vertical Slice, **A** = Alpha. `[UNV]` = unverified number, measured in the spike. Audio columns state existence and sync only; layers, ducking, fades and triggers belong to the audio-director and Adaptive Audio & Tells (designed, `adaptive-audio-and-tells.md`; contracts refreshed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*).*

### Scope: what Assembly's art owns and what it cites

Guard-rail 1 of the rulings applies to art as much as to rules. Assembly owns *which existing piece appears on which transition*, the three outcome cues, the install seat, the in-world socket cursor and the screenshot list. Everything else is cited and never redefined here.

| Visual | Owner (cited, not redefined) | Assembly's share |
|---|---|---|
| Seam bead geometry, fresh and old, straight, corner and T | Layout presentation (LY-15, LY-33); ART 6.1, 8.2 (4-px Hull Grey) | which transition shows which state; the ART 4.4 sampling test |
| Hatch plate (`FREE`), door jambs and leaf (`WELDED`), cap plate (`CAPPED`), ragged piece (`CUT_OPEN`) | Layout (LY-15; no per-state tilesets, ART 2.0) | the look rules for the cap plate and the ragged piece, since ART 6.1 says only "a cap plate and nothing beyond it" |
| Seam weep, `SEAL_STRAIN` bowed rim and rim frost | FT-14 (rows 24, 26; `trail_hull_seamweep_*`, `trail_hull_rimbow_s`, `trail_cool_rimfrost_s`) | the requirement that the weep is visible at birth and that an as-welded seam with no gradient is quiet (MSA-23) |
| Prompt plate, linear LONG bar, needle-mark, work loop, tool sparks, ACK, abort and DONE frames | IA (V-1 to V-12) | the verb tokens and the three post-channel non-DONE cues (MSA-34, C-P10) |
| RAGGED breach: stubs, hiss, alarm sweep, venting trails | Graph (LS-16), FT, IA stub sprite | nothing drawn by Assembly; the ragged plate is Layout's |
| Camera cut on a body transfer; detach translation without rotation | PIPE C2, C5 | the requirement that nothing visible jumps on a docked weld |
| Lane pipes, valve wheel, breaker lever, junction boxes | shared pipe set (ART 3.3, 6.3), IA V3, V4, V5 | the dead-until-connected state of the lanes at birth |
| Carried module sprite, drop prompt | Suit, IA V-13 | the install prompt and the bump and seat frames |

Assembly adds **no** new hue, no new HUD element class, no meter, no icon layer and no new tell vocabulary. If a row below seems to need one, the row is wrong.

### Events: visual and audio feedback

Class is TRUTH or CORRUPTIBLE per the classification table below. Audio rows name the cue's existence and its sync point only.

**Offers and cursor (before any cost is paid).**

| Id | Event or state | Visual feedback | Audio feedback | Class | Pri |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| VA-1 | `weld_offer` true (welder equipped, lit, in reach, socket `FREE`, mate passes `can_attach`, `docked` holds) | IA V-1 plate with keycap `E` and `verb_weld` (state-neutral), anchored per IA, plus the in-world socket cursor (VA-2). Cut in; no fade, slide, bounce or scale; no outline or glow on the hull | none (prompts are silent in IA) | TRUTH (meta register) | P |

### Animation and motion constraints

1. **World changes are cuts.** Bead, cap plate, ragged piece, jambs and leaf, scar and the fresh-to-old swap appear by a cut on a committed tick. No tween, scale, fade, dissolve or draw-on. The change lands on the P5 frame of the apply; catch-up ticks (`MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME` 4) coalesce to the latest state and are never replayed per tick (MSA-39).
2. **Work loop**: six frames, two-handed, tool forward, stiff-weighted (ART 5.2); the same loop, speed and spark cadence for every target and every outcome (duration and cadence depend on the channel, never the target, IA-11c). No squash, smear, overshoot or recoil on abort, completion or any outcome cue.
3. **Facing lock** during a channel; five drawn facings, three mirrored (the tool hand swaps, ART 5 consistency note 8).
4. **Chest-point lint** (ART 5.1, IA art lint): in work, carry and install frames no tool, limb, module or effect covers `anchor_chest_<facing>` or the beam's forward arc. Sparks anchor at `anchor_hand_<facing>`.
5. **Luminance**: nothing on the HUD above 3 Hz (HR-13). World sparks and outcome transients: at most 3 luminance transitions per second. No full-frame flash, ever. Positional stepping is exempt (accessibility-specialist to confirm, as IA already asks).
6. **Camera**: a weld or cut that changes the avatar's body is PIPE's `BODY_TRANSFER` hard cut, never a pan. A docked weld shows no visible `delta_abs`. The detached piece translates without rotation. Assembly adds no shake, impulse or screen effect (BDY-28, `JOLT_PX` 0).
7. **Outcome transients are one-shots**: the weld sputter at most 24 ticks, the cut gutter at most 18 ticks, the install bump 12 ticks. They end by a cut and carry no persistence.
8. **Nothing decorative moves** at the seam (ART 6.4). The bead, cap plate and ragged piece are static. The only world motion Assembly causes is the weep (FT) and whatever the player connects afterwards.
9. **Reduced motion (HR-8)**: the bar fill persists (it is information), the drain and abort snap, the notch hold is kept; spark density may drop but sparks stay; **the weep and trail propagation are never suppressed**; the outcome transients may shorten but the three family cues stay distinguishable by *shape*, not by motion alone (sputter is broken fragments, gutter is a thinning stream, bump is a displaced prop).
10. **Zoom**: the cursor and all world pieces scale with the world viewport on the integer ladder `k` in `[1, k_auto + 2]`; the plate and bar keep HUD pixel size (IA F7 guard). No piece falls below the ART 5.6 minimum feature at the widest-field step: the 4-px bead and the 2-px cursor stroke are the floor.
11. **MIRROR and restore**: a presenter plays an outcome cue only if `last_result.T` is new and inside the window; `last_result` is runtime-only and so is empty after a restore, so a restore never plays a cue, and `last_seen` is initialised on join so a joiner never sees a stale one *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (MSA-34).

### Art bible principles and sections that bind Assembly

| ART reference | How it binds Assembly |
|---|---|
| 1, Principle 1 (darkness is honest; light is a decision) | Cursor, prompt and bead are readable only inside a light. The bead is never revealed by a mask; unlit it is dark and real. The welder channel needs a lit target |
| 1, Principle 2 (state lives in the sprite, not the icon) | A welded seam is a bead, jambs and a leaf; a cut seam is a cap plate or ragged piece. No "welded" badge, no weld-quality indicator, no connection panel, no seam health bar. The prompt shows a keybind and a verb, never a condition (7.0) |
| 1, Principle 3 (emission is exposure) | Sparks are non-emissive at MVP, so the weld's Pillar 3 price is the required light plus the TOOL noise, not the sparks. The bead emits nothing |
| 1, Corollary (the lie leaks) and checklist | See the truth-channel table: a phantom seam may exist only as Filter presentation, leaks violet at an edge and never carries the raised bead. Checklist items 1 to 5 run on every asset below |
| 2.3, 2.4 | The home-ship is "foreign dialects under one warm light, seams at every join"; ART 2.4's carried element for the lost state, "a failure trail spreading along a weld seam while you watch", is VA-15 literally |
| 2.6, 2.8 | Boarding rhythm: the weld is a deliberate, low-energy act under a held breath. Breach rhythm: only a RAGGED cut enters it, with the Graph's and FT's sweep and trails; Assembly adds no red |
| 3.3 (the seam is a raised Hull Grey bead, never hidden), 3.5 (hero #3) | The bead is hero shape #3 and "is never hidden". The only change that may draw the eye at DONE is the fresh bead appearing against grey; bar, plate and cursor are supporting. No decorative circle or moving element near the seam |
| 3.4, 7.2 (UI shape grammar, focus is a shape) | The cursor is a bracket with chamfer corners, not a ring or reticle |
| 4.1, 4.2, 4.4, 4.5, 4.7 | Palette and tests below |
| 5.1, 5.2, 5.6 | Chest-point lint, stiff work loop, minimum feature size |
| 6.1 (grafts and the crew's hand) | "A graft keeps its donor dialect in full; the seam is the only thing that is the crew's." "A section the crew cut away leaves a seam with a cap plate and nothing beyond it." Patch plates are Graphite bodies, on grid and off rhythm |
| 6.2 (unshaded albedo, normals) | Bead, cap plate and ragged piece are albedo plus normal plus specular; no baked light direction, no cast shadows, crevice darkening at most one value step. No rust, brass, copper or warm plastics (scorch is Void Steel, kerf is Graphite mid, matte and hue-less) |
| 6.3, 6.5 | Kit: seams straight, corner, T by fresh and old; door states closed and open. **Do not reuse 6.5's door-leaf states `cut` and `welded`** for these meanings (naming collision, Open Questions) |
| 7.0, 7.3, 7.7 (HR-1, HR-2, HR-4, HR-6, HR-8, HR-13) | Prompt and bar on opaque Ground plates; no Alarm Red on any Assembly element; no HUD shake; reduced motion; nothing above 3 Hz |
| 8.2, 8.5, 8.9 | Sizes (4-px bead, 96 x 96 door cell, 96 x 24 hatch footprint, 48 x 48 / 96 x 96 / 144 x 144 trails), naming grammar, acceptance checklist |

### Palette rules

**Sanity palette (ART 4.4, Corollary).** No violet (`#8A3FD1`, `#B07BE6`) and no flesh (`#D9A58C`, `#B5705C`) anywhere in Assembly's presentation, with no exception: the violet gleam and fringe are the Perception Filter's, outside the sprite, on corruptible readings only. The cursor, plate, bar, bead, cap plate, ragged piece, sparks, outcome transients and seat frames are all in the sane palette.

| Element | Colour | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Bead (fresh and old); bolt rhythm and edge bead of the cap plate | Hull Grey `#8C949C` | thin element only, never a plane (4.1). Fresh is brighter and cleaner, old is dulled and scuffed; at least one value step between them |
| Cap plate body, jambs, ragged-piece body | Graphite lit `#5C6670` / Graphite mid `#2A303A` | Hull Grey is never a plane. Kerf is Graphite mid; scorch is Void Steel `#0B1018`; all matte, hue-less |
| Weep | O2 Cyan `#3FD6E8` (mist) | FT's, soft drifting. With Power Amber, the only lane hue at the MVP seam |
| Rim frost | Coolant Ice `#C8E6F5` | FT row 26 under a thermal gradient |
| Lane pipes at birth | Graphite lit, no emission | ART 4.2 "dead loop"; they take their hue only when carrying (POWER lane Power Amber `#F2C230`, O2 lane O2 Cyan) |
| Sparks | Tungsten family: core `#FFE2A8`, body `#FFCC66`, hue at least 38 degrees | never Fuel Ember, Power Amber or Alarm Red; non-emissive |
| Socket cursor | Tungsten fixture `#FFCC66` (UI accent), flat, unlit, non-emissive | not a light, so no exposure and no beam shape; geometry is the primary channel (HR-11) |
| Plate, bar, needle-mark, keycap | IA's palette unchanged: Ground `#1C2128`, border Graphite lit, Paper White `#DDE4EA`, fill Tungsten `#FFCC66` | no loop hue, no Alarm Red |
| Carried module; seat dust | the module's own palette; dust is Graphite mid | |

**Never on any Assembly element:** Alarm Red (a RAGGED breach's sweep belongs to the Graph, FT and the alarm owner); Chemlight `#86B896` (light colour only); the loop hues on the cursor, plate, bar or outcome cues (a hue is a sentence about a system); any new hue (so no new ART 4.7 entry is required).

**Required tests before approval (ART 8.9).**
1. **ART 4.4 sampling test on the fresh bead first.** A fresh bead under chest-lamp Tungsten is the documented near-case (Hull Grey under fixture Tungsten computes to about hue 43, S 56 %, V 55 %). Sample `seam_straight_fresh` and the cap plate's Hull Grey bolts under all four beam shapes; hue must stay at least 36 degrees. "Brighter and cleaner" means higher value, never higher saturation or an orange pull; if drift appears, pull toward yellow, never desaturate the light.
2. **Greyscale pass (4.5).** Bead Hull Grey (L* about 61) against Graphite lit (about 43) is an 18-point gap and must survive achromatopsia; the weep (about 79) against the bead must also separate by motion (drifting mist versus a static raised bead).
3. **25 % silhouette test (3.1)** on the seam in a lit frame: the bead reads as a line with a break at the hatch.
4. **Colourblind (4.7):** at the MVP seam there is no Coolant and no Fuel lane, so Hull Grey against Fuel Ember (L* 61 against 61) cannot occur until a Vertical Slice lane is added; the backup (raised seam versus glossy puddle) is already named. Bowed rim versus rim frost differ in shape; sputter, gutter and bump differ in shape.

### Truth channel versus corruptible

TRUTH means the consequence channel or an anchor, drawn identically on every client and never wrapped by the Perception Filter. CORRUPTIBLE means module-body presentation that a class (b) hallucination may draw wrongly, always with the palette leak.

| Visual | Class | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Seam bead (fresh and old), floor and wall pieces | **TRUTH** | Layout geometry derived from `welds[]` and `welded_at_T`; the age swap is a derived read (LY-33). The Filter never wraps it (MSA-35, LY-34) |
| Hatch plate, jambs and leaf, **leaf position** | **TRUTH** | Layout and Graph geometry and opening area; whether the seam is open is physical, consequence channel, falsified by walking or by the draught |
| Cap plate, ragged piece, scar, "nothing beyond" | **TRUTH** | Layout socket state `CAPPED` or `CUT_OPEN` |
| Socket cursor and bracket | **TRUTH (meta register)** | A function of the same static inputs as the prompt; never lie-capable; no gleam, glide or tremor at any sanity stage |
| Prompt plate, verb text, LONG bar, needle-mark, notch | **TRUTH (meta register)** | IA's classification; the bar is the actor's own elapsed time and deliberately not a measure of the job |
| Weep, `SEAL_STRAIN` bowed rim and rim frost, draught streaks, hiss | **TRUTH** | Consequence channel (FT rows 24, 26, FT-14); the strain sprite is separate from the door sprite, so the door can lie but the strain cannot |
| Sparks, work loop, a crewmate's chest beam and channel | **TRUTH (human channel)** | The actor's own animation and the crewmate's beam are anchors (ART 1 Corollary) |
| Outcome cues (sputter, gutter, bump), seat frames | **TRUTH** | Driven by `assembly.state.last_result`, a SIM block identical on all clients; carries no reason, so it is not an oracle |
| **Seam-lane valve and breaker sprites:** the wheel position of the seam's own PIPE and DUCT sub-edge valves and the lever position of the seam's CABLE breaker | **TRUTH (fallback, CD-GDD-ALIGN), until the zero-gradient witness is confirmed** | MSA-23: at zero gradient an open valve has no hiss, creak or frost and a closed breaker no flow, so the sprite is the only reachable witness and a class-(b) lie on it would make a RAGGED cut with no witness (fails P1, P2, P4). The Perception Filter may not wrap these sprites until the Graph and Failure & Trail confirm a consequence-channel lane witness that exists at zero gradient (ASK G8, ASK FT1). When both confirm in writing, this row reverts to CORRUPTIBLE and the next row's witness carries the load. AC-MSA-126 |
| Other lane node and module-body sprites at the seam: junction powered lamp, door-panel lamp, every valve, breaker and gauge that is not the seam's own sub-edge | **CORRUPTIBLE** | Module-body presentation (IA V3, V4, V5, AD-11); may lie under class (b) with the stage-1 violet gleam on the glass |
| Lane pipe emission, flow chevrons, dash-pulse | **TRUTH (immune, Perception Filter PF-5 row 17a, TRUTH_PX; registry `truth_channel_union`)** | MSA-23 needs the cut mode predictable from the world alone; the "seam breaker is closed" and "valve is open" witnesses must not rest only on a corruptible lever sprite. a state the pipe does not show (an open valve at zero gradient) is covered by the seam-lane valve and breaker fallback row above, not by this one |
| Installed module's medium and state sprites | **CORRUPTIBLE** (module body) | Module-body channel |
| Any phantom seam, false "welded" state or phantom bead (hallucination classes b and d, VS) | **CORRUPTIBLE, Filter layer only** | Must leak: a violet fringe at its edge, never the raised-bead geometry, no collision, no occlusion, no weep, no hatch subject. It never reaches `welds[]`, the consequence channel or the bead geometry (MSA-35). The check is cheap: walk into it |

Two hard lines. (1) No Assembly cue, cursor or plate is ever recoloured, shaken or glided under any sanity stage (HR-6, HR-13). (2) `CUT_BLOCKED` stays a kept truth oracle (a real person is in the hatch, a phantom is not an actor); the Filter must never draw a crewmate-as-horror out of the hatch rectangle or hide a real one in it.

### Asset types implied

Names follow ART 8.5 (`<cat>[_<dialect>]_<subject>[_<variant tokens>]_<map>.png`, variant order loop, piece, state, facing, size, index). Every normal-mapped piece ships `_alb`, `_nrm`, `_spc`, `_msk`. New stems are proposals for the ART 8.13 maintenance pass; the `state` token list gains `capped` and `ragged`.

**Cited, not authored by Assembly:** `seam_straight_fresh_*`, `seam_straight_old_*`, `seam_corner_fresh_*`, `seam_corner_old_*` (T pieces are VS), `env_foundry_door_round_*` (frame and leaf, 96 x 96 cell, states `closed`, `open`), `trail_hull_seamweep_<s|m|l>_*`, `trail_hull_rimbow_s_*`, `trail_cool_rimfrost_s_*`, `pipe_shared_*` (lane pieces and terminal fittings), `tool_welder_*` and `tool_cutter_*` (`held_<facing>`, `use_<facing>`), `char_crew_body_*` tags `work_<facing>` and `carry_<facing>`, the IA spark and stub assets, the IA prompt-plate and LONG-bar components, and the `anchor_hand_<facing>` and `anchor_chest_<facing>` slices.

**New or specified here:**

| Stem (proposal) | Cell | Maps | What it is | Pri |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `env_foundry_hatch_plate_*` | 96 x 24 hatch footprint, one piece | alb nrm spc msk | The `FREE` socket plate, HULL (Layout owns the state; listed because the cap plate must be a deliberate sibling, not a recolour) | P |
| `env_foundry_hatch_cap_*` (state `capped`) | 96 x 24 | alb nrm spc msk | **Cap plate.** Graphite body; Hull Grey only on the bolt rhythm and an edge bead; on-grid but straddling the wall panel boundary and off the dialect's fastener rhythm (the patch-plate rule of ART 6.1: "we did this in a hurry"); rectangular, chamfered, not round. Plain and quiet: a closed door that is never going to open. Must read at 25 % as a plate where a port was | P |
| `env_foundry_hatch_ragged_*` (state `ragged`) | 96 x 24 | alb nrm spc msk | **Ragged piece.** Torn plate edges built from short straight segments at two or more angles, no forks, no taper-to-point filaments, no iridescence (the web's grammar is branching and non-repeating; this must read as human damage, ART 3.3); matte Void Steel scorch, Graphite mid kerf, hue-less. Solid and light-blocking | P |
| `ui_cursor_socket_corner.png`, `ui_cursor_socket_edge.png` | 2 pieces, 9-slice to `width_tiles` x 48 by 24 | none (flat, unlit) | The VA-2 bracket; `ui_` carries no map suffix (ART 8.5). World-space placement, flat material, non-emissive | P |
| `vfx_weld_sputter_*` | 48 x 48, 6 to 8 frames | alb | VA-25 transient: broken bead fragments and irregular spark bursts, non-emissive, no residue | P |

| Verb tokens `verb_seam_cut`, `verb_install` | text tokens on IA plates | n/a | New tokens for the prompt grammar; IA's `verb_cut` is V8 module salvage and must not be reused (ASK to Interaction) | P |

| CASCADE tell kit | none new | n/a | Reuses hull-stress hairlines and the real-needle tremor (VA-24) | V |

MVP new-piece count for the ART 8.5, 8.6 budget: two hatch states (cap, ragged) plus the plate sibling, one 9-slice cursor (2 pieces) and 2 to 3 VFX strips. Everything else already sits in the Foundry MVP kit (about 50 dialect pieces and about 60 shared, ART 6.3).

**Audio existence list (sync points above).** Weld channel start, loop, cancel, interrupted, done (IA `ia_start`, `ia_loop`, `ia_cancel`, `ia_abort`, `ia_done`, shared with every channel); seam-cut channel (the same) plus a `LOUD` one-shot at DONE; bead settle at VA-14 (the single audible "it is done" for the weld, no sting); cap seat at VA-20; ragged tear at VA-21; install clunk and seat at VA-28; the three non-DONE cues, one per family, dull and neutral (VA-25 to VA-27). The mood the visuals set is stiff, sealed, patient: a hull that creaks before it fails. Each outcome cue is indistinguishable across reasons (P4).

### Evidence list (retained screenshots in `production/qa/evidence/`, naming `msa-<frame>-k<level>.png`)

1. `FREE` socket, docked, welder equipped, lit: prompt plus VA-2 bracket, at the lowest ladder step and at `k_auto + 2`.
2. The same socket not docked, or unlit: no prompt, no bracket.
3. Mid-channel weld: LONG bar part-filled, sparks at the hand anchor, work loop, **no bead**, chest beam on the socket.
4. One tick after the weld: fresh bead, jambs and closed leaf, lanes Graphite and static, weep s-trace beginning; with and without a pressure gradient (rim bow or frost versus quiet).
5. The ART 4.4 sampling sheet: fresh and old bead and cap-plate bolts under all four beam shapes, hue readout at least 36 degrees.
6. The same seam after `SEAM_OLD_AFTER_TICKS`.
7. SEALED cut result: cap plate with the bead beside the scar, nothing beyond.
8. RAGGED cut result: ragged piece, stubs, hiss and the Graph's sweep in frame.
9. Outcome strips: sputter, gutter, bump (frame sequences), proving residue-free and reason-independent.
10. Carry plus install prompt at an empty bay; the seated module afterwards.
11. A crewmate mid-weld seen from a second seat (human channel).

13. Reduced-motion variants of items 3 and 9.
