## Game Feel

*Units: 1 tick = 16.7 ms (`TICK_RATE` 60); a render frame at 60 Hz is one tick, at 30 Hz two. Every number is a design target for the presenters, the HUD and the animation budget; the simulation values are the Formulas section's (D.7 for timing, D.9 for the shape-change time, D.17 for the cue gate). No animation frame rate is invented here: ART 5.2 owns the work loop's six frames and Interaction owns the bar's cadence. Assembly's feel is mostly inherited (Interaction's channel feel, the Pipeline's camera cut, Failure and Trail's weep); this section fixes the numbers Assembly adds: two durations, a delay of one tick, three one-shot cues and the discipline of what is not drawn. The Visual/Audio section owns what is drawn; this section owns how long it takes and how it feels to operate.*

### Feel Reference

**Borrow:** Project Zomboid's timed contextual actions (a linear bar, cancelled by moving, no skill-check minigame, no hit-stop) for the *channel*, and the hands-free chest lamp of *Pandorum* (the beam stays on the job while both hands work) for the *exposure*. The quality borrowed is deliberate, stationary, stiff competence under a held breath: pressing is instant, the work takes visible time, leaving is always free, and the finished thing is a fact in the room, not a fanfare. For the cut, the borrowed quality is the clean terrible certainty of *Aliens*-style bulkhead sealing: a loud burn-through and a plate where a door was.

**Anti-references:** (1) a Barotrauma-style repair minigame or any QTE (a UI surface that could leak module-body truth, and a rhythm demand the "I can leave now" test forbids); (2) Space Engineers or Starbase-style welding that **grows geometry with progress** (a drawn bead with no sim state behind it, VA-10, would be exactly that lie); (3) a building-game ghost-placement confirm (the anti-pillar: not a freeform builder); (4) any screen shake, hit-stop or camera punch on the weld or the cut (`JOLT_PX` 0, BDY-28).

### Input Responsiveness

| Action | Max input-to-response | Ticks | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Press `interact` on the weld prompt: ACK pre-play | the same render frame | 0 (at most 1 frame) | keycap drop, bar at fill 0, first work pose; the hand never waits for the sim (PCI) |
| `begin_weld` or `begin_seam_cut` applied, CONFIRMED | at most 1 tick after the press | 1 | `queued_tick` is the next tick; the bar then fills from the committed value; Assembly adds no latency of its own (the begin only opens the channel, MSA-3) |
| First TOOL noise push | the first accrued tick | 1 (`e` = 1) | the web may hear this from tick 1 (D.7); no visible change marks it (P3: shown in the world, never a meter) |
| Immediate reject at begin (`BUSY`, `NO_TOOL`, `OUT_OF_RANGE`, `NOT_LIT`, `NOT_ELIGIBLE`, `CUT_BLOCKED`, a structural Layout or Graph reason) | ack withdrawn within 1 render frame of the result event | 1 to 2 | the standard IA rejection: dull sound, no shake, flash or red; capped by IA's `PREPLAY_MAX_TICKS` 2 (proposed) |
| Let go (release) or `cancel_channel` | the channel ends in the same P2 | 1 | the bar drain is cosmetic and never blocks a new press |
| Move intent during a channel | abort before accrual on that tick | 1 | the body moves at once; the move always wins (PC-7). A cancel wastes at most `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` - 1 = 5 accrued ticks (D.7) |
| Hold `E` to completion, weld | 360 ticks to the DONE handoff (6.0 s); the world changes 1 tick later | `D` + 1 = 361 | `T_apply - Tb` = `D` + 1 for a fresh channel (D.7) |
| Hold `E` to completion, seam cut | 240 ticks to the DONE handoff (4.0 s); the world changes 1 tick later | `D` + 1 = 241 | deliberately faster than the weld (a prepared amputation, not an escape verb; revisit if players use it as a free panic button, which would show as RAGGED cuts under attack in the playtest log) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| DONE handoff to the visible world change | one tick, 16.7 ms | 1 | the completion is queued in P3 of `T_done` and applies in P2 of `T_apply`; the apply and the P5 batch land in the same render frame, inside the bar's 12-tick DONE hold |
| Weld applied to the first weep | at most one Graph block, 100 ms | at most 6 | the first Graph hook (`graph.propagate.power`, `.atmo` or `.failure`, whichever is due next, within 6 ticks) sees the new seam *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; FT draws the s-trace from that step |
| Failed-completion cue (`REJECTED` at the handoff) | the handoff tick's presenter frame | 0 to 1 | the handoff itself finds the reason, so there is nothing to wait for |
| Failed-completion cue (`NOT_TAKEN`, a dropped completion) | `PENDING_TTL_TICKS` + 1 = 13 ticks after the submission (sim), plus at most `MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME` 4 ticks to the first rendered frame (AC-MSA-115) | 13 (217 ms) sim, 17 (283 ms) end to end | the one slow cue; it is a defence path that the MVP queue depth never normally reaches (E-33) |
| Install press (TAP) | depress frame at the press; clunk at the Applied result | 1 | a sound cannot be withdrawn, so it waits for the result; an install is one tick (no channel) |
| `BLOCKED` install cue | the Applied result's frame | 1 | the bump is played from `last_result` (D.17), one frame after the result |

### Animation Feel Targets

| Animation or cue | Startup | Active | Recovery | Feel goal |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Weld work loop | first pose held from the press (ACK) until CONFIRMED (at most 1 tick) | six-frame loop for the whole accrued length (360 ticks), **not scrubbed by progress** | **0**: a cut to idle on every terminal state (no recoil, no overshoot) | stiff, committed, readable at 50 % size |
| Cut work loop | as above | six-frame loop for 240 ticks | 0 | the same loop, faster bar (6 versus 9 ticks per px) |
| Bar fill (weld) | appears at 0 on ACK | stepped, **9 ticks per art px** (150 ms) over 40 px | DONE holds 12 ticks then cuts | a clock, not a reading of the job |
| Bar fill (cut) | as above | stepped, **6 ticks per art px** (100 ms) over 40 px | DONE holds 12 ticks then cuts | a clock |
| Cancel drain | 0 | 2x fill speed (IA `CONFIRM_DRAIN_RATE`) | cut by the next ACK | "I let go" |
| Abort | 0 | snap to empty at once | break-notch held 12 ticks | "the world stopped me" |
| Weld result: bead and jambs appear | 0: the P5 frame of the apply | **a cut, no animation** (0 ticks) | the pieces persist | quiet finality: the room changed, nothing celebrated |
| Cut result: cap plate or ragged piece | 0: the P5 frame of the apply | a cut (0 ticks) | persists; the detached section translates without rotation | a clean, terrible certainty |
| Weld non-DONE cue (sputter) | 0 | at most **24 ticks** (400 ms): three irregular spark bursts | 0: ends by a cut, no residue | "the bead did not hold", dull and neutral |
| Cut non-DONE cue (gutter) | 0 | at most **18 ticks** (300 ms): the spark stream thins to nothing | 0 | "the seam held" |
| Install non-DONE cue (bump) | 0 | **6 ticks** the carried module sprite offsets 2 art px toward the bay, **6 ticks** it returns (12 ticks, 200 ms total) | 0 | "it did not seat", positional only, no squash |
| Install DONE (seat) | 0 | the module slides at most 4 art px into the mount over **4 frames**, then still | 0 | cause and rest; weight, then waiting for the rotor |
| Fresh-to-old bead swap | 0 | a cut at `SEAM_OLD_AFTER_TICKS` 36000 (10 min) | 0 | the ship ageing; never noticed in the moment |

### Impact Moments

| Impact type | Duration | Effect | Configurable |
|---|---|---|---|
| Hit-stop (freeze frames) | none | not used at all (BDY-28; the weight comes from sound and the 6 s stillness) | no |
| Screen shake | none | not used; `JOLT_PX` 0 and `BODY_DOCK_CONTACT` moves nothing on screen | no (a Pipeline knob; Assembly adds none) |
| Camera move | 1 frame hard cut, only when the avatar's body changes | PIPE's `BODY_TRANSFER` cut; on a docked weld `delta_abs` is zero so nothing visibly jumps; the detached piece translates without rotation | the cut is the Pipeline's |
| Controller rumble | none | not used for the channel; the Adapter owns rumble and gives Assembly no cue | n/a |
| Time-scale slowdown | none | not used | no |
| Weld DONE | 1 tick of world change inside a 12-tick bar hold | the bead settles; the single audible "it is done"; no sting, no flash | audio level only |
| Cut DONE | `LOUD` one-shot one tick before the plate; the plate on the P5 frame | a loud burn-through, then a cap plate or a ragged scar and the Graph's breach for RAGGED | audio mix only |
| First weld (hero beat) | the whole 6 s plus the 1 s after | the 1 s after is quiet but for the bead settling; the first weep hiss and any creak ramp in at its end (ASK FT3) and the bar disappears at the cut *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; a **mixing preference** routed to Adaptive Audio, not a sim rule: no escalation window and no invulnerability is added (P2 already requires its own tell) | Adaptive Audio's |

### Weight and Responsiveness Profile

- **Weight**: heavy and deliberate. A weld is six seconds of standing still, lit, with both hands busy; it is the single heaviest verb the player performs at a socket, and the cut (four seconds) is lighter in time but heavier in consequence. The weight comes from stillness and exposure, never from effects.
- **Player control**: high at every moment *before* the DONE handoff (let go or step away at any tick; the cost is at most five wasted ticks and the channel's partial progress is held for `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` 600); **none** in the single tick between the handoff and the apply, by design (one tick, 16.7 ms, the price of an atomic apply).
- **Snap quality**: crisp and binary on the world side. The hatch plate becomes jambs and a bead in one frame; the door becomes a cap plate in one frame; there are no half-states and no transitions. The bar is stepped, never smooth.
- **Acceleration model**: none. The work loop starts at its first pose on the press and stops by a cut; the bar fills at a constant rate; nothing ramps, eases or overshoots.
- **Failure texture**: every failure is dull, short and neutral, and **identical across reasons** (P4): the weld sputters, the flame gutters, the module bumps. There is no red, no text, no shake and no punishment animation. The player learns *why* from the world (the lane node that was salvaged, the module already in the bay, the crewmate in the hatch), not from the cue. A failure after cost (six seconds spent) is the worst case, and it is rare by construction (`PORT_ABSENT`, `SLOT_OCCUPIED`, `NOT_TAKEN`, a hatch entered late); the cost is the time and the exposure, which are the Pillar 3 price, and nothing is lost that cannot be redone.
- **The inert-weld risk (named)**: for six seconds the world does not change (no progressive bead, VA-10), and after the bead appears a weld moves nothing (LS-15). Both are intended (beat 1 then beat 2 of the Player Fantasy) and both are the loudest feel risk in the system. The only legitimate remedies are sim-backed (the leaf and jambs make the hatch look *newly operable*; the creak or frost when a gradient exists; the weep), never a drawn progressive bead, a flourish or an auto-open. Playtested by the targets below; an open question for the playtest gate. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the remedies are now owned, not hoped for: the Beat 1 release (the tool loop ends and the room goes quiet), the starting gradient as a Derelict Authoring requirement with a fixture check (ASK D3, AC-MSA-140), preloaded and warmed-up assets, and a cold-cache, export-build, whole-frame measurement in S3 (MSA-39).)*

### Feel Acceptance Criteria

Checked by playtest unless an AC id is given (the measurable ones are in the Acceptance Criteria section). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the playtest items below are scored by the pooled rule of Validation targets, not by "no playtester ever says X": an item passes if at least 17 of 20 pooled judgements (5 players x 4 prompts) go the intended way and the two raters agree on at least 80 % of coded sentences; the word lists are the Validation targets' lists; "floaty", "laggy" and "unresponsive" are counted as coded sentences, not as an absolute ban. These items are tuning inputs, not build gates.)*

- [ ] The weld reads as heavy and exposed: playtesters volunteer that they felt watched, standing still, and none describes it as "building" (they say "our ship", "I welded that").
- [ ] No playtester asks "did the weld work?" after the bead appears. If they ask during the six seconds, that is acceptable and intended.
- [ ] The bar's fill is within 1 px of the same fraction of the bar's length at 25, 50, 75 and 100 % of `D` (AC-MSA-113).
- [ ] The fresh bead is visible on the first rendered frame after the apply tick, with zero frames of delay (AC-MSA-114).
- [ ] Every non-DONE result is written within 13 ticks of the failed submission and its cue starts on the first rendered frame at or after it, at most `MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME` 4 ticks later, and ends within its stated one-shot length (AC-MSA-115) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*
- [ ] No hit-stop, shake, rumble or time-scale effect exists on any Assembly path (AC-MSA-119).
- [ ] No weld frame exceeds `WELD_FRAME_HITCH_MAX_MS` 33 ms and the steady-state frame time after the weld is within 5 % of before (AC-MSA-63).
- [ ] No playtester describes any non-DONE cue as "it worked", and none can tell two reasons apart by the cue (AC-MSA-55).
- [ ] No reviewer uses the words "floaty", "laggy" or "unresponsive" for the press, the bar or the cancel.
- [ ] A RAGGED cut is reported as "my fault, I should have closed the valve", not "the game killed me" (Player Fantasy validation target).

## UI Requirements

**Assembly draws no pixel of its own and adds no screen, menu, meter, panel, tooltip or reason text; but it does have real UI *content*, so this section is the contract on what Assembly feeds the surfaces that others draw.** The prompt plate, the linear bar and the carry-drop prompt are Interaction's and the HUD's (HUD & Suit Layer, Designed; *Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: no longer provisional*); the in-world socket cursor is Layout's socket highlight (Layout presentation data); the outcome cues are drawn by presenters (PRESENTATION subscribers) from one Assembly block. Pillar 1 forbids the screens a ship-building game would have (no blueprint view, no build mode, no placement ghost with rotation, no connection panel, no weld-quality or seam-health bar, no surge number, no "welded" badge, no reason text); the UI of this system is a keycap, a verb word, a bar and the world itself. This is not a "no UI" system: it has an information table, and the HUD GDD must meet it.

| Information | Display location | Update frequency | Condition |
|---|---|---|---|
| Weld prompt: keycap glyph for `interact` on the current device plus `tr(verb_weld)` | beside the focused `WELD_SOCKET` subject, at `world_to_hud(anchor_o)`, on the HUD layer outside the world SubViewport (Interaction's plate) | once per render frame from `focus_view` | `weld_offer` (D.2) true: welder equipped, lit, in reach, socket `FREE`, `can_attach` passes, `docked` holds. Hidden otherwise, with no "can't weld" state |
| Seam-cut prompt: keycap plus `tr(verb_seam_cut)` | as above, on the seam's hatch frame | as above | `seam_cut_offer` true: cutter equipped, lit, in reach, socket `WELDED` on an `ACTIVE` seam, seat on the keep-side component (MSA-18). Never shown on the derelict side |
| Install prompt: keycap plus `tr(verb_install)` | anchored to the hardpoint's mount; the carry-drop prompt `G . Drop` stays at the foot origin (AD-5 keep-outs) | as above | carrying a def in `compatible_defs(h)`, hardpoint subject focused, reach, lit, not staggered. An incompatible def: nothing |
| Channel bar (LONG class, 40 art px interior) with needle-mark | attached to the plate's long edge on the side facing away from the anchor (Interaction's component) | `fill_px` per committed tick, no interpolation: weld 9 ticks per px, cut 6 ticks per px | the local seat's `channel_view.state` is ACK, CONFIRMED or PROGRESS for an ASSEMBLY-owned channel; terminal frames (drain, snap and notch, DONE hold 12 ticks) are Interaction's |
| In-world socket cursor (four chamfer-corner brackets, Tungsten, flat, non-emissive) | on the focused socket's reference rectangle, in the avatar's body frame, world space | with the prompt, by a cut | exactly the prompt's condition, and only inside the lit area (the honest edge) |
| Outcome cue (sputter, gutter or bump) | at the socket or on the carried module, in the world; **no HUD element** | once per `last_result.T` change (D.17), inside `OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS` 60 | `last_result.outcome` is `REJECTED`, `BLOCKED` or `NOT_TAKEN` for the local seat; one cue per verb family, identical across reasons, never a string or a number |
| Everything else | nothing | never | `q`, `leak_bp`, `hp_seam`, `cap_seam`, `A_seam_m`, the `surge[]` magnitude, seam age, weld count, body count, socket state as text, the cut mode and the reason of any non-DONE outcome are **never displayed** (AC-MSA-19) |

**What the surfaces read (and nothing else).** The HUD reads `focus_view` and `channel_view` (Interaction's IA-41 views); the presenters read the local seat's `last_result` from `assembly.state` (a SIM block, replicated as `SHARED_SUMMARY`) and `welds[]`-derived geometry from Layout. Neither reads `pending`, any Graph node or edge, `forced_mode`, `served_pm` or any `PERCEPTION_OUT` block. Assembly publishes two pure predicates for the surfaces (`weld_offer` and `seam_cut_offer`, used as the `eligible` of the `begin_*` declaration rows, MSA-12) and the pure read `last_shape_change_T()` for Save/Load; it publishes no other read view *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: except `ragged_breach_open()`, the pure read Save/Load consults to withhold the `ASSEMBLY_CUT` event while the keep-side cut room is venting or a keep-side port is uncapped, MSA-33)*.

**Rules the HUD and Interaction owners must meet for Assembly's verbs.**

- *Labels are verbs, not states (IA-11).* `verb_weld`, `verb_seam_cut` and `verb_install` carry no condition word (no "Weld (docked)", "Cut (sealed)", "Install (compatible)"). `verb_seam_cut` must stay distinct from V8's `verb_cut` (module salvage): an ASK to Interaction to add the two tokens, not to reuse `verb_cut`. English proposals, owned by the HUD GDD: "Weld", "Cut away", "Install". Localisation through `tr()` with StringName keys; the plate grows to `PROMPT_PLATE_MAX_W_PX` then wraps, never truncating.
- *The prompt may be late, never wrong.* Hide is immediate (the same render frame the offer goes false: a prompt for a pair that is no longer docked, or a seat that stepped into the dark, is a lie by commission); show may be debounced by the HUD (IA D.UI-2), because a late prompt omits a hint and the verb works as soon as it is focused.
- *Bar semantics.* The bar is the actor's own elapsed time, `fill_px = floor(progress_pm x L / 1000)` with `L` 40 and `progress_pm` from ticks only (IA D.UI-1). It is **identical for SEALED and RAGGED**, for every `q` and for every module-body state (AC-MSA-88); the channel length is the declared `D` and never varies with the target. A resumed channel starts from its credited offset, not from zero (IA ledger).
- *No reason text, anywhere.* A rejected begin uses the standard IA rejection (ack withdrawn, dull sound); a post-channel non-DONE outcome uses the family cue of MSA-34; neither carries a word. Accessibility announcements, when AccessKit is in scope (Alpha), are fixed phrases keyed by outcome *state* (done, did not hold), never by cause.
- *Cursor and plate are meta-register truth.* They are functions of static inputs, never lie-capable, and never recoloured, shaken or glided at any sanity stage (HR-6, HR-13). The Perception Filter must not suppress or fake the weld, cut or install prompt (ASK F1).
- *Zoom and scale.* The cursor and all world pieces scale on the integer ladder `k` in `[1, k_auto + 2]`; the plate and bar keep HUD pixel size and are re-projected every render frame; a zoom or HUD-scale change mid-channel changes nothing in the simulation (MSA-40, AC-MSA-64).
- *Reduced motion and flashing (HR-8, HR-13).* The bar fill persists, drain and abort snap, the notch hold is kept; the outcome transients may shorten but stay distinguishable by shape (fragments, thinning stream, displaced prop); no element above 3 Hz.
- *Input devices.* The keycap glyph follows Interaction's device latch (classified by event type); both Assembly channels are hold-to-work verbs cancelled by movement or release on every device; a gamepad user holds the same `interact` action (no combo, no radial menu).
- *Accessibility: hold verbs and audio witnesses* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. (1) **Every audio-first witness has a TRUTH visual equivalent.** The weld and cut channels end on sound (the bead settle, the `LOUD` burn-through, the cut's TOOL loop heard through the hull), and the cut-mode witnesses (hiss, creak) are sound-first; each must have a visual equivalent that is also TRUTH and is not a new meter: the LONG bar and work loop already show the actor's own elapsed time, the draught streaks, rim bow and rim frost are visual, the open-valve hiss needs a visual of the same descriptor from Failure & Trail's existing grammar (the lane mist), and the `LOUD` of a cut has a visible equivalent on the crew side of the seam (the spark stream and its stop). Where no visual exists the row is listed as a gap for the accessibility owner, never filled with a number. (2) **The long holds honour a toggle-hold setting.** The 6 s weld, the 4 s cut and the `[VS-B]` veto's hatch hold are hold verbs; with the toggle-hold setting on, one press starts and a second press (or movement) ends the channel, with the same cancel-by-movement rule and the same bar, and nothing about duration, noise or abort changes (ASK AX1). `design/accessibility-requirements.md` does not exist yet; it must be created before the weld story, and these two rules are its first entries (AC-MSA-133, AC-MSA-134). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: Default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20): X10. The accessibility tier is standard; hold-to-confirm gets a two-tap alternative and an adjustable hold duration. For Assembly the toggle-hold above is the two-tap form of the 6 s weld and 4 s cut; their durations stay sim-fixed (`WELD_CHANNEL_TICKS`, `SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS`) and are not made adjustable, because a duration change would change noise per channel; the adjustable hold duration applies to confirm holds such as `CONFIRM_HOLD_S`, which are not Assembly's. `design/accessibility-requirements.md` is to be authored later with `/ux-design accessibility` (noted here, not created by this edit).)*
- *Co-op `[VS-B]`.* Each seat sees its own plate, bar and cursor; a crewmate's channel is visible only as the crewmate's own work loop, sparks and chest beam in the world (the human channel); a MIRROR client plays a cue only per D.17.
- *Engine facts (verified against `docs/engine-reference/godot/`; the reference has no scripting, core, 2D or scene-tree module).* 4.6 dual focus means the plate and bar nodes are non-focusable and mouse-ignoring; 4.7 `CanvasItem` lines carry no feather, so the bar is filled rectangles on the art-px grid (Interaction's ADR candidate); nothing about `Control` theming or world-to-screen projection is asserted here.

📌 **UX Flag — Modular Ship Assembly**: this system has UI content but no screen of its own. In Pre-Production, the specs that must cover it are `design/ux/hud.md` (the three verb plates `verb_weld`, `verb_seam_cut`, `verb_install` and the LONG bar at 9 and 6 ticks per px, via the Interaction UX pass) and the Layout socket-cursor entry in the in-world pattern library (the four-bracket cursor, flat and non-emissive). No standalone `/ux-design` pass is needed for Assembly; stories that touch UI cite those specs, not this GDD.

## Acceptance Criteria

*Ids AC-MSA-01 to AC-MSA-128 keep the pre-assigned meaning of the criterion index (T8) (AC-MSA-46 and AC-MSA-66 are absorbed into AC-MSA-01 and AC-MSA-58; split ids carry letters); the ids that the corrections and the Edge Cases added continue from AC-MSA-73 (AC-MSA-122 covers E-53; AC-MSA-123 to AC-MSA-128 were added by later revisions); ids 113 to 115 and 119, which the QA review had reserved, are reassigned to the Game Feel benchmarks. Every criterion carries a tag and a rule or edge-case reference. **Tags:** `(auto)` an automated test in `tests/unit/modular_ship_assembly/` or `tests/integration/modular_ship_assembly/`; `(lint)` a static scan or data lint; `(review)` a design or code review; `(manual)` a playtest or a retained screenshot in `production/qa/evidence/`; `(spike)` only the throwaway spike (see Spike Plan) can answer it. **Status markers:** `(depends: ASK Gn / CONFLICT Cn)` means the criterion rests on a provisional ask or an open conflict in the Dependencies tables (T14, T15); a failure there means "contract not yet agreed", not necessarily a defect. `[VS]` marks a Vertical Slice criterion whose rule is specified now. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **`[VS-B]` marks a co-op criterion (it needs a second seat, a MIRROR client or a crewmate) and is excluded from the MVP gate**; where a rule has a double-based form that is single-player (for example `CUT_BLOCKED` with a Body double, AC-MSA-36), that form gates the MVP and says so. Ids AC-MSA-129 to AC-MSA-148 are added by the first design review; AC-MSA-05 is split into 05a and 05b.)**

**Gate levels** (`.claude/docs/coding-standards.md`): Logic and Integration are BLOCKING; Visual/Feel and UI are BLOCKING and need a **retained** screenshot (a captured-and-discarded one is an assertion, not evidence); Config/Data is ADVISORY. Story typing: weld, cut, install and the verifier are Integration stories (several owners' mutators in one apply); D.1, D.6 to D.11, D.17 and D.18 are Logic; the knob and data lints are Config/Data; AC-MSA-68, 71, 113 and 114 are Visual/Feel plus UI.

**Test rules.** Deterministic (no wall clock, no unseeded RNG, a fixed `world_seed`); isolated; assertions on `abs(actor)` and section-local position, never raw `pos_curr`; fixtures from constant files or factory functions; the doubles are a SYSTEM-origin submitter (tests cannot reach `weld` or `seam_cut` any other way), an Interaction double (except where a criterion says it needs the real one), a Body double with `docked` controllable, a Perception stub, and a Graph double for capacity. **Performance criteria are gated on the named reference machines** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)*: RM-DESKTOP (minimum-spec desktop: Windows 10/11 64-bit, 6-core/6-thread CPU of the Intel Core i5-9400F / AMD Ryzen 5 2600 class, 8 GB RAM, GPU of the GTX 1050 Ti 4 GB / RX 570 4 GB class, SATA SSD, 1920x1080 at 60 Hz, P99 CPU frame-time ceiling 16.6 ms) and RM-DECK (Steam Deck LCD 2022, SteamOS stable, 1280x800 at 60 Hz panel, 30 fps target, P99 ceiling 33.3 ms), recorded in `project.yaml` `qa.min_spec`; the build type for every wall-clock figure is a release export (Godot 4.7.2 release export template, not the editor and not a debug template), renderer `forward_plus`, vsync on, a 120-frame warm-up, with the startup read-back (renderer, driver) recorded with each result; a pass needs P99 <= 90 % of the machine's ceiling; the developer's machine is not a reference machine and its figures are indicative only; the measurement protocol is that release export build, 300 warm-up ticks discarded for the apply budgets, then the stated sample; apply budgets with headless render off, frame-time budgets with render on. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **the performance criteria (AC-MSA-17b, 23, 62, 63, 108) stay gating**: the machines and build type are now named (R12, above), so a criterion is pending spike measurement on RM-DESKTOP / RM-DECK, not blocked, and the spike records the measured value as an input. A criterion marked `(spike)` produces a number for the spike report as a design input; the **story evidence** is the same assertion as an automated test under `tests/performance/modular_ship_assembly/` or `tests/unit/` run in the nightly job, never the throwaway spike report. The budgets themselves are re-derived from the S0 GDScript micro-benchmark before they are judged (D.15).)*

**Lint harness** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: no harness was named, so the `(lint)` criteria could not run).* One script, `tools/lint/msa_lint.gd`, run as `godot --headless -s tools/lint/msa_lint.gd -- --rules all` from CI before the gdUnit4 suite; it scans the Assembly source folder (the code root's `modular_ship_assembly/` directory) and the data files under `assets/data/`, prints one line per finding as `LINT-MSA-<n> <file>:<line> <message>`, and exits non-zero on any finding. Each `(lint)` criterion names its rule: LINT-MSA-03 (AC-MSA-03: the only block written is `assembly.state`), LINT-MSA-06 (AC-MSA-06: no packed-array field), LINT-MSA-09 (AC-MSA-09: completion payload fields, `max_age_ticks`, TTL range), LINT-MSA-10 (AC-MSA-10: no second definition of the predicate; the equivalence itself is a unit test), LINT-MSA-13 (AC-MSA-13: no full `can_join`, `check_install_module` or node-state accessor in a PLAYER-origin `validate`), LINT-MSA-17 (AC-MSA-17a: `weld_offer` source), LINT-MSA-20 (AC-MSA-20 and 58: forbidden identifiers), LINT-MSA-30 (AC-MSA-30: calls Assembly never makes), LINT-MSA-47 (AC-MSA-47: no `push_stimulus`), LINT-MSA-57 (AC-MSA-57: no `PERCEPTION_OUT` reference), LINT-MSA-119 (AC-MSA-119: no shake, hit-stop, rumble or time-scale). The harness is a token and call-graph scan, so it can prove an absence ("this file never names X") and cannot prove an equivalence: **AC-MSA-10 ("identical bodies across three sites") is therefore not a lint.** It is made structural: the three call sites call **one shared function `_evaluate(site)`** (`weld_ready` and `seam_cut_ready` each have one), whose only per-site differences are the skips of T2a passed as an argument; AC-MSA-10 is an `(auto)` unit test with a recording double that proves all three sites reach that one function and that the first failing reason is the same at each, and the lint only asserts that no second implementation of the predicate exists.

### Fixture and vocabulary block (referenced by the criteria as FB-n)

**FB-1 MVP fixture constants.** Pair `sock_airlock_e` (home-ship, facing E, `line_px` 720, `start_px` 672) and `sock_b_w` (derelict B, facing W, `line_px` 24, `start_px` 288), `width_tiles` 2, lanes POWER 0 and O2 0, no COOLANT lane, offset `(696, 384)` (DA-10), `q` 850, cable port caps 3000 W, `L_seam` 2. Sections: home 5 compartments, B 5, A 4. Module fixtures: `mod_heater`, vacant hardpoint `hp_cargo_heater`; lane nodes `j_main` and `j_b_main` (def-backed, authored non-salvageable in MVP content, ASK D2; a salvageable stand-in exists only as a test fixture), `dj_air` and `dj_b_dock` (bare, DA-19, not salvageable) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. `REACH_PX` 72 (boundary rule as Interaction publishes it, inclusive or exclusive: cite IA, do not restate), `FOOT_BOX_W` 16, `FOOT_BOX_H` 8. Coordinates marked `[UNV]` in a criterion are recomputed from Layout's published hatch rectangles before the story starts.

**FB-2 Command rows (T1).** `begin_weld`, `begin_seam_cut`, `install_module`: origin PLAYER, `OWNED_BY_ISSUER` (`actor_source body.state`, kernel CR-7) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, coalesce NONE, `max_age_ticks` 120, modes CHANNEL, CHANNEL, TAP. `weld`, `seam_cut`: origin SYSTEM only, explicit `actor` field, coalesce NONE, `max_age_ticks` `PENDING_TTL_TICKS` 12, payload `{actor, socket_a, socket_b, cause}` and `{actor, seam_id, cause}` with `cause` CHANNEL (CASCADE for `seam_cut`, VS). Absent by decision: `remove_module`, `begin_cut`, `abort_channel`, `set_socket_state`, `install_cap_plate`.

**FB-3 Reason order (first failure wins).** Weld: kernel authority; `NO_TOOL`; `OUT_OF_RANGE`; `NOT_LIT`; `BUSY` (site 1 only, including the `pending.kind != NONE` term); `INVALID_TARGET`; `NOT_ELIGIBLE`; Layout's `can_attach` eight (`SOCKET_UNKNOWN`, `SOCKET_NOT_FREE`, `SAME_BODY`, `FACING_MISMATCH`, `WIDTH_MISMATCH`, `CLASS_MISMATCH`, `LANE_MISMATCH`, `GEOM_OVERLAP`); `MSA.NOT_DOCKED` (sites 1 and 2 only); the Graph's nine structural reasons of `can_join_static` (`SECTION_UNKNOWN`, `SECTION_NOT_LOADED`, `SOCKET_OCCUPIED`, `PORT_INCOMPAT`, `SEAM_LIMIT`, `ALREADY_JOINED`, `CAPACITY`, `POWER_CYCLE`, `STABILITY`). Then, in the `apply` only, `PORT_ABSENT` (DEFERRED). Cut: kernel authority and the actor terms; `NOT_ELIGIBLE` (wrong component); Layout's `can_detach` (`SEAM_UNKNOWN`, `SEAM_NOT_ACTIVE`); Body's `can_rebase_detach` (`BDY_REBASE_UNKNOWN_BODY`, `BDY_REBASE_POSE_OUT_OF_RANGE`, `BDY_VEL_OUT_OF_RANGE`); `MSA.CUT_BLOCKED`; `MSA.CUT_STRANDS` `[VS-B]` (handoff and completion only, MSA-18) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. Install: the actor terms and `compatible_defs(h)` (IMMEDIATE); in the `apply` only `SLOT_OCCUPIED` (DEFERRED) and the defect-class backstop `PORT_INCOMPAT`. Layout reasons surface as `VALIDATION_FAILED(layout.<REASON>)`, owner reasons keep their owner's namespace, only `CUT_BLOCKED`, `NOT_DOCKED` and the install outcome `SLOT_OCCUPIED` are `MSA.<CODE>`.

**FB-4 Site skips (T2a).** Sites: 1 the `begin_*` `validate`, 2 the handoff pre-check in `assembly.step`, 3 the completion `validate` (authoritative). `BUSY` is evaluated at site 1 only; `NOT_DOCKED` is skipped at site 3 only; every other term is identical at all three sites; the full `can_join` and `check_install_module` run only in the completion `apply`.

**FB-5 Apply call orders, with the real identifiers.** Weld: `can_join` (A0) -> `weld_join` (Graph M2) -> `attach_section` (Layout LM2) -> `rebase_on_attach` (Body BM3) -> `sync_section` for each of the two sections -> the `assembly.state` write. Cut: derive the mode (D.6) -> `cut_detach` (Graph M3) -> `detach_section` (Layout LM3) -> `rebase_on_detach(..., frame_shift_px, rects[])` (Body BM4, fifth argument *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*) -> `sync_section` for each of the two sections -> the `assembly.state` write. Install: `check_install_module` -> `install_module` (Graph M5, `hp_start := condition_bp`, `hp_cap := cap_bp`, `patched_at_step` carried through, *Amended 2026-10-27 — design review*) -> `release_carried` (Suit) -> `clear_carry_ref` (Controller, published) -> `sync_section` -> the `assembly.state` write. **Calls Assembly never makes** (MSA-17): `instantiate_section_emitters`, `discard_section_emitters` (Emitter E1, E2), `set_opening_area` (M13), `apply_damage` (M18), `discard_section` (M4), `discard_section_geometry` (LM4), `place_actor`, `remove_actor`, `transfer_actor`, `set_body_velocity` (BM1, BM2, BM5, BM6), `push_stimulus`, and any Save/Load call.

**FB-6 `assembly.state` v1 fields (T6).** `schema_version` 1; `seats` map keyed by PlayerId (0 to 4 entries, never peer id); per seat `pending {kind NONE|WELD|SEAM_CUT, target_ref, submit_T}` (a live gate, **stored as NONE**, MSA-4 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*) and `last_result {verb_id WELD|SEAM_CUT|INSTALL, target_ref, outcome DONE|REJECTED|BLOCKED|NOT_TAKEN, reason 0-99, T}` (**runtime-only**, outside `snapshot()` and the hash *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*); `cascade_deferred` (at most one entry, `[VS]`, empty in MVP). Class SIM, `persist` true, `persist_scope` WORLD, `repl_hint` SHARED_SUMMARY, `restore_after [layout.state, graph.state, interaction.state, body.state]`. `T` values are int64 (a string or two 32-bit integers at the serialisation boundary).

**FB-7 Verifier invariants (T7).** INV-1 an `ACTIVE` Graph SeamRecord is exactly one `ACTIVE` `welds[]` row with both sockets `WELDED(seam_id)`. INV-2 a `CUT_SEALED` or `CUT_RAGGED` row is exactly a `CUT` SeamRecord of the same mode with sockets `CAPPED` or `CUT_OPEN`. INV-3 each body's section set is the connected component over `ACTIVE` welds and the relation is a forest (LY-14). INV-4 every live Layout body has exactly one Body pose row, a `DISSOLVED` body none. INV-5 every actor's `body_id` exists and its `pos_curr` lies in that body's floor area. INV-6 `body_count <= MAX_BODIES` (hard) and `body_count <= BODIES_LIVE_MAX` (warn-only, `MSA_BODIES_LIVE_WARN`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. INV-7 id monotonicity: no `seam_id` or `body_id` at or above the owner's `next_*`, none reused. INV-10 the `pending` target exists and is in a legal state of its verb (weld: `FREE` or `WELDED`; cut: `ACTIVE` or `CUT`) and `pending.submit_T` is not in the future (live state only; vacuous at restore); `last_result.outcome`, when present, is in range *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. Conservation and fresh-seam disconnection (the former INV-8, INV-9) are the Graph's own LS-24 assertions and are cross-checked by AC-MSA-25 and AC-MSA-67.

**FB-8 Knobs (T17) with defaults and ranges.** `WELD_CHANNEL_TICKS` 360 (240-480, multiple of 6); `SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS` 240 (240-480, multiple of 6); `WELD_Q_DEFAULT` 850 (range stated once, in D.1: `Q_MIN` = `max(400, ceil(LEAK_HP / 10))` to 990); `PENDING_TTL_TICKS` 12 (8-119) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; `OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS` 60 (12-180); `CASCADE_CUT_DEFER_MAX_TICKS` 600 `[VS]` (60-1800, multiple of 6); `SOCKET_STANCE_CLEARANCE_PX` 8 (8-24); budgets `MSA_WELD_APPLY_BUDGET_MS` 0.5, `MSA_CUT_APPLY_BUDGET_MS` 0.5, `MSA_INSTALL_APPLY_BUDGET_MS` 0.2, `MSA_STEP_IDLE_BUDGET_MS` 0.02, `MSA_WELD_FRAME_P5_BUDGET_MS` 8, `PROJECTOR_WELD_BUDGET_MS` 4 `[UNV]`, `WELD_FRAME_HITCH_MAX_MS` 33, `MSA_WELD_OFFER_BUDGET_US` (set in the spike, working figure 5 `[UNV]`). `CUT_WITNESS_LOOKBACK_S` 60 is a manual-AC parameter, not a sim knob.

### A. Shape, block and the two-phase protocol (MSA-1 to MSA-6; D.7, D.11)

- **AC-MSA-01 (auto)** GIVEN the registered command table, WHEN it is enumerated, THEN exactly `begin_weld`, `begin_seam_cut`, `weld`, `seam_cut` and `install_module` carry Assembly as owner; `remove_module`, `begin_cut` and `abort_channel` are absent; `weld` and `seam_cut` allow SYSTEM origin only. *(Absorbs the former AC-MSA-46.)* [MSA-1, MSA-28]
- **AC-MSA-02 (lint)** GIVEN every constant or formula name Assembly cites from another owner, WHEN compared with `design/registry/entities.yaml`, THEN each name resolves to a registry entry or, for a knob its owner keeps unregistered (for example `A_CUT`, `A_SEAM_MAX`, `SEAM_WEARIN_BP_PER_100`, `MAX_SEAMS_PER_SECTION`, `MAX_GRAPH_EDGES`, `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET`, `PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE`), to the owner GDD's Tuning Knobs row; the value or expression used in this GDD and in the data files equals that source; an unresolvable name is a failure, not a skip. [MSA-2]
- **AC-MSA-03 (lint)** GIVEN Assembly's source, WHEN scanned, THEN the only block it writes is `assembly.state`; every other state change is a call to a published mutator of FB-5. [MSA-2]
- **AC-MSA-04 (auto)** GIVEN a seat at the docked pair holding the welder, WHEN `begin_weld` applies in P2 of `Tb` = 437039 and the seat stays still and lit, THEN accrual ticks are 437040 to 437399, the DONE handoff is in P3 of 437399, `weld` is submitted then with `queued_tick` 437399, and it applies in P2 of 437400 at age 1 with `last_result = DONE`; the same script for the cut (`Tb` 439759, `D` 240) applies at 440000; exactly `D` TOOL pushes are recorded per full channel. [MSA-3, D.7]
- **AC-MSA-05a (auto)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* GIVEN `assembly.state` v1 per FB-6 on a fixture with two seats (one with `pending {WELD, ref, T}`, one with a runtime `last_result {INSTALL, DONE, 0, T}`), WHEN snapshot and restore run, THEN the snapshot contains no `last_result` key at all and every seat's `pending` is stored as NONE (kind NONE, empty target ref, `submit_T` 0) even though the live fixture held `{WELD, ref, T}`, the restored block equals the snapshot, `snapshot(restore(snapshot()))` is byte-identical to the snapshot (restore-idempotence), `last_result` is empty, and the canonical bytes of the block contain no `last_result` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. [MSA-4]
- **AC-MSA-05b (auto, two layers)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (a) **Pre-check layer (Save/Load SL-11, T-5):** GIVEN a payload whose `assembly.state` `schema_version` is 2 or 0 (outside `supported_versions`), WHEN the loader's pre-check runs, THEN the load is refused with the closed `SL_PRECHECK_*` reason (INCOMPATIBLE), the kernel control-plane spy records zero calls, the kernel never enters `FAULTED` and the pre-restore `state_hash` is unchanged. (b) **Block layer (CR-8, CR-18):** GIVEN a payload that passes the pre-check but whose `assembly.state` `restore()` returns a failure (a dictionary of the wrong shape), WHEN `request_restore` runs, THEN `restore()` reports the failure, the kernel goes `LOADING -> FAULTED` naming `assembly.state`, no tick runs, and **the pre-restore state is not claimed to be intact** (whole-block replace; kernel edge case on restore failure; Save/Load AC-SL-57a). The two layers are never mixed: the earlier criterion's "restore fails and keeps the hash" held only for (a). [MSA-4, SL-11]
- **AC-MSA-06 (lint + auto)** GIVEN the `assembly.state` schema, WHEN scanned, THEN no packed-array typed field exists; GIVEN `pending.submit_T`, `last_result.T` and `last_shape_change_T` set to 2^53 + 1 (9007199254740993), WHEN serialised and parsed, THEN the value is recovered exactly and the serialised form is a string or two 32-bit integers, never a JSON number. [MSA-4, SL-2]
- **AC-MSA-07a (auto)** GIVEN the same `assembly.state` and `channel_view`, WHEN `assembly.step` runs twice, THEN the submitted command lists are byte-identical. [MSA-5]
- **AC-MSA-07b (auto)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **MVP form:** GIVEN one seat reaching DONE on a tick, WHEN `assembly.step` runs, THEN exactly one submission is made for it and never two for one seat on one tick. **`[VS-B]` form:** GIVEN seats 3, 1 and 2 reaching DONE on one tick, THEN submissions are in ascending PlayerId order 1, 2, 3. - **AC-MSA-07c (auto)** GIVEN the hook registry, THEN `assembly.step` is `(owner Assembly, divisor 1, offset 0, after [interaction.step])`; registering `interaction.step` with `after [assembly.step]`, or leaving `interaction.step` unknown, fails boot; on a MIRROR client the hook does not run. ### C. Weld (MSA-11 to MSA-17; D.1 to D.5, D.12, D.14, D.18)

- **AC-MSA-15 (auto)** GIVEN the seat focuses `sock_b_w` from the derelict side, WHEN the pair is derived, THEN `socket_a` is `sock_airlock_e` (kind HOME first), the survivor is the home-ship body and the absorbed body is B's; the same pair results when `sock_airlock_e` is focused; the lane set (POWER 0, O2 0, no COOLANT) and offset `(696, 384)` are asserted against DA-10. [MSA-11, D.3]

[Game Feel, UI Requirements]
- **AC-MSA-114 (auto)** GIVEN a presenter spy on the weld apply, WHEN the weld applies at tick `T`, THEN the fresh bead, jambs and leaf are in the first rendered frame whose committed tick is at least `T`, with zero frames of delay, and no bead is drawn in any frame while `welds[]` has no row. [Game Feel, VA-10, VA-14]
- **AC-MSA-115 (auto)** GIVEN each non-DONE outcome, WHEN `last_result` is written, THEN, in two separate bounds *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the single 13-tick bound had zero slack for `NOT_TAKEN`, because a 30 Hz frame or a catch-up frame overshoots it by construction)*: **(i) sim latency** `last_result.T - submit_T` is exactly 13 for `NOT_TAKEN` (D.11) and the handoff `REJECTED` and `BLOCKED` results are written within 1 tick; **(ii) presenter latency** the cue starts on the first rendered frame at or after `last_result.T`, at most `MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME` 4 ticks later (so the end-to-end bound for `NOT_TAKEN` is 13 + 4 = 17 ticks); and the cue ends within its stated one-shot length (weld sputter at most 24 ticks, cut gutter at most 18, install bump 12) with no residue sprite left in the world. [Game Feel, VA-25 to VA-27]
- **AC-MSA-119 (lint)** GIVEN every Assembly path, WHEN scanned, THEN no hit-stop, screen shake, camera impulse, rumble call or time-scale change exists (`JOLT_PX` 0 is not read by Assembly). [Game Feel, VA-19]

(depends: ASK AX1) [UI Requirements, MSA-30]

### I. Lines of play and the playtest gate

- **AC-MSA-69 (manual, playtest target, not a pass/fail criterion; Validation targets)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* GIVEN five playtesters, THEN at least two take the weld line (a completed weld) and at least two take the strip-without-weld line (at least one completed V8 salvage on derelict B before any weld attempt); if either is below 1 of 5, re-tune the welded section's payoff or exposure. A tuning input, not a gate; AC-MSA-131 is the gating form of the strip line. [MSA-42]
- **AC-MSA-125 (Vertical Slice GATE, auto + manual)** GIVEN the Vertical Slice build and its content script, WHEN a scripted player runs weld, cut, weld, cut, weld (`VS_WELD_CYCLES_MIN` `[PLACEHOLDER]` 3 consecutive weld cycles, range 2-6), THEN every weld finds a `FREE` home-side socket through exactly one of Layout's three options (a re-cut verb that reopens a `CAPPED` socket keeping its history; more home sockets; derelicts that bring three or more sockets), `SOCKET_ECONOMY_LOW` does not fire, and the option chosen is recorded in Layout and this GDD. The criterion is not evaluated at MVP (the MVP playtest gate still must not require a second weld, MSA-25); **it blocks the Vertical Slice exit**: `/gate-check` for the Vertical Slice fails while it is unmet. [MSA-25, Open Question 7]
- **AC-MSA-127 (manual, playtest target, not a pass/fail criterion; Validation targets)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* GIVEN five playtesters shown, with no HUD and no explanation, four seams in randomised order (two fresh, welded under 60 s ago; two settled, past the end of the wear-in at period 100 (LSG D.2), about 16.7 minutes), WHEN each is asked "newer or older?" using the weep or the bead alone, THEN **at least 17 of the 20 pooled judgements are correct** and none cites a number or an indicator. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the bead and the weep are shown and scored **separately** (two blocks of four seams, bead-only and weep-only), because `SEAM_OLD_AFTER_TICKS` 36000 (10 min) and the 16.7-minute weep age on different clocks, VA-17.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the weep now tapers (LSG D.2), which is the settling gradient the earlier flat weep lacked; the criterion is unchanged.)* A failure is a tuning input to the Graph and Failure & Trail (Open Question 11), not a defect of Assembly. [Player Fantasy Beat 2, Open Question 11]
- **AC-MSA-128 (manual, co-op) `[VS-B]`, playtest target, not a pass/fail criterion** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* GIVEN two-seat co-op playtests of the first weld and the first cut, THEN the Fellowship targets of Validation targets (3 of 4 first welds with the second seat standing off the beam or on the far side without being told; at least half of the pairs use "we" language) are recorded, with no Assembly rule added for either. A miss is a playtest finding, not a mechanic request. [Player Fantasy, co-op beat]
- **AC-MSA-70 (manual, playtest target, not a pass/fail criterion; Validation targets)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* GIVEN five playtesters with instrumented telemetry events (prompt first appears, channel begins, bead appears, first seam-breaker close, each with a tick), THEN the median interval from the weld prompt first appearing to the channel beginning is at most 45 s, and the median interval from the bead to the first seam-breaker close is within 60 to 180 s inclusive. **An attempt** is one prompt-to-begin interval, and an attempt that aborts and resumes is one attempt: the interval ends at the first `begin_weld`, and a ledger resume (IA-16) neither starts nor ends one; a session in which the player never begins is excluded, not scored as a miss *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G2)*. Both are `[PLACEHOLDER]` playtest targets: a failure is a tuning input, not a defect. (depends: DA OQ-DA-31, which answered ASK D1; the DA-10 repeat remains) [MSA-42]

### Cross-system criteria recorded here (owners outside this GDD)

These belong in the owners' lists; Assembly records them so the tester knows they exist. None gates Assembly's story.

- **Lighting (ASK LT1):** five live bodies against `BODIES_LIVE_MAX` 4 degrade to `OTHER_BODY` stand-ins with the sim hash unchanged (AC-MSA-65 repeats it).
- **Interaction and Lighting (E-51):** GIVEN `lit` satisfied by a glowstick alone, THEN the weld proceeds with no Assembly special case.
- **Derelict Authoring (ASK D1, answered by OQ-DA-31):** GIVEN a new game, THEN V3 (`j_cryo`) and V4 are exercised on `home_ship` before any sortie, so no dock gate exists; `weld_offer` reads no player history (a content-script check on the fault order, not a runtime read).
- **Attention (ASK A1):** GIVEN a cut after the locus has moved, THEN the Attention level is not reset.
- **Per-ASK contract tests:** one criterion per row of T15 is added when the owner accepts the ask (ids are assigned then); until then the rows are the Gaps below.

1. **O2-lane `cap_seam` (`cap_duct` `[UNV]`, ASK G6(d)) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*:** only the O2-lane `cap_seam` of D.1 and D.4 cannot be asserted (the formula is tested with an injected value); `hp_seam`, `leak_bp` and `A_seam_m` of the O2 lane are cap-independent and are asserted (AC-MSA-18, AC-MSA-94); the POWER-lane half is fully tested. The gap was unowned (Item DB OQ 30, Graph row 26, OQ-DA-33) and now has an assigned owner, Derelict Authoring (re-review, D.4), and **gates the weld Integration story only**, not the kernel, the PROJECTOR (S3), the fault model (S6) or the power-lane weld.

3. **The cut witnesses (AC-MSA-93) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*:** the open-leaf draught, the seam-valve hiss under a gradient and the powered-junction state exist as consequence-channel descriptors; the zero-gradient open seam valve and closed seam breaker have none (Failure & Trail concurs, FT row 21), so the seam's own valve and breaker sprites are permanently TRUTH (Perception Filter row 12, AC-MSA-126 gating) and MSA-23b "feel the seam" (ASK IW1) is the diegetic witness. AC-MSA-39 and 93 are no longer BLOCKING; the Graph's answer to G8 is still owed.

## Spike Plan

*This system is the riskiest MVP build item (index risk PR-SCOPE) and the kernel's stress test (kernel Interactions row 5), so it is the first spike. The spike is **throwaway** (concept: 4 to 8 weeks) and answers questions the design cannot: cost on a real tree, determinism under a moving body, and whether the atomic apply holds. It runs **headless first** (kernel H10: `Kernel.step()` with no scene tree), then once in a scene for the frame-time items. The spike produces numbers and a go or no-go; it does not produce shippable code, and its measured values are written back into T9, T17 and the Acceptance Criteria by the author who reads the report. Performance numbers are recorded on RM-DESKTOP and RM-DECK in the release export build (R12) and are spike deliverables until measured.*

**Engine coverage statement.** `docs/engine-reference/godot/` contains `animation`, `audio`, `input`, `navigation`, `networking`, `physics`, `rendering` and `ui` modules and **no scripting, core, 2D, tilemap or scene-tree module**. Every engine behaviour below that concerns `Node2D` re-parenting, Y-sort tie rules, `TileMapLayer` batching after a merge, `Dictionary` iteration order or integer precision in a build is therefore `[UNV]` until the spike measures it. The only engine facts verified from the reference are that `TileMapLayer` replaces `TileMap` (breaking-changes), that 4.6 added scene-tile rotation, that setting one element of a packed array no longer calls the property setter in 4.7 (the reason `assembly.state` holds no packed array and every mutator marks its dirty keys explicitly), and that an override of a method with a typed return inherits that type and needs an explicit `return` (the reason command handlers are plain-data records, not an inheritance hierarchy).

**PROJECTOR shape options to evaluate in S3 (behaviour only, no recipe; the decision is ADR-LAY-03).** (a) a per-body scene root, with absorbed sections re-parented under the surviving body's root; (b) per-section roots stay in place and only the body-frame offset changes (no re-parenting); (c) per-section roots stay and the PROJECTOR recomputes section positions in world space each frame. Test (b) first: it has the fewest scene mutations on the weld frame. Which is cheapest is `[UNV]` until measured.

**Order and gates.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: run S0 first, because it supplies the budgets S1 is judged against; the MVP scope is decided (Open Question 1, resolved by user ruling 2026-10-27 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*) so the spike may start, and it needs RM-DESKTOP / RM-DECK (Open Question 6, closed by R12) for the numbers, but not `cap_duct`, which gates only the weld Integration story, not S0 to S3, S6 or S7.)* Run S1, S3 and S6 first: they are the three that can sink the design (determinism of the atomic apply, the render cost of the merge, and the failure model). If S1 (an `abs` drift, a FAULTED, or the whole weld tick over `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4, not merely the 0.5 ms allocation) or S6 fails, the atomic one-tick command is not safe as designed and the fallback is to split the apply across ticks behind a Graph-held "welding" state, which re-opens MSA-14, the Graph's LS-24 and the kernel's CR-9 and is escalated to the technical-director. If S3 fails for every option, the fallback is a one-frame fade-free hide behind the existing camera cut (S5) with the PROJECTOR rebuild spread over the hold of the DONE bar, and the budget is renegotiated. S2, S7 and S8 then run continuously as regression tests (AC-MSA-67 and 72 are tagged nightly), and S4 to S5 run once per scene change. S1 to S3 and S6 are the exit gate for starting the MVP build of this system.

**What the spike does not answer.** It does not answer the feel of the six stationary seconds, the readability of the cut witnesses or the first-connect tension (those are playtest items, Open Questions), and it does not validate the Graph's, Layout's or Body's own spikes, which it only consumes.

**ADR candidates fed by this plan** (none exists yet): the atomic weld, cut and install apply order and failure model, including the verifier's placement (CR-9); channel hosting (Interaction's record with `owner_system`, `open_channel`, the DONE handoff); the PROJECTOR shape after a weld (ADR-LAY-03, spike-gated); read-view injection for `eligible` predicates (Interaction to Assembly); block data as a Resource versus a RefCounted for `assembly.state`; the test-only fixture-template policy for the 1,000-sequence property test.
